# Parity matrix

What the professional 2D+ tools can do, chapter by chapter, and where Doodleberry stands (WP-41). Each capability
is described in our own words from the tool's manual and release notes; the data is in `parity/<tool>.json`, and
`python -m dbe parity.check` validates it and measures coverage. Generated by `python -m dbe parity.matrix`; re-run
at the end of each parity phase.

Match: **command** (an existing command does it), **data** (a field of the film or puppet data), **metric** (a QA
measure), **partial** (partly there; the WP that finishes it), **gap** (missing; the WP that closes it), **n/a**
(not relevant to an agent-run studio).

Overall: 1396 capabilities; covered 37%, covered or partial 69% (of those that apply).

| Tool | Chapters | Capabilities | Command | Data | Metric | Partial | Gap | n/a | Covered | Covered or partial |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Adobe After Effects | 19 | 161 | 30 | 33 | 1 | 41 | 43 | 13 | 43% | 71% |
| Blender Grease Pencil | 26 | 150 | 9 | 23 | 1 | 52 | 56 | 9 | 23% | 60% |
| Toon Boom Harmony | 25 | 182 | 25 | 47 | 0 | 52 | 50 | 8 | 41% | 71% |
| Moho Pro | 34 | 186 | 24 | 42 | 1 | 63 | 38 | 18 | 40% | 77% |
| Foundry Nuke | 32 | 189 | 28 | 25 | 2 | 39 | 55 | 40 | 37% | 63% |
| OpenToonz | 26 | 191 | 33 | 33 | 1 | 49 | 65 | 10 | 37% | 64% |
| Synfig Studio | 26 | 169 | 34 | 38 | 2 | 42 | 40 | 13 | 47% | 74% |
| TVPaint Animation | 26 | 168 | 30 | 13 | 1 | 68 | 48 | 8 | 28% | 70% |

Gaps and partial matches by the work package that closes them:

WP-36 161, WP-35 106, WP-18 96, WP-42 66, WP-19 61, WP-15 60, WP-37 36, WP-23 23, WP-16 23, WP-20 20, WP-34 19, WP-43 18, WP-40 17, WP-38 13, WP-11 12, WP-39 11, WP-14 11, WP-22 10, WP-12 9, WP-21 8, WP-13 8, WP-30 7, WP-29 4, WP-17 3, WP-24 3, WP-33 2, WP-26 1, WP-27 1, WP-25 1, WP-28 1

## Adobe After Effects (After Effects 2025 (25.x line: 25.0 Oct 2024 to 25.6 late 2025); a few 26.x additions noted where marked)

Sources: [Adobe After Effects User Guide (official, helpx.adobe.com)](https://helpx.adobe.com/after-effects/user-guide.html); [What's new in After Effects (official)](https://helpx.adobe.com/after-effects/desktop/what-s-new/whats-new.html); [After Effects feature summary, April 2025 release (25.2)](https://helpx.adobe.com/after-effects/desktop/using/whats-new/2025-2.html); [CG Channel: Adobe releases After Effects 25.2](https://www.cgchannel.com/2025/04/adobe-releases-after-effects-25-2/); [CG Channel: Adobe releases After Effects 25.3](https://www.cgchannel.com/2025/06/adobe-releases-after-effects-25-3/); [Digital Production: After Effects 25.4](https://digitalproduction.com/2025/08/15/after-effects-25-4-lands-in-august-hdr-metadata-text-format-paste-and-layer-staggering/); [Newsshooter: What's new in After Effects 26.0](https://www.newsshooter.com/2026/01/22/whats-new-in-adobe-after-effects-26-0/); [Keyframe interpolation in After Effects](https://helpx.adobe.com/after-effects/desktop/animate-in-after-effects/animation-keyframes/keyframe-interpolation.html)

### Getting started: setup, formats and help

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Published system, GPU and hardware requirements plus a list of every import and export format and codec the app reads or writes. | Studios check a workstation or render node against the list before a job, and confirm a client's codec is readable before footage arrives. | **command**: `engine.doctor`, `engine.info` (engine.doctor checks the machine (core, audio, tools) and prints the exact fix for each gap; engine.info reports the renderers and tool versions found. Doodleberry has no general codec matrix because it reads few footage formats.) |
| Preferences for application-wide defaults: auto-save, default interpolation, still-sequence frame rate, import interpretation rules, cache locations. | A facility sets house defaults once so every artist's comps start the same way. | **data**: `dbe/profiles.json`, `shots.json defaults (size, fps)` (House defaults live in versioned data: dbe/profiles.json (encoding profiles, per-style QA thresholds) and schema defaults. There is no per-user preferences store; it is not needed for agents.) |
| Customisable keyboard shortcuts with a visual keyboard map. | Animators remap keys to speed up repetitive keyframing and timeline work. | **n/a** (Doodleberry is driven by commands from agents and people (CLI, HTTP, MCP, Studio); there is no keyboard-heavy editing surface whose shortcuts would need mapping.) |
| A built-in AI assistant that answers how-to questions about the app and can carry out some actions in the project. | Newer artists ask it how to do a task instead of searching the manual; experienced users use it for repetitive setup. | **command**: `engine.commands` (Agent-native by design: engine.commands exposes every command with its input schema as an agent tool (MCP), so an agent can both explain and do. A chat beside the storyboard is WP-21.) |
| Knowledge base of known issues, fixed issues and FAQs per release. | Teams check known issues before upgrading mid-production. | **n/a** (In-house tool with its history in git and the docs (docs/build-plan.md, feature map); docs.check keeps the docs and command registry in agreement, but a public issue knowledge base has no role here.) |

### Workspaces and interface

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Dockable, saveable workspaces made of panels (project, composition viewer, timeline, effect controls, properties) arranged per task. | Artists keep separate layouts for animation, compositing, review and text work and switch between them per stage. | **partial**: `timeline.get`, `film.preview`, WP-21 (Studio has fixed pages per task (/timeline, /board, /plan, Rig Canvas, /doodle, /actout) built on timeline.get and the player page from film.preview; user-arranged panels and a director's desk combining storyboard, animatic and chat are WP-21.) |
| A context-sensitive Properties panel showing the most-used controls of whatever layer is selected, editable in place. | Motion designers tweak position, scale, text and shape settings without opening the layer's full property tree. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, `film.write`, WP-19 (timeline.edit edits clip layers from the Studio timeline and film.write writes any source file with validation; an in-place property inspector for every shot, cast and overlay field is not built (Studio timeline 2, WP-19).) |
| The core workflow: create a project, build compositions from footage and layers, animate, add effects, preview, then render. | Every job follows this spine, which teams map onto their own stages and checklists. | **command**: `film.create`, `film.write`, `film.preview`, `film.render` (Doodleberry's spine is data-first: film.create, edit brief/facts/script/shots with film.write, preview with film.preview, then film.render (which also mixes and runs QA).) |

### Projects and compositions

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A project file that references (not copies) footage and organises items into folders, with relinking of missing or replaced files. | Large jobs keep hundreds of assets in a tidy folder structure and swap a placeholder for final art in one step. | **data**: `films/<name>/ (brief.md, facts.json, script.json, shots.json, score.json)`, cast library (cast/) (A film is a folder of text files referencing cast puppets and sets by path; replacing a puppet file updates every shot that uses it. film.get returns the whole set.) |
| Composition settings: frame size, pixel aspect ratio, frame rate, duration, background colour, start timecode. | Deliverable specs (9:16 social, 16:9 broadcast, 24/25/30 fps) are fixed per composition at the start of a job. | **data**: `shots.json size`, `shots.json fps`, `shots.json aspects` (Size and fps are film fields validated on write; aspects re-stage one film natively for 16:9 and 1:1. Pixel aspect and timecode are not modelled (square pixels only).) |
| Nesting and pre-composing: grouping layers into a sub-composition that can be reused, transformed and effected as one layer. | Character rigs, lower thirds and repeated graphics are built once as precomps and dropped into many shots. | **partial**: `film.cuts`, WP-36 (Puppets and kit elements are reusable units and film.cuts can insert segments from other films' renders, but a shot cannot contain another shot or group arbitrary layers; nested scene graphs belong with the per-shot compositor (WP-36).) |
| Composition and layer markers holding comments, chapter points, cue points and durations. | Editors and animators mark beats, sync points and client notes on the timeline. | **data**: `script line ids + timing.json (line-relative times)`, `shots.json cues`, `score.json beat grid` (Spoken lines are named time anchors ('hi.start+0.2') that any time field can reference, cues mark sound events and film.beats reports the beat grid; frame notes are review.add (see Collaboration).) |
| Composition Profiler showing how much render time each layer, effect and mask costs. | Compositors find the one expensive effect that is slowing previews and renders. | **gap**: WP-20 (No per-element cost breakdown. WP-20 brings a measured cost and time estimate before rendering; per-element profiling should join it.) |
| Pre-rendering a heavy nested composition and swapping the render in as a proxy. | Finished background passes are baked once so the rest of the comp previews quickly. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-15 (film.render re-renders only shots whose content hash changed and dedupes identical frames; a static-layer cache for unchanging set layers is WP-15.) |
| Project and composition templates to start new jobs from a standard structure. | Studios start every episode of a series from the same template project with naming and render settings in place. | **command**: `film.from_template`, `film.create` (film.from_template builds a storyboard from a format template (myth vs data, explainer, dialogue) and a brief; film.create makes an empty film.) |
| Auto-save and incremental versions of the project file. | Artists recover from crashes and roll back to an earlier version of a shot. | **data**: films as text in git (Films are text under version control, so every change is a diffable commit and any old version re-renders exactly (frames are pure functions of time).) |
| Scene Edit Detection: finding the cuts inside an imported edited video and splitting it into layers. | Used when an offline edit or a reference cut arrives as one flat movie. | **n/a** (Doodleberry builds pictures from shot data, so shot boundaries are always known; it does not ingest flattened edits.) |

### Importing footage

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Importing layered Photoshop and Illustrator files as compositions, keeping each layer separate and editable. | Illustrators design characters and backgrounds in layered files that animators bring in with layers intact. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, WP-18 (puppet.import reads layered SVG, PSD and ORA and builds a rig from layer names (parts, swaps, pivot markers); Krita .kra and Illustrator .ai import are WP-18.) |
| Importing SVG files directly as editable vector shape layers (added in the 26.0 release). | Vector assets from design tools come in without a detour through Illustrator. | **command**: `puppet.import` (Layered SVG is a native input of puppet.import (as puppet parts, not free-floating shape layers in a shot).) |
| Importing video and audio footage and interpreting it: frame rate, field order, pixel aspect, looping, start timecode. | Live-action plates, stock clips and reference video are conformed to the composition's settings before compositing. | **gap**: WP-36 (Shots have no footage layer; video is only read as performance input (actout.clip). A video or plate layer belongs with the compositor (WP-36).) |
| Interpreting alpha as straight or premultiplied to avoid fringes on semi-transparent edges. | Compositors fix halos on rendered elements from other packages. | **gap**: WP-36 (Internally Synfig and Grease Pencil renders are composited with alpha over the set by ffmpeg; user-supplied footage with alpha needs WP-36 (and alpha outputs WP-23).) |
| Importing still images and numbered image sequences (PNG, TIFF, EXR) as footage. | Painted backgrounds, hand-drawn frame sequences and CG passes arrive as stills or sequences. | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, `doodle.snap`, WP-35 (Photos and scans become raster-part puppets (puppet.from_ink, doodle.snap); a sequence of drawings with exposures is the drawn-level part type in WP-35. Painted background plates as set layers would sit in WP-36.) |
| Recorded voice-over and music import, placed on the timeline and synced to picture. | Productions bring in recorded dialogue and licensed music and cut the picture to them. | **partial**: `script.slots`, `voice.build`, WP-13 (Narration slots and word alignment exist (script.slots, voice.build aligns words); fitting recorded human takes into slots with phone-level alignment is WP-13.) |
| Importing 3D models (glTF, GLB, OBJ and, since 25.2, FBX) into a composition. | Motion designers drop product models or props into 2D designs and light them in place. | **partial**: `puppet.from_blend`, WP-42 (puppet.from_blend flattens a Blender cut-out rig into a 2D puppet; GLB models play in the three.js 3D pages (tools/three/run3d.mjs, e.g. films/screwworm-3d) but not through a command. 3D sets and models in films are WP-42.) |
| HDR footage import and export with colour metadata (HLG and PQ, CICP tags; extended in 25.4). | HDR deliverables for streaming and broadcast keep correct colour tags end to end. | **gap**: WP-37 (SDR only today; an HDR master is in WP-37.) |
| Importing Premiere Pro sequences and After Effects projects into a project. | Editors send a cut to motion graphics, who rebuild it as compositions. | **partial**: `film.cuts`, WP-22 (film.cuts writes OTIO per cut for editors; reading timelines back in (FCPXML, EDL, round trips through melt) is WP-22.) |

### Layers, markers and camera

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Many layer types: footage, solids, text, shapes, nulls, adjustment layers, cameras and lights, all stacked in one timeline. | Everything in a motion graphics shot is a layer, so artists build scenes by stacking and ordering them. | **partial**: `film.write`, WP-36 (A shot has a fixed stack: set, underlays, cast, overlays (kit elements, signs, text), bubbles, then film-level captions, plus camera keys. No free solids, nulls or adjustment layers; those come with the compositor (WP-36).) |
| Layer in and out points, trimming, sliding and splitting layers in time. | Editors retime when a graphic appears and cut a layer to reuse half of it. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (Clip layers move and resize with timeline.edit; shot, overlay and cue times are start/end fields (line-relative allowed). Dragging lines and shots in Studio is WP-19.) |
| Parenting layers to each other and to null objects so children inherit transforms. | Rigs and grouped graphics are driven from one controller null. | **data**: puppet parts parent (bone hierarchy), prop attach keys (parent switching), kit face attach {cast, part} (Hierarchy exists inside puppets and for props passing from hand to hand; arbitrary parenting between unrelated shot elements does not.) |
| Transform properties on every layer: anchor point, position, scale, rotation, opacity, each keyframable. | The basic vocabulary of motion graphics: moves, pops, spins and fades. | **partial**: `film.write`, WP-36 (Characters have place keys (x, y, scale, flip) and per-part angles via clips; kit elements have position, size and start/end with built-in fades. Whole-element rotation and opacity keys are missing; general layer transforms should land with WP-36.) |
| Blending modes (multiply, screen, add, overlay, colour dodge and others) per layer. | Used for light effects, shadows, textures laid over art and colour treatments. | **gap**: WP-36 (No user-facing blend modes; the node compositor (WP-36) lists them.) |
| Layer styles such as drop shadow, inner glow, bevel and stroke, editable like Photoshop styles. | Quick finishing on text and logos. | **gap**: WP-36 (Only ground shadows and toon cel shading/rims exist; the AI-look lint (WP-04) deliberately flags drop shadows and glows, so styles would be opt-in within WP-36.) |
| Layer and composition markers that carry comments, durations and cue information. | Animators note client feedback and sync points directly on layers. | **command**: `review.add`, `review.list` (review.add pins a note to a frame; review.list shows notes with status. Sync points are line-relative times and cues (see Projects).) |
| 3D layers: any 2D layer can move and rotate in X, Y and Z and be seen through a camera. | Motion designers build 2.5D parallax scenes from flat artwork. | **gap**: WP-36 (No 2.5D layers in shot films; true 2D multiplane depth (layers on planes, parallax) is WP-36. Code-built three.js pages exist for full 3D (see 3D chapter).) |
| Camera moves on a 2D frame: pan, zoom, push-in, follow a subject, handheld shake. | Directors add life and emphasis without redrawing art. | **data**: `shots.json camera [[t, [cx, cy, zoom]]]`, `shots.json camera_auto`, `style.float` (Camera keys are baked into every renderer; camera_auto frames the speaker, eases between shots, punches in on emphasis and adds seeded handheld; float adds slow drift.) |
| 3D cameras (one-node and two-node) with focal length, point of interest, orbit and depth of field. | Used for dimensional fly-throughs and focus pulls across layered art. | **gap**: WP-36 (Camera is 2D (centre and zoom). Depth of field is available only as blur filters in hand-coded scene.js films (engine/draw.js); a multiplane camera with depth of field is WP-36.) |
| Lights (point, spot, parallel, ambient, environment) that shade 3D layers and cast shadows. | Lighting gives flat art depth and mood and unifies elements from different sources. | **gap**: WP-42 (style.toon gives a fixed-direction cel shadow and rim on vector parts, which is the 2D answer; real lights on layers or models come with the 3D layer (WP-42).) |
| Continuous rasterisation of vector layers so they stay sharp at any scale. | Logos and shapes can be pushed in close without softening. | **data**: renderer: svg (Vector puppets, sets and kit elements are drawn as SVG every frame, so they are resolution-independent (4K for free); raster parts are the exception.) |
| Quick Offset (25.4): staggering the timing of several selected layers or keyframes in one gesture. | Cascading reveals of lists, letters or icons in seconds. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (Staggers exist inside kit elements (title per_word, draw stagger) and acting; a general stagger edit on many selected items in the timeline belongs with timeline 2 (WP-19).) |

### Animation, keyframes and time

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Keyframes on any property, with setting, selecting, copying, pasting and moving keys across layers. | The base of all hand animation and motion graphics work. | **data**: `shots.json keys (place, look, view, poses, ik, camera)`, puppet clips tracks, Rig Canvas Key / Save clip (Keys are [time, value] pairs in film and puppet data, line-relative where useful; the Rig Canvas records keyed poses into clips. Not every field is keyable (e.g. element opacity).) |
| Temporal interpolation choices per key: linear, Bezier, auto and continuous Bezier, and hold. | Animators control whether a move snaps, glides or cushions into a pose. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (Clips ease between keys with a smooth curve, views and swaps hold, and the scene.js library (engine/timeline.js) offers named eases (cubic, expo, back). shots.json keys have no per-key interpolation choice; that comes with the curve editor (WP-19).) |
| Graph Editor: value and speed graphs with tangent handles for shaping motion. | Senior animators polish timing and spacing on curves rather than by moving keys. | **gap**: WP-19 (No curve editor; WP-19 lists a graph editor for keys.) |
| Keyframe assistants: Easy Ease in/out, exponential scale, sequence layers, time-reverse keys. | One-click cushioning and quick sequencing of many layers. | **partial**: `acting.plan`, WP-19 (Ease is the default, the principles filter adds anticipation and overshoot, and acting.plan sequences gestures from the voice; key-level assistants on a selection are WP-19.) |
| Spatial interpolation and editable motion paths in the viewer, with auto-orient along the path. | Moves follow designed arcs; a plane or arrow turns to face where it is going. | **partial**: `shots.json place.path`, `shots.json props path`, WP-20 (Characters walk and props travel along Catmull-Rom paths with ease, auto-oriented; editing the paths in a viewer is WP-20 (graph view).) |
| Roving keyframes that redistribute timing so speed stays even along a multi-key path. | Smooth camera and object moves through several positions. | **gap**: WP-19 (Not available; it is a key-editing feature for the curve editor work in WP-19.) |
| Puppet tools: pins placed on flat artwork deform it as a mesh (position, starch, bend and advanced pins). | Flat illustrations get organic, rubbery motion without a full rig. | **data**: `puppet mesh strokes with skinning (v0.5)`, bendy limbs, `chains (v0.6, FABRIK)`, springs (Doodleberry's puppets deform by skinned meshes, bendy limbs and N-bone chains with springs: a rig rather than pins, renderers other than SVG pending (WP-14).) |
| Animating shape and mask paths by keying the path itself, morphing one outline into another. | Logo morphs, liquid transitions and shape-changing icons. | **gap**: WP-43 (Shapes change by swap (drawing substitution), not by path morph; morphs between views are planned in WP-43, and general path morphing for graphics should join it.) |
| Motion Sketch: recording a mouse drag in real time as position keyframes. | Quick organic paths for flies, sparks or a hand-held feel. | **command**: `puppet.save`, `actout.clip` (The Rig Canvas records drags into poses and clips that puppet.save stores; actout.clip turns a filmed performance into a clip.) |
| Wiggler and Smoother assistants: adding seeded random variation to keys, or thinning noisy keys. | Organic jitter on flickers and handheld moves; cleaning tracked or recorded data. | **data**: `camera_auto.handheld`, timeline seed, boil seeds (Variation comes from seeded noise (camera handheld, idle, boil); actout.clip reduces recorded keys against the played curve.) |
| Time remapping, time stretch, freeze frames and reverse playback of layers. | Speed ramps, holds on a pose and slow-motion beats. | **partial**: `film.soundoff`, WP-19 (Clip layers have a speed field and the sound-off cut warps film time through timewarp knots (film.soundoff); arbitrary per-shot ramps, freezes and reverse are not exposed. Editing them in the timeline fits WP-19.) |
| Frame blending and pixel-motion interpolation when footage is retimed. | Smooth slow motion from footage shot at normal speed. | **n/a** (Picture is re-rendered at the warped time (every frame is a pure function of time), so there are no source frames to blend.) |
| Per-layer motion blur with shutter angle and phase. | Fast motion graphics read naturally and lose strobing. | **gap**: WP-15 (Speed lines exist (fx); sub-frame motion blur on fast shots is WP-15.) |
| Posterize-time style stepping so animation plays on twos or threes. | A hand-made, limited-animation look in motion graphics. | **data**: `style.step (12, 8, 6)` (Stepped timing per shot or character, with identical frames deduplicated at render.) |
| Data-driven animation: charts and properties driven from JSON or CSV files. | Infographics update by swapping the data file instead of re-animating. | **command**: `film.data` (film.data fetches cached sources, runs analysis scripts and binds results into text ({{path/format}}) and charts; it reports which shots and lines the new data changes.) |
| Converting audio amplitude to keyframes to drive animation from sound. | Visuals that pulse to the music or the voice. | **command**: `acting.plan`, `film.beats` (acting.plan places gestures from loudness, pitch and stress of the voice; film.beats gives the beat grid for cutting and cues. Raw amplitude-to-property mapping is not exposed.) |

### Motion tracking, stabilising and keying

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Point tracking (one, two or four points, corner pin) and applying the track to another layer or effect. | Labels, screens and effects are stuck to moving objects in footage. | **data**: kit face attach {cast, part}, prop attach keys, bubble tails track the speaker (Doodleberry needs no image tracking: every element's position is known, so attaching to a moving part is exact. Tracking live footage is outside its scope.) |
| Stabilising shaky footage (point stabilise and Warp Stabilizer). | Handheld plates are smoothed before compositing. | **n/a** (No live-action plates. The related need, compensating camera motion in Act it out takes, is in WP-12.) |
| 3D Camera Tracker: solving camera motion from footage and placing 3D elements in the scene. | Integrating graphics and text into live-action shots with matched perspective. | **n/a** (Films are fully synthetic, so the camera is known exactly; there is nothing to solve.) |
| Planar tracking through the bundled Mocha plug-in. | Screen replacements and tracking flat surfaces that rotate in perspective. | **n/a** (No footage pipeline.) |
| Face tracking: following face outlines and detailed features (eyes, brows, mouth) to drive effects or animation. | Retouching, masking faces, or driving a character from a performer's face. | **command**: `actout.clip` (actout.clip reads face blendshapes (MediaPipe Face Landmarker) and makes eye, brow, gaze and mouth keys on a character; live tracking is WP-12.) |
| Mask tracking: masks that follow an object through footage. | Isolating a moving region for colour or blur. | **n/a** (No footage to mask; masks themselves are a gap under Drawing and paths (WP-36).) |
| Keying: Keylight and other colour keys with spill suppression and matte choking. | Pulling green-screen subjects and compositing them over new backgrounds. | **n/a** (No green-screen footage; Act it out reads pose from ordinary takes and never composites the performer. If live action is ever composited it would come with footage layers in WP-36.) |

### Text and graphics

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Point and paragraph text layers with full typographic control: font, size, tracking, leading, alignment, fill and stroke. | Titles, lower thirds and on-screen copy set to a brand's type system. | **partial**: `film.write`, WP-17 (Text comes as overlays (text, sign), kit elements (title, card, paper, quote, citation, wordmark), bubbles and styled captions (caption_style), in pinned OFL fonts with Sinhala fallback; per-character typographic control and narration boxes are WP-17.) |
| Text animators with range, wiggly and expression selectors that animate properties per character, word or line. | Kinetic typography: words pop, cascade and jitter in designed patterns. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-15 (Word-highlight captions (burned in by film.render), per-word title pop-ins and voice-synced bubble reveals exist; general per-character animators are WP-15 (kinetic typography).) |
| Text animation presets such as typewriter, fade or blur in by character or word. | Fast, consistent text reveals across a series. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-17 (Bubbles reveal word by word in sync with the voice; letter-by-letter typewriter with punctuation pauses is WP-17.) |
| Text on a path, with characters flowing along a curve. | Titles that follow a shape, circular badges, text riding a line. | **gap**: WP-15 (Not available; fits kinetic typography in WP-15.) |
| Source-text expressions and data-driven text that change with other properties or a data file. | Counters, live numbers and template text filled from data. | **command**: `film.data` (Any text in shots.json or script lines can hold {{path/format}} bindings to data.json, and counter elements animate numbers.) |
| Animating variable font axes such as weight and width (added in 26.0). | Type that breathes or swells for emphasis from one font file. | **gap**: WP-15 (Not available; would be part of kinetic typography (WP-15).) |
| Extruded 3D text and shapes with bevels in the Advanced 3D renderer. | Dimensional titles and logo stings. | **gap**: WP-42 (No 3D text; the 3D layer (WP-42) would carry it.) |
| Motion Graphics templates: packaging a composition with chosen editable controls for editors in another app. | Design teams ship branded lower thirds and titles that editors fill in without opening the project. | **partial**: `film.from_template`, `brand.check`, WP-29 (Format templates and kit elements take fields, and brand kits flow into every film that opts in; packaging rigs, boil presets and loop packs as reusable templates for others is WP-29.) |
| Essential Properties: overriding chosen properties on each instance of a nested composition. | One master graphic used many times with different text, colour or image per instance. | **data**: `shots.json set_opts`, puppet palette slots (runtime recolour) (Each use of a set or puppet can recolour or reconfigure it without copying the source; arbitrary property overrides would need nested scenes (WP-36).) |
| Pasting text formatting and character styles between text layers (25.4). | Keeping type consistent across many titles. | **data**: `brand/<id>.json (fonts, caption colours)`, `shots.json caption_style` (Style is set centrally in the brand kit and caption style, so consistency is by construction rather than copy and paste.) |

### Drawing, painting and paths

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Shape layers: parametric rectangles, ellipses, polygons and stars plus free Bezier paths with fills and strokes. | Flat-design motion graphics, icons and diagrams drawn inside the app. | **data**: overlays/underlays: draw {paths, stroke, width, fill}, Rig Canvas vector drawing (Graphics are kit elements, code-drawn sets and Rig Canvas drawings for puppets; there are no parametric primitive shapes to place freely in a shot.) |
| Shape operators: repeater, merge paths, offset paths, pucker and bloat, twist, round corners, zig zag. | Procedural patterns, bursts and stylised outlines from one simple shape. | **gap**: WP-42 (No path operators; repeaters and instancing fit procedural 2D in WP-42.) |
| Trim Paths: animating how much of a stroke is drawn, for write-on and line-draw reveals. | Signature reveals, routes on maps and diagrams that draw themselves. | **data**: overlays: draw (duration/until, stagger) (The kit's draw element makes paths draw themselves and then optionally fill, timed to a line.) |
| Wiggle paths and turbulent displacement for a hand-drawn, shimmering line. | Gives clean vector art a boiling, hand-made feel. | **data**: `style.boil {fps, amount, freq}` (Deterministic line boil on characters, bubbles and signs (cycled seeds).) |
| Tapered and wavy strokes on shape paths. | Expressive, ink-like lines from vector paths. | **data**: Rig Canvas pressure strokes, `engine/draw.js tapered strokes`, fx speed_lines (Pressure ink in the Rig Canvas, tapered strokes in the scene library and tapered speed lines; there is no per-stroke taper control in shots.json.) |
| Masks: Bezier masks with feather (including variable feather), expansion, opacity and add/subtract/intersect modes. | Revealing parts of layers, vignettes, wipes and isolating regions for effects. | **gap**: WP-36 (SVG clipping is used internally (toon shading, mesh triangles) and polygons cut parts on import, but there are no user masks in shots; masks and mattes are WP-36.) |
| Paint tools: Brush, Clone Stamp and Eraser strokes on layers, frame by frame or held. | Hand-drawn effects, clean-up and quick touch-ups directly on footage. | **gap**: WP-35 (No frame-by-frame painting; drawn levels and hand-drawn effects are WP-35, brush engines in WP-34.) |
| Roto Brush and Object Matte: AI-assisted separation of a subject from its background across frames, with edge refinement. | Isolating people or objects in footage without hand rotoscoping. | **gap**: WP-18 (No footage roto. The related need, click-to-mask cutting of drawings into parts (SAM 2.1), is WP-18; doodle.snap already suggests cuts automatically.) |
| Content-Aware Fill: removing objects from footage by filling from surrounding frames. | Erasing rigs, signs or crew from live-action shots. | **n/a** (Every element is data, so unwanted things are deleted from the film rather than painted out.) |
| Auto-trace: creating masks or paths from a layer's alpha or luminance. | Turning scanned artwork or logos into editable vectors. | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, WP-18 (puppet.from_ink thresholds ink to alpha and keeps the raster line; vector tracing (VTracer, potrace) is WP-18.) |

### Transparency and compositing

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Alpha channels throughout: rendering, compositing and outputting with transparency. | Elements are rendered separately and layered, or delivered with alpha for other tools. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-23 (Synfig and Grease Pencil characters render with alpha and are composited over the set; delivering video with alpha (ProRes 4444, WebM) is WP-23.) |
| Track mattes (alpha and luma, inverted) using any layer as the matte. | Text filled with footage, reveals through shapes, holdouts. | **gap**: WP-36 (Not available; mattes are part of the node compositor (WP-36).) |
| Adjustment layers that apply effects to everything below them for a time range. | One grade or blur across a group of layers, timed to a section. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-36 (The post finishing pass (grade, LUT, vignette, grain) applies to the whole joined picture before captions; per-shot or per-layer-range adjustment is WP-36.) |
| Preserve underlying transparency, stencil and silhouette modes. | Textures confined to the art beneath, cut-outs through a stack. | **gap**: WP-36 (Not available; belongs to the compositor (WP-36).) |
| Compositing order and per-element depth switching. | Arms passing in front of and behind a body, props behind or in front of characters. | **data**: overlays vs underlays, puppet controls z, `clip swaps.z` (Elements stack in fixed bands, and puppets switch part order by control or clip (static in Lottie and Synfig, WP-14).) |
| Glows, light wraps and edge blends to integrate elements from different sources. | Making graphics and characters sit naturally in a background. | **gap**: WP-36 (Not available; WP-36 lists glows and light wraps.) |

### Adjusting colour

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Project bit depth of 8, 16 or 32-bit float, with linear-light blending. | High-end compositing avoids banding and blends light realistically. | **partial**: `film.master`, WP-37 (Masters are half-float linear ACEScg; compositing inside the player stays 8-bit sRGB (linear-light blending is WP-37).) |
| ICC colour management: a working space, per-footage input profiles and output simulation. | Colour stays consistent across cameras, design files and deliverables. | **gap**: WP-37 (Everything is assumed sRGB (Grease Pencil colours are converted to linear internally); colour management is WP-37.) |
| OpenColorIO and ACES colour management with display and view transforms plus OCIO effects. | Film and VFX pipelines share one colour space across every app. | **command**: `film.master` (film.master manages colour with OpenColorIO 2.6 and its built-in ACES 2.1 studio config (ACEScg working space); film.from_master applies the inverse texture transform or the ACES output transform for review.) |
| Colour correction effects: curves, levels, hue and saturation, colour balance, Lumetri, and LUT application. | Grading shots to a look and matching elements to each other. | **data**: `shots.json post {preset, grade, lut, vignette, grain}` (Film-wide finishing with presets (crisp, film, warm, cool, paper, noir), arbitrary ffmpeg grade filters and a .cube LUT. Per-shot grades and a colour script are WP-37.) |
| HDR monitoring of compositions in HLG or PQ on local and external displays (25.2) and HDR metadata on export (25.4). | HDR deliverables are judged on a real HDR display while working. | **gap**: WP-37 (SDR only; an HDR master is in WP-37.) |
| Colour scopes (waveform, vectorscope, histogram) for judging exposure and colour. | Graders check legal levels and balance objectively. | **partial**: `brand.check`, WP-24 (brand.check measures palette contrast and colour-blind separation; checks on rendered frames (colour-blind, flash) are WP-24. No scopes.) |
| Recolouring art consistently across a project. | Night versions, seasonal variants or a client's new brand colours. | **partial**: `puppet.lint`, WP-16 (Puppets use palette slots (puppet.lint flags raw colours) and sets recolour with set_opts; palette variants per scene (night, sick, cold) are WP-16.) |

### Effects and animation presets

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A large built-in effect library, stacked per layer in any order with every parameter keyframable. | Most looks in motion graphics are built by stacking a few standard effects. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-36 (A fixed set of looks (toon shading, boil, motion fx, post grade/grain/vignette/LUT, depth-of-field blur in scene.js) with no general effect stack; the node compositor is WP-36.) |
| Blur and sharpen effects: Gaussian, directional, camera lens blur, sharpen. | Depth cues, focus pulls and softening backgrounds. | **gap**: WP-36 (Depth-of-field blur filters exist only in hand-coded scene.js films (engine/draw.js); a shot-level blur node is WP-36.) |
| Distort effects: turbulent displace, warp, bulge, mesh warp, bend. | Organic wobble, hand-made shimmer and cartoon squish. | **data**: `style.boil`, puppet bendy limbs and squash (Turbulent displacement is the boil; squash and bendy limbs come from the rig. General warp effects are not available.) |
| Generate effects: gradients, fractal noise, grids, fills, audio waveforms, strokes along paths. | Backgrounds, textures and design elements made without imported art. | **data**: `sets (sets/sets.js, sets/more.js)`, `shots.json set_opts`, kit elements (Sets and kit elements are drawn in code as functions of time (paper, water, sections, roots and others); there is no general generator library.) |
| Stylize effects: glow, cartoon, posterize, find edges, roughen edges. | Stylised and graphic looks from plain art. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-36 (style.toon gives cel shadows and rims; glow and stylize nodes are WP-36, and medium simulation for lines (pencil, marker, crayon) is WP-15.) |
| Noise and grain effects: add, match and remove grain. | Film texture and matching grain between elements. | **data**: `shots.json post.grain (seeded)` (Seeded grain over the joined picture; matching and removal are not needed for synthetic frames.) |
| Simulation effects: particle systems, shatter, card dance, foam, rain and snow. | Sparks, dust, weather and explosions in motion graphics. | **partial**: `shots.json comp particles`, WP-42 (Seeded particles (confetti, snow, rain) on compositor planes, motion-derived dust and impact stars, and prop physics exist; shatter, card dance and foam are WP-42.) |
| Time effects: echo, posterize time, time displacement, timewarp. | Trails, stepped motion and time-based distortions. | **partial**: `shot.render`, `shots.json fx.arcs`, WP-36 (Stepped timing (style.step), speed lines, motion arcs and prop smears with multiples exist; echo, time displacement and timewarp are WP-36.) |
| Transition effects: dissolves, linear, radial, gradient and block wipes, card wipes. | Moving between scenes and revealing graphics. | **gap**: WP-15 (Cuts carry a whoosh and QA checks seam smoothness, but picture transitions (dissolves, match cuts, wipes) are WP-15.) |
| Perspective effects: drop shadow, bevel alpha, 3D glasses, sphere wrap. | Depth on flat graphics and quick dimensional looks. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-36 (Characters get ground shadows (style.shadow); drop shadows on graphics are deliberately discouraged by the AI-look lint, other perspective effects would be WP-36 nodes.) |
| Audio effects: EQ, reverb, delay, tone, and (26.0) gate and compressor, with level keyframes. | Basic sound shaping and level rides without leaving the comp. | **command**: `film.render` (film.render mixes automatically: voice cleanup and compression, ducking of music and effects, seeded room reverb, loudness to -14 LUFS with true-peak limiting, cue gains. Hand editing cues in a waveform lane is WP-40, room reverb per set WP-13.) |
| Animation presets: saving effects, keyframes and expressions as a reusable preset and browsing a preset library. | Studios build libraries of reveals, bounces and looks to apply in one step. | **data**: clip library (33 template-level clips), puppet poses and clips, post presets, `acting.direct gags` (Clips, poses, finishing presets and gag patterns are reusable presets that apply to any character on a template.) |
| Effect Manager: seeing which effects and plug-ins are installed and turning them on or off. | Pipeline teams keep render nodes consistent and troubleshoot plug-in conflicts. | **partial**: `engine.info`, `vendor.check`, WP-34 (engine.info lists renderers and tools found; vendor.check lists vendored components. There is no effect plug-in interface to manage yet (open-source integration, WP-34).) |
| Detail-preserving upscaling of footage. | Using low-resolution sources in high-resolution deliverables. | **data**: renderer: svg (vector) (Vector art re-renders at any resolution; raster parts would need upscaling but none is planned.) |

### Expressions and automation

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| JavaScript expressions on any property that compute its value every frame. | Procedural motion, rigs and automation without hand-placed keys. | **data**: `films/<name>/scene.js frame(f)`, `engine/timeline.js (eases, springs, seeded noise)` (Code films are expressions all the way down: every value is a pure function of the frame number. Shot-based films expose no per-property code; adding it would join WP-36.) |
| Pick-whip linking: driving one property from another layer's property. | Rigging controls, linking a shadow to its object, chaining motion. | **data**: puppet controls (driven keys), timeline look (look-at), kit face attach (Driven keys map a control to angles, shifts, swaps and z; look-at and attachment link elements. No general property-to-property links in shots.) |
| Expression controls (slider, checkbox, colour, point, angle, layer, dropdown) used as rig controls. | Animators drive a rig from a few named controls instead of many properties. | **data**: `puppet controls (e.g. turn)`, timeline emotion, view, look (Named controls such as turn drive head three-quarter offsets and swaps; emotion, view and gaze are keyable controls on the acting timeline.) |
| Looping and noise functions (loopOut, loopIn, wiggle, time-based maths). | Cycles, idles and organic jitter set once and left running. | **data**: puppet clips loop, chains wave, timeline seed, idle clip (Looping clips (idle, walk), travelling chain waves and seeded noise give the same results deterministically.) |
| An expression editor with syntax checking and clear error messages. | Catching broken expressions before they reach a render. | **command**: `film.write`, `film.validate` (Film data is checked against JSON schemas and its own references on every write and before every render, with errors reported; there is no code editor for scene.js.) |
| Reading JSON and CSV data inside expressions to drive properties. | Data-driven graphics and template automation. | **command**: `film.data` (film.data runs analysis scripts into data.json and binds it into text and charts; it re-voices lines whose numbers change.) |
| Scripting (ExtendScript and JavaScript) and script panels to automate project tasks. | Pipeline tools batch-create comps, rename layers, build renders and integrate with asset systems. | **command**: `engine.commands` (Every feature is a command reachable from CLI, HTTP, Studio and MCP; engine.commands lists them with schemas. This is Doodleberry's core strength.) |
| Responsive design for time: protecting regions of a composition so stretching it keeps intros and outros intact. | Templates that change length without breaking their animation. | **command**: `film.retime`, `script.slots` (Times reference spoken lines, so film.retime re-derives shots after a voice change; script.slots fixes line slots before voicing.) |

### 3D, immersive video and VR

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| The Advanced 3D renderer: a GPU renderer for 3D layers and models with realistic shading. | Motion designers add 3D objects to 2D designs without a separate 3D app. | **gap**: WP-42 (A three.js renderer exists for hand-coded 3D pages (tools/three/run3d.mjs, software WebGL, about 1 s per frame) but not as a shot renderer or command; 3D sets and EEVEE passes are WP-42.) |
| Playing embedded animations in imported 3D models and retiming them. | Animated product shots and characters from 3D apps used directly. | **gap**: WP-42 (The three.js pages play GLB clips deterministically and CMU mocap can be retargeted in Blender (tools/blender), outside the command set; WP-42 brings it in.) |
| Image-based lighting from HDR images, including animated environment lights (25.2). | Realistic reflections and ambient light that match a background. | **gap**: WP-42 (Not available.) |
| Cast and accept shadows per layer, and shadow catchers for grounding 3D objects on footage. | Making 3D objects sit in a scene. | **gap**: WP-42 (2D ground shadows and toon cel shadows only; 3D shadows come with WP-42.) |
| Materials on 3D models and layers, including Substance 3D materials (26.0). | Changing the look of models without re-exporting them. | **gap**: WP-42 (Not available.) |
| Native parametric 3D meshes (cubes, spheres, cones; 26.0) created and animated inside a composition. | Simple 3D design elements without modelling software. | **gap**: WP-42 (Not available.) |
| 3D navigation and transform gizmos, a 3D design space, and extracting cameras and lights from models. | Fast staging of 3D scenes. | **gap**: WP-42 (No 3D staging; scenes are 2D with a 2D camera.) |
| In-renderer depth of field and depth data extraction. | Focus pulls and depth-based compositing. | **gap**: WP-36 (Blur-based depth of field exists in scene.js films only; multiplane depth of field is WP-36, depth estimates for drawings in WP-43.) |
| VR 360 and 180 tools: VR environments, a VR comp editor, immersive effects, cube-map output. | Producing 360-degree video for headsets and platforms. | **n/a** (Doodleberry targets flat social and web video; no 360 delivery is planned.) |
| Stereoscopic 3D camera rigs. | Stereo deliverables for 3D cinema or headsets. | **n/a** (No stereo deliverables in scope.) |

### Views and previews

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Real-time cached playback using RAM and disk (a new high-performance preview engine in 25.2). | Animators judge timing at full speed while they work. | **command**: `film.preview`, `export.web` (film.preview writes a live player page where every frame is a pure function of time, so it plays and scrubs without a render; export.web packages it as a standalone player.) |
| Scrubbing, frame stepping and previewing from the current time in a range. | Checking a pose or a sync point frame by frame. | **command**: `film.preview`, `timeline.get` (Studio /timeline scrubs the live player with clip, emotion, gaze and dialogue lanes from timeline.get.) |
| Draft preview: lower resolution, skipped frames, region of interest, fast-draft modes. | Rough timing passes on heavy comps. | **command**: `film.animatic`, `film.storyboard` (Different mechanism, same purpose: film.animatic holds storyboard stills over the sound in seconds; film.storyboard makes the stills and contact PDF.) |
| Guides, grids, rulers and title/action-safe overlays in the viewer. | Keeping text and key action inside safe areas for each platform. | **metric**: `qa.run` (qa.run measures every word's box against the platform-safe area and minimum type sizes, with platform UI overlays; brand.assets also writes safe-area overlays.) |
| Transparency grid and channel views (alpha, individual RGB channels; custom grid colours in 25.2). | Checking mattes and edges before delivering with alpha. | **gap**: WP-23 (No alpha deliverables yet, so no alpha views; they come with alpha outputs in WP-23.) |
| Video preview on an external broadcast monitor (Mercury Transmit), including HDR. | Colour-critical review on a calibrated display. | **gap**: WP-37 (Not available; colour-critical and HDR review fit WP-37.) |
| Snapshots and side-by-side views to compare two states of a frame. | Before-and-after checks of a tweak. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-19 (shot.render with a renderer override writes a second file for side-by-side comparison; a visual diff between two versions of a film is WP-19.) |

### Rendering and exporting

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Render Queue with render settings and several output modules per item. | One render pass writes the master, a review proxy and an alpha version at once. | **partial**: `film.render`, `film.cuts`, WP-23 (film.render writes the master (plus aspects, sound-off cut, captions); film.cuts assembles many cuts from it. Extra formats per render (WebM, ProRes, GIF, APNG) are WP-23.) |
| Saved output-module and render-setting templates. | House delivery specs applied consistently. | **data**: `dbe/profiles.json (encoding profiles)` (Encoding profiles per platform (H.264 High CRF 18, AAC 48 kHz, faststart, size caps).) |
| Codecs with alpha and intermediates (ProRes 4444, PNG sequences), and hand-off to Media Encoder for H.264 or HEVC. | Handing elements to editors and compositors at full quality. | **gap**: WP-23 (H.264 masters only; alpha video and intermediates are WP-23.) |
| Rendering still frames and image sequences (PNG, TIFF, EXR) including multi-channel output. | Posters, thumbnails, print stills and passes for further compositing. | **partial**: `film.storyboard`, `cover.make`, `puppet.sheet`, WP-37 (Stills come from storyboards, covers and model sheets; print-ready stills are WP-23 and OpenEXR multi-pass output WP-37.) |
| Multi-frame rendering that uses many CPU cores at once. | Large speed-ups on long or heavy renders. | **command**: `film.render`, `shot.render` (Rendering runs a browser per worker on frame ranges and dedupes identical frames.) |
| Command-line rendering (aerender) for unattended and scripted renders. | Render farms and overnight batches. | **command**: `film.render`, `shot.render` (Every render is a command (CLI, HTTP with async jobs, MCP).) |
| Network rendering and watch-folder rendering across several machines. | Splitting big jobs across a farm. | **gap**: WP-38 (Single machine; distributed render workers and a shared cache are WP-38.) |
| Exporting a composition as a Premiere Pro project for editing. | Moving finished graphics into the edit with structure intact. | **partial**: `film.handoff`, WP-22 (film.handoff writes the cut as OTIO, FCP 7 XML (Premiere, Resolve), FCPXML, EDL and Kdenlive with stems and captions; a native Premiere project is WP-22.) |
| Notifications when a render finishes (desktop or mobile). | Artists leave long renders running and get pinged. | **gap**: WP-20 (Async jobs exist but no progress events or webhooks; WP-20 (and owner notifications in WP-03).) |
| Rendering audio along with picture and as separate stems. | Sound teams get clean stems for final mixing. | **command**: `film.render` (The mix writes stems and a music-only version; film.cuts reuses the master's stems.) |

### Working with other applications

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Dynamic Link: compositions used live in Premiere Pro or Media Encoder without rendering first. | Editors see graphics updates in the edit immediately. | **gap**: WP-22 (No live link; hand-off is by rendered cuts and OTIO. Round trips with editors are WP-22.) |
| Round-trip editing of Photoshop and Illustrator sources, updating in the comp. | Artists repaint a layer and the animation updates. | **command**: `puppet.export_layers`, `puppet.import` (puppet.export_layers hands a puppet to an artist as a layered file; puppet.import brings the repaint back onto the rig.) |
| Cinema 4D integration through Cineware and the bundled C4D renderer. | 3D elements built in a companion app rendered inside the comp. | **partial**: `proc.cook`, WP-42 (Blender renders Geometry Nodes 3D sets into shots (proc.cook), besides cut-out rigs in and Grease Pencil out; a live 3D scene link is WP-42.) |
| Lottie export through the Bodymovin or LottieFiles plug-ins (third-party), for web and app animation. | UI and web animations made in After Effects play natively in apps. | **command**: `export.lottie` (export.lottie bakes one shot's puppet animation (views, swaps, lip-sync, springs) to Lottie JSON. dotLottie, Telegram stickers and Lottie QA in two renderers are WP-23.) |
| Creative Cloud Libraries: shared colours, character styles and graphics across projects and team members. | Brand assets stay consistent across a team. | **data**: `brand/<id>.json brand kits`, `cast library (cast.list)`, sets library (Brand kits, the cast library and the set library are shared, versioned data that every film can inherit.) |
| A large third-party plug-in ecosystem (effects, rigging and particle tools). | Studios extend the app with specialised tools for particles, rigging and 3D. | **partial**: `vendor.check`, `vendor.build`, WP-34 (Doodleberry vendors open-source code with recorded licences (vendor.check, vendor.build); a plan for driving or linking open tools (Natron, OpenFX, Krita brushes) is WP-34.) |
| Round trip to an audio editor (Audition) for deeper sound work. | Cleaning dialogue and building sound design outside the comp. | **gap**: WP-40 (Sound is mixed automatically; driving Audacity or Ardour and editing cues in Studio is WP-40.) |

### Collaboration: Frame.io and Team Projects

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Frame.io review: upload renders from the app and see reviewers' frame-accurate comments as timeline markers. | Client and director feedback arrives on the exact frame without email. | **command**: `review.add`, `review.list`, `review.fix` (Notes are pinned to frames and review.fix turns them into data edits, re-renders and measures the change; review on a phone is WP-19.) |
| Team Projects: a shared cloud project that several artists edit, publish and sync. | Editors and motion designers work on the same project at once. | **partial**: `prod.launch`, `prod.merge`, WP-39 (Each film gets a git worktree and branch (prod.launch) and approved work merges in scope (prod.merge); people, roles and locks are WP-39.) |
| Approval and version tracking for deliverables. | Knowing which version the client signed off. | **command**: `prod.approve`, `ledger.add`, `post.approve` (The owner approves finals that passed QA; ledger.add records gate verdicts with before/after numbers; each post needs a named approval.) |
| Syncing settings and presets between machines. | An artist's setup follows them to a new workstation. | **command**: `engine.doctor` (The setup is in the repo: engine.doctor checks a machine and tools/setup.sh applies the fixes.) |
| Collect Files: packaging a project with all its footage for hand-off or archive. | Archiving a job or sending it to another studio. | **data**: films as text in git, `assets/manifest.json` (A film is text plus references to versioned cast, sets and manifest-tracked assets; renders are rebuilt rather than archived. There is no single zip export.) |

### Memory, storage and performance

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Persistent disk cache and RAM cache that keep rendered frames between sessions. | Reopening a project does not mean re-rendering everything. | **command**: `film.render` (Per-shot content hashes skip unchanged shots across sessions and identical frames are reused; build/ holds outputs.) |
| GPU acceleration for rendering and effects. | Real-time playback of heavy effects. | **n/a** (The container has no GPU (software WebGL and Vulkan); GPU-only work waits for a GPU, per WP-42.) |
| Proxies and low-resolution stand-ins for heavy footage. | Keeping interaction fast on big projects. | **partial**: `film.animatic`, WP-15 (The animatic and storyboard stand in for the full render; a static-layer cache and rendering only unique stepped frames are WP-15.) |
| Handling long, memory-heavy projects (purging, low-memory modes). | Feature-length or heavy VFX projects stay workable. | **gap**: WP-38 (A long-form (20-minute) test of memory, cache and assembly limits is WP-38.) |
| Diagnostics for GPU drivers and hardware readiness. | Ruling out the machine when something renders wrong. | **command**: `engine.doctor` (engine.doctor reports every missing package, binary, model or voice with the exact fix.) |

## Blender Grease Pencil (Grease Pencil v3 (Blender 4.3+); manual 'latest' read 2026-11-02 shows Blender 5.2 LTS; release notes 4.3, 4.4, 4.5)

Sources: [Blender Manual: Grease Pencil (table of contents)](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/grease_pencil/index.html); [Grease Pencil: Introduction](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/grease_pencil/introduction.html); [Grease Pencil: Structure](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/grease_pencil/structure.html); [Grease Pencil: Primitives](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/grease_pencil/primitives.html); [Grease Pencil: Properties (object, data, curve data, settings)](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/grease_pencil/properties/index.html); [Grease Pencil: Layers](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/grease_pencil/properties/layers.html); [Grease Pencil: Onion Skinning](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/grease_pencil/properties/onion_skinning.html); [Grease Pencil Modifiers: Introduction](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/grease_pencil/modifiers/introduction.html)

### Introduction and stroke structure

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Drawings are stored as strokes: ordered point lists placed in a 3D scene, captured from mouse or tablet. | Studios mix hand-drawn 2D characters, storyboards and motion graphics into 3D sets in one file. | **data**: `puppet.parts[].strokes[].d` (Puppet strokes are SVG path data on a 2D artboard; there is no 3D placement of strokes (2D layers in 3D space belong to WP-36/WP-42).) |
| Each point carries its own thickness, opacity, weight and texture rotation, so a line can vary along its length. | Animators get tapered, pressure-shaped ink without extra geometry and can edit width point by point later. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-15 (Rig Canvas ink bakes pressure into an outline shape (perfect-freehand) and puppet strokes carry one width/opacity per stroke; per-point attributes and line styles are WP-15.) |
| A stroke can be drawn as an outline line, a filled area, or both, from one material. | Line-and-fill character art: ink lines over flat colour, coloured independently. | **data**: `puppet.parts[].strokes[].fill`, `puppet.parts[].strokes[].stroke`, `puppet.parts[].strokes[].width` (Every stroke has a fill slot and an ink slot; the grease-pencil renderer splits such strokes into fill-only and line-only GP strokes.) |
| Per-stroke curve types (polyline, Bezier, Catmull-Rom, NURBS) with resolution, closing and cap shape settings. | Clean-up artists switch rough polylines to smooth curves and tune caps for a consistent ink look. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, WP-18 (SVG paths already hold Bezier curves and closed shapes (read by puppet.import); caps are always round and there is no curve-type conversion or curve editing tool (WP-18).) |
| A ready-made 2D animation starting file: white background, plain colour view, front drawing plane, line and fill layers, auto-keying on. | Teams start every shot from the same template so settings never drift between artists. | **command**: `film.create`, `film.from_template` (film.create makes a film folder with empty sources; film.from_template builds a storyboard from a format template.) |

### Primitives

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Add an empty drawing object, or one holding a single reference stroke, to start drawing at once. | Quick set-up of a new character or prop container before drawing. | **data**: `puppet.template`, puppet/templates (A new puppet starts from a template (humanoid, doodle) that defines its parts; cast.generate can also build a full original character.) |
| A built-in 2D test head with preset materials and layers. | Used as a standard test object for checking materials, modifiers and effects. | **command**: test (Doodleberry's equivalent is its benchmark films and golden frames (python -m dbe test) plus the example puppets.) |
| One-click line-art set-ups that create a drawing object tracing the whole scene, a collection or one object. | Fast toon outlines for 3D layouts and animatics. | **gap**: WP-42 (No 3D-to-ink line generation; Blender in-process for 3D sets and Grease Pencil 3 is WP-42.) |

### Object and data properties

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Per-object (and per-layer) switch for whether strokes react to scene lights. | Keep flat colour for cel style, or let lights shade drawings placed in a lit 3D set. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-14 (style.toon gives cel shadows and rims from a fixed light in the SVG renderer; the grease-pencil renderer turns GP lights off. Toon shading in other renderers is WP-14.) |
| Drawing data can be linked so several objects share one drawing. | Instancing the same character or prop many times; one fix updates them all. | **data**: `shots.shots[].cast[].puppet` (Cast entries reference one puppet file; any number of shots and cast members reuse it.) |
| Stroke depth order chosen between the 2D layer list and real 3D position. | 2D order for flat cut-out work; 3D order when drawings live inside a 3D scene. | **data**: `puppet.parts[].z`, `pose.z`, `puppet.controls (z.<part>)`, `clip swaps.z` (2D draw order only, but it can be animated by controls and clips; no 3D depth ordering.) |
| Custom attributes on layers and custom properties on the drawing data. | Pipelines tag layers and drawings with data their scripts read (status, pass names, variants). | **data**: `puppet format v0.9 attrs` (Parts carry free attributes (attrs: any JSON), kept by every tool and ignored by the renderers.) |
| Vertex groups: named, weighted sets of points used by deformers and filters. | Bind parts of a drawing to bones or limit an effect to an area. | **data**: `puppet strokes mesh.weights`, `puppet.parts[].parent` (Mesh strokes (v0.5) store per-vertex part weights; ordinary strokes follow their single part.) |
| Per-curve render settings: fill opacity, start and end caps, edge softness, texture scale and aspect. | Fine-tuning line ends and soft fills for a painterly look. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-15 (Stroke opacity exists; caps are fixed round and softness/texture scale are missing (medium simulation, WP-15).) |

### Layers and masks

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A layer tree with layer groups; each stroke belongs to one layer and new strokes go to the active one. | Separate line, colour, shadow and effects layers, grouped per character. | **data**: `puppet.parts`, `puppet.parts[].parent`, `shots.shots[].overlays`, `shots.shots[].underlays` (Puppet parts act as layers in a bone hierarchy; scene-level layers are the set, underlays, cast and overlays. No free layer groups.) |
| Per-layer visibility, locking, hide-others and auto-lock of inactive layers. | Protects finished work while drawing on one layer. | **n/a** (Viewport editing conveniences; agents edit JSON through commands. Locks on films and characters for people are WP-39.) |
| Any layer can mask others (above or below), with invert and several masks per layer. | Clip drawings inside shapes (eyes inside sockets, a character inside a window). | **gap**: WP-36 (Masks and mattes are WP-36 (node compositor). SVG clip paths are used internally (toon, mesh triangles) but not exposed as a mask field.) |
| Per-layer blend modes and opacity. | Multiply shadow layers, add glows, fade layers in and out. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-36 (Stroke opacity exists; blend modes and keyed layer opacity are WP-36.) |
| Per-layer location, rotation and scale. | Nudge or animate parts of a drawing without redrawing. | **data**: `shots.json timeline scales`, `shots.json timeline z` (Parts rotate about pivots, move with pose shifts and scale per part (v0.9), with layer order keys; every renderer draws them.) |
| Layer adjustments: tint all colours on a layer by a factor and override stroke thickness. | Push a character into night or silhouette quickly; unify line weight. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-16 (Palette slots allow runtime recolour; palette variants per scene are WP-16. A layer-wide line-weight override is missing (WP-15).) |
| A layer can be parented to an object or bone and inherit its motion. | The basis of cut-out rigs: each body part on its own layer riding a bone. | **data**: `puppet.parts[].parent`, `puppet.parts[].pivot` (This is the puppet skeleton itself.) |
| Layer pass index for restricting modifiers and effects to some layers. | Apply a noise or tint only to the effects layers. | **n/a** (Doodleberry targets parts by id; it has no per-layer modifier stack to filter (see gp-modifier-stack-3).) |
| Assign layers to render view layers to split a drawing into passes for compositing. | Render characters, lines and shadows separately and combine them in comp. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-37 (Characters are already rendered with alpha and composited over the set video; arbitrary passes (OpenEXR multi-pass) are WP-37.) |
| Duplicate a layer with empty keyframes, and merge layers down, by group or all. | Set up clean-up and colour layers that follow the rough animation's timing. | **gap**: WP-35 (Drawn levels, clean-up of rough lines and colour styles across frames are WP-35.) |

### Onion skinning

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Ghosts of earlier and later frames or keyframes around the current frame, with counts before and after. | Core of frame-by-frame animation: spacing and arcs are judged against neighbouring drawings. | **gap**: WP-35 (The Rig Canvas 'Onion' shows the rest of the character faintly in space, not neighbouring frames in time.) |
| Onion skin switched per layer, and per material whether it appears in ghosts. | Ghost only the layer being animated so the view stays readable. | **gap**: WP-35 (Part of onion skins in WP-35.) |
| Custom colours for past and future ghosts, overall opacity and fading with distance. | Tell direction of motion at a glance. | **gap**: WP-35 (Part of onion skins in WP-35.) |
| Ghost only keyframes picked by hand in the timeline. | Compare against chosen key poses, not every in-between. | **gap**: WP-35 (Part of onion skins in WP-35.) |
| Show the first frame as a ghost when on the last frame of a loop; limit ghosts to the active object. | Seamless cycles (walks, idles, smoke loops). | **gap**: WP-35 (Clip loops exist for rigged motion (clip loop flag), but there is no visual loop check for drawings.) |

### Modifier stack and influence filters

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Non-destructive stack of procedural operations on strokes, evaluated in order and editable at any time. | Artists keep drawings clean and add wobble, thickness and colour changes as reversible steps. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-36 (Shot style options (boil, toon, step, shadow) and post presets act non-destructively, but in a fixed order; a general ordered effects graph is WP-36 (with the graph view of WP-20).) |
| Apply a modifier to the current keyframe or to all keyframes, turning the result into real strokes. | Freeze procedural results so they can be hand-tweaked. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-35 (The bake (tools/bake.mjs) and the grease-pencil exporter write deformed per-frame strokes, but not back into editable drawings; editable drawn frames are WP-35.) |
| Influence filters: limit a modifier by layer or group, layer pass, material, material pass, vertex group, or a falloff curve along the stroke. | Wobble only the hair, thin only the outline, taper only stroke ends. | **gap**: WP-15 (Looks apply per shot (boil has per-layer seeds); per-part or per-palette-slot targeting of line styles fits WP-15.) |
| Custom modifiers built as Geometry Nodes groups. | Technical artists write studio-specific stroke effects. | **partial**: `proc.cook`, WP-42 (A Geometry Nodes set is built and rendered by Blender (proc.cook gn_set); user-written node groups are WP-42.) |

### Generate modifiers

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Array: repeat strokes with offsets, object-driven spacing and random variation. | Fences, crowds of simple shapes, repeated motion-graphic elements. | **partial**: `proc.cook`, WP-42 (Seeded scatter and instancing (crowds, Geometry Nodes blocks) exist; arrays of strokes with offsets are WP-42.) |
| Build: strokes appear or vanish over time as if drawn or erased, one by one or together, optionally at the recorded drawing speed, with fades. | Write-on titles, drawings that draw themselves, explainer reveals. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-15 (Kit 'draw' elements draw themselves with a pen-scratch sound; puppet strokes cannot be written on. Extending draw-on to character and prop lines fits WP-15 (kinetic and stroke effects).) |
| Dot Dash: break strokes into dash-and-gap patterns, each segment with its own material and thickness. | Dashed guide lines, stitches, motion trails, map routes. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-15 (Whisper bubbles are dashed and some generated cast details use fixed dashes; no dash field on puppet strokes (WP-15).) |
| Envelope: generate extra strokes around existing ones for a sketchy or hairy look. | Rough pencil looks and stylised fur from simple lines. | **gap**: WP-15 (Medium simulation for vector lines is WP-15.) |
| Length: extend or shorten strokes, following curvature, with randomness. | Overshooting construction lines, animated line growth. | **gap**: WP-15 (No stroke-length operations.) |
| Mirror: mirror strokes across axes. | Draw half of a symmetric object and get the other half. | **partial**: `puppet.coach`, WP-18 (Whole characters flip (place.flip) and mirrored clips exist; puppet.coach measures left/right mismatch. Symmetric drawing in the Rig Canvas is WP-18.) |
| Multiple Strokes: duplicate strokes with offsets and fading for a multi-pass sketch look. | Hand-sketched, re-traced line quality. | **gap**: WP-15 (Medium simulation for vector lines is WP-15.) |
| Outline: turn strokes into outlines around their shape with a chosen material, as seen from the camera. | Consistent ink outlines around fills, sticker-style borders. | **data**: `puppet.parts[].strokes[].stroke`, `puppet.parts[].strokes[].width`, `style.toon` (Every filled shape can carry an ink outline; toon shading uses outline offsets. Title kit elements have an outline option.) |
| Simplify: reduce point counts by fixed steps, adaptive tolerance, resampling or merging close points. | Lighter files and cleaner curves after rough drawing. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (The Rig Canvas simplifies strokes while drawing (Ramer-Douglas-Peucker) and imports flatten paths; no simplify operation on existing strokes.) |
| Subdivide: add points to smooth strokes. | Smoother deformation of strokes that bend with bones. | **n/a** (SVG paths are resolution-independent curves; mesh strokes are subdivided at import when needed.) |

### Deform modifiers

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Armature: bones deform stroke points by vertex groups or bone envelopes. | Cut-out and bendy character rigs inside Blender. | **data**: `puppet.parts[].parent`, `puppet.parts[].bend`, `puppet strokes mesh.weights` (Rigid parts on bones, bendy limbs and skinned mesh strokes; every renderer reads the baked result.) |
| Hook: points follow a controller object with falloff. | Pull a cheek or tail tip with a handle. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-11 (Pose shifts and driven controls move whole parts and swaps; point-level hooks and further constraints are WP-11.) |
| Lattice: deform strokes through a cage. | Squash, stretch and warp whole drawings smoothly. | **partial**: `shots.json timeline scales`, WP-18 (Whole-body squash, bendy limbs and per-part squash and stretch exist; free-form cage warps are WP-18 (Rig Canvas tools).) |
| Noise: seeded jitter of position, thickness, opacity and texture, changing every N frames or on keyframes. | Hand-made line boil on clean digital lines. | **data**: `style.boil` (style.boil {fps, amount, freq} gives deterministic, stepped displacement on characters, bubbles and signs; thickness and opacity noise are not included.) |
| Offset: shift, rotate and scale strokes per layer, material or stroke, with randomisation. | Break the uniformity of repeated drawings; animate parts offset from each other. | **partial**: `proc.cook`, WP-42 (Crowds vary each copy (puppet, facing, idle phase and speed, gaze, reaction timing) from a seed; per-stroke random offsets are WP-42.) |
| Shrinkwrap: project strokes onto 3D surfaces. | Draw markings that stick to a 3D model. | **n/a** (Doodleberry characters are 2D; 3D sets with Grease Pencil 3 are in WP-42 if this becomes useful.) |
| Smooth: smooth position, thickness, opacity or texture along strokes. | Remove jitter from tablet input or from deformations. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (Rig Canvas ink is smoothed while drawing; no smoothing of existing strokes.) |
| Thickness: set or scale line width, normalise it, or shape it along the stroke with a curve. | Consistent line weight across a production; tapered ends. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-15 (Each stroke has a width and ink import keeps line weight under squash; no global or along-stroke width control.) |

### Color modifiers

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Hue, saturation and value shift on strokes and fills. | Colour variants and mood shifts without repainting. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-16 (shots.json post grades shift the whole picture and set_opts recolour sets; per-character palette variants are WP-16.) |
| Opacity: change stroke and fill opacity, set hardness, or make it uniform. | Fade characters or effects; soft-edged lines. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-36 (Stroke opacity is static data; keyed opacity of parts or characters and soft edges are WP-36.) |
| Tint: blend a colour over strokes and fills, solid or as a gradient by distance to an object. | Atmospheric depth, a light source tinting nearby drawings. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-16 (Palette slots support recolouring; palette variants per scene are WP-16; distance-based gradient tint is not planned.) |

### Edit modifiers

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Texture Mapping: move, rotate and scale the texture along strokes or across fills. | Brush-texture lines and animated patterns. | **gap**: WP-15 (Textured line styles (pencil, marker, crayon) are WP-15.) |
| Time Offset: shift drawing keys in time, reverse them, show a fixed frame, ping-pong or chain modes, loop and speed. | Reuse cycles (walks, smoke, rain), desynchronise copies, pick mouth drawings by frame. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-35 (Rigged motion already has clip start, speed and loop, and swaps pick drawings by name; time remapping of drawn frames is WP-35.) |
| Vertex Weight Angle: weights from stroke direction. | Calligraphic thick-thin lines that depend on angle. | **gap**: WP-15 (A pen-nib look belongs with the vector line styles of WP-15.) |
| Vertex Weight Proximity: weights from distance to an object. | Fade or thin lines with depth or near a light. | **gap**: WP-36 (Depth-driven effects fit the multiplane depth of WP-36.) |
