| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Generate ink strokes from 3D meshes as seen by the camera, with occlusion. | Toon outlines on 3D sets and props so they match 2D characters. | **partial**: `proc.cook`, WP-42 (3D sets get ink lines from their meshes as the camera sees them, with occlusion (Freestyle in the gn_set render); writing them as editable strokes is WP-42.) |
| Choose edge types: contour and silhouette, creases by angle, intersections, material borders, marked edges, light contours and cast-shadow edges. | Control which lines read as drawn ink. | **gap**: WP-42 (Part of 3D line art in WP-42.) |
| Illumination filtering (lit or shaded regions) and filled shapes from enclosed regions. | Shadow shapes and two-tone fills generated from 3D lighting. | **gap**: WP-42 (Part of 3D line art in WP-42.) |
| Occlusion levels, line chaining and smoothing, and a shared cache across several line-art modifiers. | Hidden-line views and performance on dense scenes. | **gap**: WP-42 (Part of 3D line art in WP-42.) |
| Write results to a chosen layer and material with set radius and opacity, then bake to editable strokes. | Hand-touch generated lines for final shots. | **gap**: WP-42 (Part of 3D line art in WP-42.) |

### Visual effects

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Blur (also usable as depth-of-field style blur) over the whole object. | Soften background drawings, fake focus. | **gap**: WP-36 (Blurs and depth of field are WP-36.) |
| Colorize: greyscale, sepia, duotone, transparency or a custom colour. | Flashbacks, silhouettes and stylised looks. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-36 (Picture-wide post presets (noir, warm, cool) and ffmpeg grades exist; per-object colour effects are WP-36.) |
| Flip horizontally or vertically. | Mirror a character to face the other way. | **data**: `shots.shots[].cast[].place.flip` (Placement flip mirrors a whole character.) |
| Glow: bloom around colours above a threshold. | Magic, screens, lamps and neon. | **gap**: WP-36 (Glows are WP-36; note that WP-04's lint flags neon glow as a generic-AI look.) |
| Pixelate. | Retro looks, censoring, transitions. | **gap**: WP-36 (An image-space node in the compositor (WP-36).) |
| Rim: offset coloured rim around the drawing with a blend mode. | Back-light accents that separate characters from the background. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-14 (style.toon rim lighting works in the SVG renderer only; other renderers are WP-14.) |
| Shadow: offset drop shadow with blur and optional wave. | Lift characters off flat backgrounds. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-36 (Ground shadows (style.shadow) and cel shadows exist; offset drop shadows are WP-36.) |
| Swirl distortion. | Dizziness, hypnosis and transition gags. | **gap**: WP-36 (Distortion nodes belong to WP-36.) |
| Wave distortion. | Heat haze, underwater wobble, dream sequences. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-36 (style.boil is a turbulent displacement for line shimmer; directed wave distortion is WP-36.) |

### Materials

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Each material has independent stroke and fill parts that can be switched on or off. | Line-only, fill-only or line-and-fill shapes from one set of materials. | **data**: `puppet.parts[].strokes[].fill`, `puppet.parts[].strokes[].stroke` (Either can be 'none'.) |
| Shared material slots: changing a material updates every stroke that uses it. | Global colour changes and colour-model consistency across a production. | **data**: `puppet.palette` (Palette slots name every colour; puppet.lint flags raw colours; cast.generate sets skin, hair and outfit colours.) |
| Line styles of dots or squares with spacing by count, radius or density, alignment, rotation and random size, strength, rotation and colour. | Stippled, chalky or particle-like lines and dotted trails. | **gap**: WP-15 (Medium simulation for vector lines is WP-15.) |
| Image textures mapped along strokes and over fills, mixed with the base colour. | Paper, pencil grain and fabric patterns on drawings. | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, WP-15 (Raster parts and skinned mesh strokes carry painted texture (SVG renderer only, WP-14); textured vector lines and fills are WP-15. Paper grain exists as a picture-wide post pass.) |
| Gradient fills, linear or radial, with a second colour and an in-viewport tool to place the gradient. | Soft shading, skies and glowing objects. | **gap**: WP-15 (Puppet strokes are flat fills; puppet.import flattens SVG gradients. A gradient fill style would join WP-15.) |
| Holdout: a stroke or fill erases what lies beneath it. | Cut holes and reveal effects without masks. | **gap**: WP-36 (Masks and mattes are WP-36.) |
| Self-overlap control for semi-transparent strokes. | Even transparency where a translucent stroke crosses itself. | **n/a** (An SVG path with opacity never darkens where it crosses itself.) |
| Material visibility, locking, isolation and ghost visibility in the slot list. | Protect colour work while editing lines. | **n/a** (Viewport editing conveniences.) |

### Animation

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Animate the drawing object as a whole: position, rotation and size over time. | Slide-ons, camera-relative moves, simple motion graphics. | **data**: `shots.shots[].cast[].place`, `timeline.travel`, `shots.shots[].camera` (Placement, travel with foot pins and camera keys, folded into the bake for every renderer.) |
| Frame-by-frame drawing: keyframes that each hold a complete drawing. | Traditional hand-drawn animation and effects (splashes, smoke). | **gap**: WP-35 (Drawn levels with exposures are WP-35.) |
| Inherited animation from parents, hooks and armatures (cut-out). | Rig-driven acting with reusable motion. | **command**: `timeline.edit`, `acting.plan`, `acting.direct` (Rigged clips, poses, IK and controls, placed by hand, from prose or from the voice.) |
| Auto-key and additive drawing: a new key starts from the previous drawing. | Fast straight-ahead animation that changes only what moves. | **gap**: WP-35 (Part of drawn levels and the X-sheet in WP-35.) |
| Interpolate tool: drag to create a breakdown drawing between two keys at a chosen blend. | Quick in-betweens for simple shapes. | **gap**: WP-35 (In-between assistance for drawn levels is WP-35, with ML proposals in WP-43.) |
| Interpolate sequence: fill every frame between two keys, with step, easing type, layer scope, smoothing and stroke flip handling. | Generate spacing for tweened shape changes on twos or with easing. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-35 (Rig poses and clips already interpolate with easing and stepped timing; interpolating between two drawings is WP-35.) |
| Insert blank, duplicate or delete keyframes on the active or all layers, with a duration. | Block timing and holds in drawn animation. | **gap**: WP-35 (Exposure sheet (X-sheet) is WP-35.) |
| Bake object transform animation into drawing keyframes over a frame range with a step. | Turn moves into editable per-frame drawings or hand to another tool. | **command**: `shot.render` (The grease-pencil renderer bakes acting and camera into world-space strokes and writes one GP keyframe per frame.) |
| Animating on twos or threes by stepped keys and interpolation steps. | A hand-made cadence for cartoon timing. | **data**: `style.step` (style.step animates a shot on 12s, 8s or 6s while the film stays at 30 fps.) |
| Key types (keyframe, breakdown and others) shown and filtered separately. | Separate key poses from in-betweens during blocking and polish. | **gap**: WP-35 (Belongs with the X-sheet of WP-35.) |

| Fill tool: detects closed regions from geometry or pixels, closes gaps by radius or extension, supports fill guides, inverted fill and layer scope. | Fast flat colouring of line art, including slightly open lines. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (The Rig Canvas bucket closes gaps up to about 10 px, tucks the fill under the ink and refuses open outlines; fill guides, solver choice and layer scope are missing (WP-18).) |
| Erasers: soft, hard, point and whole-stroke, cutting strokes at intersections; box and lasso erase. | Clean up and shape lines while drawing. | **gap**: WP-18 (The Rig Canvas has undo and clear-slot only; an eraser is in WP-18.) |
| Trim tool: cut stroke ends back to where they cross other strokes. | Tidy overshooting lines after rough inking. | **gap**: WP-18 (Drawing tools in WP-18.) |
| Shape tools: line, polyline, arc, curve, box and circle. | Props, backgrounds and graphic elements with precise shapes. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (Agents can write any SVG path directly and kit elements draw shapes in code; the Rig Canvas has freehand and fill shapes only.) |
| Eyedropper to pick colours or materials from the screen. | Match colours from reference. | **n/a** (Colours are palette slots chosen by name.) |
| Drawing planes and stroke placement: draw at the origin, the cursor, on surfaces or on other strokes. | Draw in 3D space around models and sets. | **n/a** (Drawing is on a 2D artboard; 3D drawing would come with WP-42.) |
| Drawing options: auto-merge onto stroke ends, draw behind existing strokes, add bone weights to new strokes. | Paint colour under ink on one layer and keep new drawing bound to the rig. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (Fills are tucked under ink and every stroke drawn into a part follows that part's bone; auto-merge is missing.) |

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |

| Transform points: move, rotate, scale, bend, shear, push to a sphere, change radius. | Correct proportions and fix drawings without redrawing. | **partial**: `doodle.cut`, WP-18 (Pivots and cut polygons can be moved; general point editing is WP-18.) |
| Pen tool for Bezier strokes and handle and corner types. | Precise vector shapes for props and clean-up. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, WP-18 (Bezier paths come in through layered SVG (e.g. from Inkscape); no Bezier editing tool.) |
| Stroke operations: subdivide, smooth, simplify, join, split, close, set caps, flip direction, set start point. | Day-to-day clean-up of vector lines. | **gap**: WP-18 (Vector stroke editing belongs with the Rig Canvas upgrade (WP-18).) |

| Move strokes to another layer, assign a material, and arrange draw order. | Reorganise drawings and recolour them. | **data**: `puppet.parts[].z`, `puppet.parts[].strokes[].fill`, `puppet.parts[].strokes[].stroke` (Edited in the puppet JSON (written back through puppet.save).) |
| Clean up: remove loose points, duplicate frames and near-duplicate points, reproject strokes, remove fill guides. | Lighter, tidier files before final render. | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, WP-35 (Ink import removes specks and renders link duplicate frames by hash; clean-up of drawn frames is WP-35.) |
| Separate, duplicate, copy and paste strokes between layers and objects. | Reuse drawings and split one drawing into rig parts. | **partial**: `doodle.cut`, WP-18 (doodle.cut and puppet.from_ink cut one drawing into parts; puppet.export_layers/puppet.import round-trip parts; free copy and paste is WP-18.) |

### Sculpt mode

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Brushes to smooth lines and raise or lower their thickness and opacity locally. | Refine line quality after inking. | **gap**: WP-18 (No sculpt brushes on existing strokes.) |
| Brushes to grab, push, twist, pinch and inflate strokes. | Reshape drawings quickly between poses. | **gap**: WP-18 (No reshaping brushes.) |
| Randomize brush: add local jitter to points. | Hand-made imperfection in chosen areas. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-18 (style.boil jitters whole shots deterministically; brushed local jitter is missing.) |
| Clone brush: stamp copied strokes under the brush. | Scatter leaves, grass or repeated marks. | **gap**: WP-42 (Scatter and instancing are WP-42.) |
| Selection masks and auto-masking by stroke, layer or material. | Sculpt only the intended lines. | **gap**: WP-18 (Follows the sculpt brushes.) |
| Multiframe sculpting across keys. | Fix shapes over a whole drawn sequence. | **gap**: WP-35 (Needs drawn levels (WP-35).) |

### Vertex paint mode

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Paint colour onto points, mixed over the material colour, on strokes, fills or both. | Shading and colour variation without new materials. | **gap**: WP-15 (Colour is per stroke from palette slots; per-point colour belongs with medium simulation (WP-15).) |
| Tint tool in draw mode to recolour strokes with a brush. | Quick colour corrections on finished drawings. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (Palette slots can be recoloured in the Rig Canvas; brushed per-stroke recolour is missing.) |
| Blur, average, smear and replace colour brushes. | Blend painted colour smoothly. | **gap**: WP-15 (With per-point colour in WP-15.) |
| Colour adjustments on painted colours: levels, hue/saturation/value, brightness/contrast, invert, reset. | Grade a character's colours to match a scene. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-16 (Palette edits and picture-wide grades exist; palette variants per scene are WP-16.) |

### Weight paint mode

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Paint bone influence weights on points for cut-out deformation. | Smooth bends at joints in rigged drawings. | **partial**: `puppet.from_blend`, WP-18 (Skinned mesh strokes with per-vertex weights come from Blender cut-out rigs via puppet.from_blend; bendy limbs need no weights. No weight-painting tool (WP-18).) |
| Blur, average and smear weight brushes. | Even out deformation. | **gap**: WP-18 (Follows weight painting.) |
| Weight heat-map display while painting. | See which bone moves which line. | **gap**: WP-18 (puppet.coach annotates joint gaps under bending, not weights.) |
| New strokes and painting automatically add to the active vertex group. | Keep new drawings bound to the rig. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (Strokes drawn into a part follow that part's bone automatically; group weights are not painted.) |
| Weights drive other effects (thickness, opacity, influence filters). | Taper or fade lines by painted weight. | **gap**: WP-15 (Per-point attributes and line styles are WP-15.) |

### Object mode: trace and convert

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Trace a black-and-white image into strokes with a threshold, stroke radius and turn policy. | Bring paper drawings and logos in as editable lines. | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, `doodle.snap`, WP-18 (Scans and photos become raster-part puppets in the artist's own line and doodles get cut and rigged; vector tracing (VTracer, potrace) is WP-18.) |
| Trace an image sequence into one drawing per frame. | Convert paper animation tests into drawn keys. | **gap**: WP-35 (Drawn levels from scanned sequences are WP-35.) |
| Convert meshes and curves into drawing strokes. | Use 3D or curve shapes as a starting point for 2D drawings. | **gap**: WP-42 (3D and Blender in-process work is WP-42.) |

### Rigging with armatures

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Bind drawings to an armature so bones deform stroke points (vertex groups or envelopes). | Cut-out characters animated by posing bones. | **data**: `puppet.parts[].parent`, `puppet.parts[].pivot`, `puppet strokes mesh.weights` (puppet.from_blend also imports Blender cut-out rigs (bones, sprite and shape-key variants) into puppets.) |
| Automatic weights from bone envelopes, without painting. | Fast rigging of simple shapes. | **data**: `puppet.parts[].bend` (Bendy limbs curve smoothly out of their parent with no weights; puppet.import estimates pivots from the drawing.) |
| Blender's IK, constraints and drivers applied to bones that move drawings. | Planted feet, reaching hands, controls that drive several parts. | **data**: `timeline.ik`, `timeline.pins`, `puppet.chains`, `puppet.controls`, `puppet.parts[].limits` (Two-bone IK, FABRIK chains, joint limits, look-at and driven controls; IK/FK blending, copy rotation and follow path are WP-11.) |
| Reusable actions and poses applied to rigs. | Library of cycles and gestures shared across shots. | **command**: `timeline.edit`, `acting.direct`, `actout.clip` (Template-level clip library and per-character clips; actout.clip turns a filmed take into a clip.) |
| Objects parented to bones and hooks so props follow hands. | Props passed between characters in cut-out scenes. | **data**: `shots.shots[].props[].attach` (Props attach to a character's part and switch parents with arcs (SVG player only; other renderers refuse props, WP-14).) |

### Dope Sheet: Grease Pencil mode

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Each layer is a channel of drawing keys that can be moved, scaled and retimed. | Block a shot's timing and adjust holds. | **partial**: `timeline.get`, `timeline.edit`, WP-35 (The Studio timeline moves and resizes clip layers per character, line-relative; channels of drawn keys are WP-35.) |
| Copy and paste keys between layers and objects, including paste by layer. | Reuse timing and drawings across characters. | **gap**: WP-35 (With the X-sheet in WP-35.) |
| Breakdown keys shown distinctly, with an operator to delete them. | Strip generated in-betweens and re-time. | **gap**: WP-35 (With in-between assistance in WP-35.) |
| Layer opacity, mask, onion, visibility and lock toggles, solo and isolate in the channel list. | Manage layers while timing. | **n/a** (Editor conveniences.) |

### Rendering

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Strokes rendered in EEVEE with SMAA thresholds and SSAA sample count for anti-aliasing. | Clean, smooth lines in final renders. | **command**: `shot.render` (renderer 'grease-pencil' renders with EEVEE at half resolution and upscales; DBE_GP_SAMPLES sets the samples.) |
| Motion blur steps for strokes. | Natural blur on fast moves. | **gap**: WP-15 (Sub-frame motion blur on fast shots is WP-15.) |
| A dedicated Grease Pencil render pass and per-view-layer inclusion. | Comp drawings separately from 3D. | **command**: `film.master` (Characters render with alpha over the set, and film.master writes beauty, line, fill and matte passes.) |
| Transparent background rendering for compositing. | Lay drawn characters over painted or live backgrounds. | **command**: `shot.render` (The grease-pencil path renders RGBA frames composited over the SVG set by ffmpeg.) |
| Output resolution, frame rate and colour view transform. | Deliver to spec. | **metric**: `qa.run` (qa.run checks 1080x1920 and 30 fps; the renderer uses the Standard view transform. Colour management (OCIO, ACES) is WP-37.) |
| Save the drawing as a native, editable project for further work in the tool. | Hand a shot to a Blender artist for hand-finishing. | **gap**: WP-14 (shot.render writes build/shots/<id>.gp.json and frames, not a .blend; the feature map lists Grease Pencil project export as done, which WP-01 should correct. Saving the .blend fits WP-14 (renderer parity).) |

### Import and export

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Import SVG as strokes with resolution, scale and centring options. | Bring vector art from Inkscape or Illustrator into animation. | **command**: `puppet.import` (Layered SVG becomes a puppet, with parts, swaps and pivots taken from layer names.) |
| Export SVG for one frame, selected keys or the whole range as animation, with fill, uniform width and camera clipping options. | Vector stills and animations for the web and print. | **partial**: `puppet.export_layers`, `export.lottie`, `export.web`, WP-23 (Layered SVG of a puppet in rest pose, Lottie and a live web player exist; animated SVG and other formats are WP-23.) |
| Export PDF with one page per frame. | Flipbooks, print checks and review packets. | **partial**: `film.storyboard`, WP-30 (film.storyboard writes a contact PDF (six shots a page); per-frame print books fit WP-30 (comics and storybooks from shot data).) |

### Geometry Nodes for Grease Pencil

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Read and write layers, strokes and attributes of drawings in node trees. | Procedural generation and editing of 2D drawings. | **gap**: WP-42 (Procedural 2D as data and Blender in-process are WP-42.) |
| Nodes to set stroke colour, depth and softness, merge layers, and convert between drawings and curves. | Build custom procedural looks and pipelines. | **gap**: WP-42 (Part of WP-42.) |
| Node Tools: node groups exposed as custom operators in drawing and edit modes. | Studio-specific one-click tools for artists. | **partial**: `engine.commands`, WP-42 (Doodleberry's own tools are @command functions listed by engine.commands (CLI, HTTP, Studio, MCP); procedural tools on strokes are WP-42.) |

## Toon Boom Harmony (25 (25.2, March 2026), Premium)

Sources: [Harmony 25 Premium documentation](https://docs.toonboom.com/help/harmony-25/premium/book/index.html); [Harmony 25 Premium User Guide](https://docs.toonboom.com/help/harmony-25/premium/book/about-user-guide.html); [Harmony 25 Release Notes](https://docs.toonboom.com/help/harmony-25/premium/release-notes/harmony/harmony-25-release-notes.html); [Harmony 25.1 Release Notes](https://docs.toonboom.com/help/harmony-25/premium/release-notes/harmony/harmony-25-1-release-notes.html); [Harmony 25.2 Release Notes](https://docs.toonboom.com/help/harmony-25/premium/release-notes/harmony/harmony-25-2-release-notes.html); [AI Operations](https://docs.toonboom.com/help/harmony-25/premium/ai/ai-operations.html); [Harmony Server](https://docs.toonboom.com/help/harmony-25/premium/project-creation/about-database.html)

### Scene Creation

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Scene settings that fix the frame size, aspect ratio and frame rate of a scene | Set once per production to match the delivery spec so every scene is consistent | **data**: `shots.json size`, `shots.json fps` (Film-level size and fps in shots.json; qa.run checks 1080x1920 at 30 fps) |
| Start a new scene from an empty setup or a studio template | Each episode or shot starts from a template carrying the camera, palettes and rigs | **command**: `film.create`, `film.from_template` (Empty film folder, or a storyboard drafted from a format template) |
| Compact single-file scene storage that cuts the number of files on disk (new in 25) | Lighter scenes for remote artists and faster transfers between studios | **data**: films/<name>/ source files (A film is already a handful of small JSON/text files; generated files stay in build/ (git-ignored)) |
| Stand-alone scenes saved with version history and restorable earlier saves | Artists roll back to a previous save after a bad change | **data**: films/<name>/ in git (Films are text under version control; history and branches come from git) |
| Central server database where many artists work on the same project at once | Large studios keep every asset in one place and assign scenes to artists | **partial**: `prod.launch`, `prod.merge`, `prod.status`, WP-39 (One git worktree and branch per film with a scoped merge; people, roles and assignments are WP-39) |
| Scene and drawing locks so only one person edits an element at a time | Prevents two animators overwriting the same drawing in a shared database | **gap**: WP-39 (Assignments and locks on films and characters are in WP-39's scope) |
| Organising projects into environments, jobs and scenes | Production management sorts shows, episodes and shots in a hierarchy | **partial**: `plan.channels`, `prod.status`, WP-39 (Channels, quarters and the production board organise films; per-level permissions wait for WP-39) |
| Checking a scene's integrity for missing or broken references | Run before handing off or rendering to catch missing files | **command**: `film.validate` (Schema and reference check (lines, puppets, clips, shot order)) |

### User Interface

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Configurable workspaces made of dockable views and toolbars | Each department (layout, animation, compositing) keeps its own screen layout | **n/a** (Agents work through the command registry, not a window layout; Studio pages are fixed views over the same data) |
| Keyboard shortcuts for every action | Speed for artists doing repetitive keyframing and drawing | **n/a** (Agent-run studio: actions are commands with JSON arguments) |
| Interface theme colours and interface language | Comfort and localisation for international crews | **n/a** (No per-user desktop UI to theme; Studio is a small web front end) |
| Preferences holding studio-wide defaults for tools, display and rendering | Studios ship a standard preference set so all seats behave alike | **data**: `dbe/profiles.json` (Per-style QA thresholds and per-platform encoding profiles live in one checked-in file) |
| Several synchronised views of one scene (camera, drawing, timeline, xsheet, node graph) | Animators see picture, timing and structure side by side | **partial**: `film.preview`, `timeline.get`, WP-20 (Live player and Studio timeline exist; the node/graph view is WP-20 and the xsheet WP-35) |
| Built-in, searchable help for tools and commands | Artists look up a tool without leaving the application | **command**: `engine.commands` (Every command with its input schema, which is what agents see as tools) |

### Layers and Columns

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Typed layers for drawings, pegs, groups, sound, camera, effects and annotations | The scene is assembled from many layer types stacked in the timeline | **data**: `shots.json shots[].cast`, `shots.json overlays`, `shots.json cues`, `shots.json camera` (A shot's cast, overlays, underlays, cues and camera play the role of layer types) |
| Adding, deleting, renaming and reordering layers | Everyday scene assembly | **command**: `film.write`, `timeline.edit` (Layers are entries in shots.json written by film.write; timeline.edit moves, resizes, adds or deletes clip layers) |
| Enabling, disabling, soloing and locking layers while working | Isolate one element to check it, protect finished layers | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-39 (One shot renders on its own; per-layer solo is unnecessary for agents, but locks are WP-39) |
| Clone layers that share drawings versus independent duplicates | Reuse one character's artwork in several places without copying files | **data**: `shots.json shots[].cast[].puppet` (Cast entries reference a puppet file by path, so any number of shots share one character) |
| Group layers to collapse and move related elements together | Keeps a character's many parts manageable in the timeline | **data**: puppet skeleton (parent) (A cast member is one entry whose parts are grouped by the puppet's skeleton) |
| Annotation columns for notes drawn or typed on the timeline | Directors and leads leave notes for animators on specific frames | **command**: `review.add`, `review.list` (Notes pinned to frames in review.json) |
| Timing columns that hold exposure only and point at drawings elsewhere | Separates timing from artwork so a timing pass can be redone cheaply | **gap**: WP-35 (Drawn levels with exposures and an X-sheet are WP-35) |
| Element manager listing the drawing elements a scene uses | Clean up unused elements before archiving | **partial**: `film.provenance`, `film.credits`, WP-29 (What a film uses is listed with licences; screen time per asset (usage accounting) is WP-29) |

### Timing

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Setting scene length and inserting or removing frames | Fits a scene to its slot in the edit | **command**: `film.retime` (Shot start and end derive from line references, so length follows the voice) |
| Exposure editing: holding, extending and filling drawing exposures | Core timing work in the xsheet for hand-drawn and cut-out scenes | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-35 (Clip timing is editable; per-drawing exposures need drawn levels and an X-sheet (WP-35)) |
| Animating on ones, twos or threes | Classic 2D look and cheaper animation on held frames | **data**: `shots.json style.step` (Stepped timing per shot or character (e.g. 12 fps while the film runs at 30)) |
| Frame markers and scene markers that label points or ranges in time | Mark key poses, act breaks or sync points for the team | **data**: `script.json line ids (line-relative times)` (Times are written relative to named lines ("mid.start+0.9"), which act as markers that survive re-voicing) |
| Locking the timeline ruler so scene markers and length cannot shift by accident (new in 25) | Protects locked edit timing during animation | **command**: `script.slots` (Narration slots fix each line's start and maximum length before any voice exists) |
| Tempo markers that lay a beat grid on the timeline | Animate dance, music and comic beats on tempo | **command**: `film.beats` (Beats and bars from the score, with how far each cut and line falls from them) |
| Xsheet view with a dialogue column for phonetic breakdown | Animators read sound breakdown next to exposures | **partial**: `timeline.get`, WP-35 (Dialogue lanes with words exist in the Studio timeline; a true exposure sheet is WP-35) |
| Printing the exposure sheet | Paper xsheets for animators and checkers | **gap**: WP-35 (An X-sheet (and a printable export of it) belongs with WP-35) |
| Ripple versus overwrite behaviour when timing changes | Keeps downstream timing in place or pushes it along deliberately | **partial**: `film.retime`, `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (Line-relative times ripple with the voice; dragging lines and shots is WP-19) |

### Drawing

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Vector brush and pencil (centre-line) tools with pen pressure | Primary drawing tools for design, animation and cleanup | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (Rig Canvas pressure ink and fills saved with puppet.save; centre-line pencil lines and more tools are WP-18) |
| Shape, line, polyline and text tools | Fast construction of props, backgrounds and signage | **data**: puppet strokes d (SVG path), `shots.json overlays (sign, text)` (Shapes are SVG path data; text and signs are overlays drawn in the film's ink) |
| Stroke editing: contour, centre-line and smoothing editors, cutter | Fix and refine lines after drawing | **gap**: WP-18 (Polygon and stroke editing in the Rig Canvas is WP-18) |
| Stroke stabiliser and adjustable pressure curve | Smooth confident lines on any tablet | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (Pressure strokes and simplification exist; stabiliser is WP-18) |
| Drawing guides: perspective grids with vanishing points | Draw backgrounds and props in consistent perspective | **gap**: WP-18 (No perspective guides; fits the Rig Canvas tools of WP-18) |
| Optimising drawings: flatten, remove extra strokes, deduplicate, shrink textures | Keep scenes light and fast before rendering or archiving | **partial**: `puppet.import`, `puppet.lint`, WP-18 (Imports simplify outlines (RDP) and lint flags problems; a dedicated optimiser is WP-18) |
| Converting between brush strokes and pencil lines | Switch a drawing to the representation that suits cleanup or ink | **gap**: WP-34 (Vector editing via Synfig core or Inkscape is a WP-34 candidate) |
| Bitmap layers and vectorising bitmap artwork | Mix painted textures with vectors; turn scans into editable vectors | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, `doodle.snap`, WP-18 (Raster parts from photos or scans exist; vector tracing (VTracer, potrace) is WP-18) |
| Pencil Retouch: adjust thickness or opacity of existing pencil lines like natural media (new in 25) | Gives clean vector lines a hand-drawn variation during ink and paint | **gap**: WP-15 (Medium simulation for vector lines (pencil, marker, crayon) is WP-15) |
| Importing Photoshop brushes for the brush and eraser tools (new in 25) | Artists keep the brushes they already use | **gap**: WP-34 (Brush engines (Krita, libmypaint) are WP-34 candidates) |
| Envelope and perspective transforms of selected artwork | Warp a drawing to fit a surface or angle | **gap**: WP-18 (Not available on drawings; fits WP-18 editing tools) |

### Painting & Colours

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Colour swatches referenced by drawings, so editing a swatch repaints every drawing | Change a character's colours globally late in production | **data**: puppet strokes fill/stroke palette slots (Strokes name palette slots ("cloth1", "ink"); puppet.lint flags raw colours) |
| Palette management: scene and element palettes, linking, cloning, palette lists | Night, day or wet versions of a character from cloned palettes | **partial**: `shots.json set_opts`, WP-16 (Runtime recolour of sets and slots exists; palette variants per scene are WP-16) |
| Gradient and texture swatches | Soft shading and painted textures inside fills | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, WP-15 (Raster parts carry painted texture; vector gradients are flattened on import; texture passes are WP-15) |
| Paint bucket with gap closing for unclosed lines | Fast ink-and-paint of every frame | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-35 (Rig Canvas bucket closes gaps up to about 10 px; batch painting of drawn sequences belongs with WP-35) |
| Colour models as the reference for ink and paint | Painters match each drawing to an approved colour model | **command**: `puppet.sheet` (Model sheet with every view and expression in its palette) |
| Dirt cleanup and verifying unpainted zones | Scanned or rough drawings are cleaned before painting | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, `puppet.lint`, WP-35 (Speck removal on ink import; cleanup of rough lines for drawn animation is WP-35) |
| Recolouring drawings in bulk and protecting chosen colours | Swap a colour across a whole scene safely | **data**: puppet strokes fill/stroke palette slots, `shots.json set_opts` (Slots recolour in one place; no protect flag is needed because nothing paints raw colours) |
| Optimising palettes: merge duplicates and remove unused colours (new in 25) | Clean palettes before delivery | **partial**: `puppet.lint`, WP-16 (Lint reports raw colours and missing slots; merging unused slots is not built (WP-16 palette work)) |
| Colour space management for elements, scene, display and export | Consistent colour from painting through grading to delivery | **gap**: WP-37 (OpenColorIO and ACES are WP-37) |
| Separate line art and colour art layers in each drawing | Ink and paint stay independent for easier fixes | **data**: puppet strokes (fill strokes, outline drawn last) (Fill and ink are separate strokes per part; outlines are drawn last) |

### Paperless Animation

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Rough, tie-down and clean-up passes drawn frame by frame | Traditional hand-drawn character animation done digitally | **gap**: WP-35 (Drawn levels and cleanup of rough lines are WP-35) |
| Onion skin and light table showing neighbouring drawings | Spacing and arcs between drawings | **gap**: WP-35 (Onion skins are in WP-35 (WP-18 for Rig Canvas boards)) |
| Shift-and-trace to offset and trace over previous drawings | Inbetweening with control over overlap | **gap**: WP-35 (Part of hand-drawn animation tools) |
| Flipping and playing drawings in place, mini playback | Check motion while drawing | **command**: `film.preview` (Live player with scrubbing; drawing-level flipping waits for drawn levels (WP-35)) |
| Marking drawings as key, breakdown or inbetween | Lead animators hand keys to assistants | **gap**: WP-35 (Key poses with in-between assistance are WP-35/WP-43) |
| Art Layer Manager to move, swap or consolidate art layers across a frame range (new in 25.2) | Bulk fixes to layered artwork across many drawings | **gap**: WP-35 (Needs drawn levels first) |
| Drawing thumbnails in the timeline | Find a drawing quickly | **partial**: `film.storyboard`, `timeline.get`, WP-35 (Shot stills exist; per-drawing thumbnails need drawn levels) |

### Scene Staging

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Positioning, scaling and rotating layers in the scene | Layout artists block characters and props | **data**: `shots.json shots[].cast[].place` (Keys [t, {x, y, s}] per cast member; re-staged per aspect) |
| Multiplane: elements at different depths for parallax | Depth in pans and trucks without 3D | **gap**: WP-36 (Some sets have built-in parallax (forest); true multiplane is WP-36) |
| 3D space staging: layers rotated and moved in z with a perspective camera | 2.5D scenes such as fly-throughs | **gap**: WP-36 (WP-36 multiplane depth; tools/three is a test harness only) |
| Alignment guides and snapping | Precise manual placement | **n/a** (Placement is numeric in data; safe-area and framing are measured by qa.run and film.lint instead) |
| Z-depth ordering and nudging between layers | Control what is in front | **data**: puppet parts z, puppet controls z, `timeline swaps.z`, `shots.json cast order` (Part order inside a character; cast order between characters) |
| Layer opacity and transparency | Fades, ghosts and see-through elements | **gap**: WP-36 (No per-layer opacity in shots.json; belongs with the compositor) |
| Flipping a layer horizontally | Reuse a character facing the other way | **data**: `shots.json shots[].cast[].place flip` (Whole-character flip (1 or -1)) |

### Digital Animation

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Pegs: transform-only parents that move whatever is attached | Move groups, set up hierarchies and camera-like moves | **partial**: `shots.json shots[].cast[].place`, WP-36 (Each cast member has its own transform; a free peg shared by several elements needs layers on planes (WP-36)) |
| Keyframes on position, scale, rotation and other parameters | Basic motion for props, characters and camera | **data**: `shots.json shots[].cast[].place`, `shots.json camera`, puppet clips (Keys in place, camera and clips) |
| Function curves with Bezier and ease handles in a graph editor | Polish timing and spacing | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (Easing exists in keys and clips; a curve editor is WP-19) |
| Motion paths with tension, bias and continuity | Arcs and trajectories for moving objects | **partial**: `shots.json place.path`, WP-11 (Characters walk along Catmull-Rom paths (place.path) with ease in and out; props draw motion arcs (fx.arcs). Tension, bias and continuity handles are WP-11.) |
| Copy, paste-special, reverse and cycle motion | Reuse animation across scenes and loop walks | **data**: `puppet/clips/<template>.json (loop)`, puppet clips (Clips are template-level, reusable on any character, with looping clips; reverse paste is not offered) |
| Velocity and ease control on keyframes | Slow-in, slow-out | **data**: puppet clips, `shots.json camera_auto ease` (Easing in clips and camera; anticipation and overshoot filter) |
| Stepped keyframes and step-on functions | Pose-to-pose blocking | **data**: `shots.json style.step` (Stepped timing per shot or character) |
| Expression columns computing values from other values | Link rotations or drive repeated motion procedurally | **partial**: `puppet format v0.9 copy_rotation`, WP-20 (Piecewise-linear driven keys and copy-rotation constraints (v0.9); scene.js frame functions can compute anything. Expression columns are WP-20 (graph view).) |

### Morphing Animation

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Automatic shape morph between a source and destination vector drawing | Effects, smoke and simple shape changes without drawing inbetweens | **gap**: WP-43 (In-between proposals and morphs between views are WP-43) |
| Morph hints controlling which points correspond | Fix twisting or collapsing morphs | **gap**: WP-43 (Comes with the morph work) |
| Morph velocity and timing control | Ease the morph to the action | **gap**: WP-43 (Comes with the morph work) |
| Converting a morph into editable inbetween drawings | Touch up generated inbetweens by hand | **gap**: WP-35 (Needs drawn levels (WP-35)) |

### Importing & Scanning

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Importing bitmap images as layers | Backgrounds, references and textures | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, WP-16 (Images become puppet raster parts; sets are drawn in code, image sets and prop packs are WP-16) |
| Multi-layer PSD import keeping layers separate | Bring Photoshop character designs into a rig | **command**: `puppet.import` (Layered SVG, PSD and ORA by layer name, with pivots estimated) |
| PSD layout import with layer positions | Background painters deliver layouts in Photoshop | **partial**: `puppet.import`, WP-16 (Characters only; layered set import is not built) |
| Vector import (AI, PDF, SVG, SWF/FLA) | Reuse vector assets from other tools | **partial**: `puppet.import`, WP-18 (SVG done; AI and others in WP-18) |
| Importing movie files as reference layers | Rotoscope or match to live-action reference | **partial**: `actout.clip`, WP-35 (Video takes become action clips; video as a tracing layer needs drawn levels) |
| Scanning paper drawings with registration and vectorisation | Paper animators feed drawings into the digital pipeline | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, `doodle.snap`, WP-18 (Photos and scans become raster parts; vectorisation is WP-18) |

### Node View

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Node graph wiring drawings, pegs, effects, composites and outputs | Technical directors build and debug the render pipeline of a scene | **gap**: WP-36 (Per-shot node compositor is WP-36, with WP-20's graph view as its editor) |
| Composite nodes with blending and pass-through modes | Control how layers merge before effects | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-36 (Renderer layers are composited over the set with ffmpeg; blend modes are WP-36) |
| Groups, backdrops and waypoints to organise large graphs | Keep complex rigs and comps readable | **gap**: WP-20 (React Flow node view is WP-20) |
| Display nodes to preview any branch | Inspect one part of the comp | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-20 (A shot renders alone, or in another renderer for comparison; per-node preview is WP-20) |
| Node and rig caching to speed up heavy scenes | Interactive playback of complex rigs | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-15 (Content-hash per shot and frame dedupe; static-layer cache is WP-15) |
| Port filtering and multi-layer write outputs | Split one comp into many outputs | **partial**: `film.master`, WP-37 (Multi-layer EXR output (beauty, line, fill, matte); port filtering is WP-37.) |

### Camera

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Several cameras in a scene with a selectable active camera | Alternate angles and cuts within one setup | **data**: `shots.json shots[].camera` (One camera per shot; cuts change shots) |
| Animating camera framing: pan, truck, zoom | Camera moves on layout | **data**: `shots.json shots[].camera` (Keys [t, [cx, cy, zoom]] folded into the bake for every renderer) |
| Camera shake | Impacts and comedy accents | **data**: `shots.json camera_auto (handheld, punch)` (Camera grammar includes shake and punch-ins) |
| Perspective or orthographic camera type and clipping planes | Control depth in 3D-space scenes | **gap**: WP-36 (True 2D multiplane with depth) |
| Locking drawings to face the camera angle | Flat elements stay readable when the camera moves in 3D | **gap**: WP-36 (Needs 3D-space staging) |
| Camera rotation about the view axis | Dutch angles and tilted framing | **gap**: WP-36 (Camera keys have no roll; add to WP-36's camera) |

### Character Rigging

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Breaking a character design into parts at the articulations | First step of every cut-out rig | **command**: `puppet.import`, `doodle.cut`, `puppet.coach` (Parts by layer name or confirmed cuts; the coach checks the drawing first) |
| Naming conventions that keep rigs consistent | Scripts and templates rely on predictable layer names | **data**: puppet template part ids (Template part ids (head, upperarm_R) are the convention; puppet.lint checks them) |
| Setting pivot points per part | Correct rotation of every joint | **command**: `puppet.import` (Pivots from markers or estimated from the drawing; stored as part pivots) |
| Peg and drawing hierarchies (parent-child structure) | Move a forearm with the upper arm | **data**: puppet skeleton (parent) (Bone hierarchy with FK) |
| Drawing substitutions inside the rig (hands, mouths, eyes) | Swap drawings for expressions and poses | **data**: puppet swaps (Swap sets per slot) |
| Fine-tuning joints: invert cutting and overlaps that hide seams | Clean joints at every angle | **command**: `puppet.coach`, `doodle.cut` (Coach bends each joint and measures the hole; ink cuts keep overlap seams) |
| A master peg that moves the whole character | Place and travel the character in layout | **data**: `shots.json shots[].cast[].place` (Per cast member transform) |
| Model reference and size relation between characters | Characters keep correct relative sizes | **partial**: `puppet.sheet`, WP-18 (Model sheets exist; a turnaround with height lines is WP-18) |

### Cut-out Animation

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Posing a hierarchy with a transform tool | Main cut-out animation workflow | **data**: puppet poses, acting timeline angles (Agents pose through data; people pose in the Rig Canvas and save with puppet.save) |
| Keyframes and inbetweens with eases | Pose-to-pose cut-out animation | **data**: puppet clips, `shots.json shots[].cast[].timeline.clips` (Clips stack with fades and additive layers) |
| Breakdown Assistant: blend between neighbouring key poses to make breakdowns (new in 25) | Faster breakdowns with a slider rather than hand-posing | **partial**: `level.inbetween`, WP-43 (Clip blending gives weighted mixes and drawn levels get in-between proposals (level.inbetween); breakdowns for cut-out puppets are WP-43.) |
| Swapping drawings over time | Hand poses and mouth changes in a cut | **data**: acting timeline swaps, puppet swaps (drawing.requests lists swaps a character lacks) |
| Onion skin for cut-out poses | Compare poses across frames | **gap**: WP-18 (Onion skin in the Rig Canvas boards is WP-18) |
| Flipping parts and mirroring poses | Reuse a pose for the opposite side | **partial**: `shots.json shots[].cast[].place flip`, WP-11 (Whole-character flip and mirrored clip pairs exist; per-part flip is not built) |
| Lip-sync from a sound track mapped onto a mouth chart | Dialogue animation in minutes | **command**: `voice.build`, `lipsync.check`, `doodle.say` (Visemes from phoneme timings, cross-checked against the voice) |
| Changing z-depth of parts over time | Arm in front of or behind the body | **data**: `acting timeline swaps.z`, puppet controls z (Clips carry z; controls drive z) |
| Creating cycles (walks, idles) | Loops for walks and background action | **data**: puppet clips (loop) (Looping clips in the clip library) |
| Inverse kinematics with nails that pin parts | Plant feet, reach for objects | **data**: acting timeline ik, acting timeline pins (Two-bone IK, FABRIK chains, foot pins, joint limits; IK/FK blending is WP-11) |
| Pose copier and pose libraries | Reuse approved poses | **data**: puppet poses, `puppet/clips/<template>.json presets` (Per-character poses plus shared presets) |

### Deformations

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Bone deformers that bend artwork smoothly along a chain | Rubber-hose limbs, smooth elbows and knees | **data**: puppet parts bend, puppet strokes mesh (Bendy limbs and skinned mesh strokes (SVG renderer; others WP-14)) |
| Curve deformers for hair, tails and flexible parts | Organic shapes that curve rather than hinge | **data**: puppet chains (FABRIK chains with a living wave, puppet v0.6) |
| Envelope deformers that reshape outlines | Squash a head or bulge a belly | **partial**: `shots.json timeline scales`, WP-18 (Per-part squash and stretch and bendy limbs reshape parts; contour envelope deformers are WP-18.) |
| Free-form deformers with an editable point lattice | Arbitrary warps of a drawing | **gap**: WP-11 (Mesh strokes follow parts, not free animated points) |
| Weighted and shape-aware deformation with regions of influence | Deform drawings without tearing lines | **data**: puppet strokes mesh (skin weights) (Linear blend skinning with weights; pinches past about 90 degrees) |
| Kinematic outputs that attach rigid parts to deformed points | Keep a hand drawing attached to a bent arm | **data**: puppet anchors (Named anchors and look-at targets on other parts) |
| Dynamic deformations for follow-through | Secondary motion without hand keys | **data**: puppet springs (Deterministic springs re-simulated per frame) |
| Converting a deformed result into a drawing | Touch up a single frame by hand | **gap**: WP-35 (Needs drawn levels) |
| Multi-pose rigs with a rest position per drawing or view | One deformer set that works for front and side drawings | **data**: puppet views (Views override parts and pivots per angle) |

### Master Controller

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|

| Grid controllers for two-axis poses (head turn left/right and up/down) | Fast head turns on complex rigs | **partial**: puppet controls, WP-11 (The turn control is one axis; a two-axis grid is not built) |
| Stack controllers that toggle groups of layers | Switch costumes or accessories | **data**: puppet controls swap targets (Controls can drive swaps; outfits as swap sets are WP-16) |
| Deformer-on-deformer setups | Layered deformation for squash on top of bones | **gap**: WP-11 (Not supported) |
| Scripted custom on-screen widgets | TDs build bespoke controls | **n/a** (Agents set control values in data; no on-canvas widgets needed) |

| Glows, highlights, tones and drop shadows | Lighting and finish on 2D characters | **partial**: `shots.json style.toon`, WP-36 (Cel shadow bands and rim light in SVG; glows WP-36) |
| Masking and cutting with mattes | Reveal, hide or clip elements | **gap**: WP-36 (Masks and mattes are WP-36) |
| Colour override, fade, curves and levels | Grade or recolour parts of a scene | **partial**: `shots.json post (grade, lut)`, WP-36 (Whole-frame grade and LUT; per-layer correction WP-36) |
| Light and surface shading from drawn shapes | Volume lighting on flat art | **partial**: `shots.json style.toon`, WP-36 (Fixed-light cel shading on vector fills) |
| Particle systems (rain, snow, sparks) | Environmental and impact effects | **data**: `shots.json comp particles`, `proc.cook` (Seeded particle presets (rain, snow, confetti) drawn as a pure function of time on any depth plane; speed lines, dust and impact stars from motion.) |
| Animated matte generator from drawing strokes | Quick mattes that follow animation | **gap**: WP-36 (Comes with the compositor) |
| OpenFX plugin support | Third-party effects inside the comp | **gap**: WP-36 (Natron and OpenFX are WP-34/WP-36 candidates) |
| Animating effect parameters over time | Effects that build and fade | **gap**: WP-36 (post is static per shot) |
| Real-time effect preview in the camera view (improved in 25) | Judge effects without a full render | **partial**: `film.preview`, WP-36 (The player previews boil, toon shading and motion effects; compositor nodes come later) |

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |

| Sound editor: trim, fade and volume envelopes | Clean up and mix tracks in the scene | **gap**: WP-40 (Moving, trimming and replacing cues in Studio is WP-40) |
| Waveform display in the timeline | Animate to visible sound | **gap**: WP-40 (Waveform lanes are WP-40) |

| Automatic lip-sync detection into mouth shapes | Fast dialogue breakdown | **command**: `voice.build`, `lipsync.check` (Phoneme-level visemes; Rhubarb comparison when installed) |
| Mapping detected phonemes to a character's mouth drawings | Fit each character's own mouth chart | **data**: puppet swaps mouth (talk, emotion charts) (Emotion-aware mouth charts) |

| Audio folder in the library to preview and drag sounds into the timeline (new in 25.2) | Shared sound effects across scenes | **partial**: `asset.check`, WP-13 (Synth effects in the manifest; a sample library (Kenney CC0) is WP-13) |

| Batch rendering of many scenes | Overnight renders on render machines | **partial**: `film.render`, `prod.launch`, WP-38 (Parallel workers per machine and films in parallel sessions; a render farm is WP-38) |

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
