## Moho Pro (14.5 (Moho 14 series; covers 14.0 to 14.5 and the 13.5 features still in the manual))

Sources: [Lost Marble FAQ: where to find the Moho manuals](https://lostmarble.com/support/kb/faq.php?id=8); [Moho 14 Users Manual (official online wiki)](https://manual.lostmarble.com/); [Moho 14 Users Manual: top-level table of contents (27 chapters, appendices A to G)](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1UxA8Gi5DttJku9AmFlSpO0gJw4U9flX3); [Manual 1.02: What's New in Moho 14](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1JQiteCeMqxlAdhDW21kNVsjMoxyAHPo-q0QHnIEnhYI/edit); [Manual 1.02b: What's New in 14.1](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1FU5G9IPZ5f0NrbjMHWiUj8AvVQvLa2gX1oqj3A8A4tE/edit); [Manual 1.02c: What's New in 14.2](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1SQAoXD4ZZ2sai5o81wjrvoI9P1p51MHSmCbAiGXDzrs/edit); [Manual 1.02d: What's New in 14.3](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1hmWinmAPVyqnGpAtAkaZcKgyPNG0TXUhv2j58CA_vTY/edit); [Manual 1.02e: What's New in 14.4](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1h_zs407rIl56m1mjoRnE0pyJir_VPCLRll5uhfi85Is/edit)

### Chapter 1: Introduction (incl. What's New 14.0 to 14.5)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| System requirements and installation: the supported OS, GPU and tablet setup, and a one-step install. | Studios image every workstation the same way so files open and render identically. | **command**: `engine.doctor`, `engine.info` (engine.doctor checks core, audio and tool dependencies and prints the exact fix for each (tools/setup.sh applies them); engine.info reports the renderers available.) |
| Getting-started projects and startup content: ready-made documents and sample characters to start from. | Teams start a new episode from a house template rather than an empty canvas. | **command**: `film.create`, `film.from_template` (film.create makes an empty film folder. film.from_template builds a storyboard from a format template (dialogue, explainer, myth_vs_data).) |
| A custom content folder that holds user brushes, scripts, styles and presets in one place. | A studio keeps its own brushes, rigs and tools in one place, version-controlled and shared by everyone. | **data**: cast/ library, `puppet/clips/<template>.json`, `assets/manifest.json` (Characters, clip libraries and third-party assets are files in the repo, and every asset has a manifest row with its licence.) |
| Licence activation, deactivation and automatic update checks. | IT moves seats between machines and controls which version is in use. | **n/a** (Doodleberry is in-house code with no seats or activation. The version is whatever git checkout is in use.) |
| Live Performance Mode (14.5): drive a rig in real time from a webcam, microphone, keyboard or game controller, on top of existing animation. | Used for fast dialogue blocking, live streams and VTuber-style shows with a studio character. | **partial**: `actout.clip`, WP-12 (actout.clip turns a recorded take (MediaPipe body and face) into a clip. Real-time live tracking (E9) is a WP-12 follow-on.) |
| Live Playback capture: keep a performance as editable keyframes, with separate passes per character or per selected layers. | Animators capture a rough take and then polish the keys by hand, layering several passes. | **partial**: `actout.clip`, `timeline.edit`, WP-12 (A take becomes a reduced-key clip on the character, and timeline.edit moves or resizes it. Multi-person takes and live passes are in WP-12.) |
| Face-tracked head turns and expressions: tracking drives a head-turn control bone and facial Smart Bones, including eyes, brows and mouth. | Gives quick, natural head and face acting for talking-head and dialogue scenes. | **partial**: `actout.clip`, WP-12 (Face Landmarker blendshapes already become eye, brow, gaze and mouth keys. Feeding head yaw into the puppet's turn control is named in WP-12.) |
| Gamepad and joystick controls: buttons trigger actions and sticks move bones, optionally holding the pose on release. | Lets a puppeteer trigger gestures and expressions live, like playing an instrument. | **gap**: WP-12 (There is no live input device path. It fits WP-12's live tracking follow-on.) |
| Virtual webcam output: broadcast the live-performed character to meeting or streaming software. | Puts a branded character into live calls, classes and streams. | **gap**: WP-12 (Live 2D avatars of the cast (E9) are a WP-12 P2 follow-on.) |

### Chapter 2: License

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Licence types (Pro vs Debut, perpetual, floating or network licences for studios). | Studios use floating licences so seats follow the artist. | **n/a** (Doodleberry is in-house code with no product licensing. The licences of components it uses are tracked separately (licence.ledger).) |
| The end-user licence agreement, which sets how the software and the bundled content may be used. | Legal checks whether the bundled content may appear in client or broadcast work. | **partial**: `licence.ledger`, `film.provenance`, WP-33 (Doodleberry has no EULA. The matching control is a licence class for every component, with restricted inputs flagged at the posting gate. WP-33 finishes per-render recording.) |

### Chapter 3: The Draw Tools

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Bezier curve editing: add points, then shape curvature with full handles or smooth and peaked corners. | Cleans character outlines precisely so they deform well under bones. | **partial**: `puppet.export_layers`, `puppet.import`, WP-34 (Parts are SVG path data, so they can be edited through a layered-SVG round trip (for example in Inkscape). There is no in-house handle editor. Vector editing is a WP-34 integration candidate.) |
| Freehand drawing with pressure, smoothing, auto-fill, Shift for straight lines, trimming of stray tails and welding of endpoints (14.0, 14.5). | Used for rough-to-clean drawing straight into the rig, and for frame-by-frame effects. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (The Rig Canvas ink uses perfect-freehand pressure and RDP simplification, and puppet.save writes the result. A stabiliser, tail trimming and welding are in WP-18.) |
| Boolean shape operations: add, subtract and intersect shapes. | Builds complex props and cut-outs (holes, highlights) quickly. | **gap**: WP-18 (Only even-odd fill rules exist (strokes 'rule': 'evenodd'). Shape booleans belong with the Rig Canvas tools in WP-18. Skia-pathops or Clipper2 are already used for toon shading.) |
| Point selection and transformation: lasso or box select, then move, scale and rotate about a pivot. | Used to adjust a mouth or a hand drawing without redrawing it. | **gap**: WP-18 (The feature map lists lasso and transform under WP-18.) |
| Shape primitives (rectangle, oval, star, arrow) and the blob brush for filled organic shapes. | Gives fast blocking of props, backgrounds and filled masses. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (Ink strokes are already filled outline shapes (blob-like). There are no primitives in the Rig Canvas. Sets and props are drawn in code (sets/*.js).) |
| An eraser that removes both fills and strokes, optionally limited to the selected shapes. | Used to fix drawings in place. | **gap**: WP-18 (The eraser is in the feature map's brush-engine row, owned by WP-18.) |
| Point reduction: simplify a curve to fewer points. | Keeps rigged art light so it deforms cleanly and renders fast. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, WP-18 (Strokes are RDP-simplified on capture. Cleaning up imported parts (picosvg, svgo, vpype) is in WP-18.) |
| Scatter brush: stamp many varied copies of a shape (leaves, grass, crowds of dots). | Used to dress backgrounds quickly. | **command**: `proc.cook` (proc.cook scatters points (seeded, minimum spacing) and instances cast members or 3D blocks on them.) |
| Point deformers: perspective, shear and bend applied to selected points. | Used to fake a turn or a foreshortening on a drawing without redrawing it. | **gap**: WP-34 (WP-34 covers vector editing and deformers through Synfig core and Inkscape.) |
| Noise tool and vector noise: random wobble on points, optionally animated, or only while the shape moves. | Gives a hand-made, jittery line look. | **data**: `shots.json style.boil` (Deterministic line boil (cycled displacement fields at a chosen fps, amount and frequency) gives the animated-wobble look. A permanent jitter of points is not offered.) |
| Magnet tool: drag points with a soft falloff, and Shift-drag back toward their frame-0 shape (14.5). | Used to sculpt squash, cheeks and cloth on a frame. | **gap**: WP-18 (There is no point-level sculpting in the Rig Canvas.) |
| Liquid shapes (14.0): soft vector shapes that merge, subtract and intersect live as they animate. | Used for fire, smoke, water and blobby character effects. | **gap**: WP-34 (This needs live vector booleans in the engine (WP-34 vector editing). The effects use (fire, water, smoke) also overlaps WP-35.) |

### Chapter 4: Fill Tools

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Create shapes and paint-bucket fills from linework, with gap closing, a trace mode that outlines visible regions across layers, and weld-crossings (14.3, 14.5). | Colours line art quickly, including rough lines that do not quite close. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (The Rig Canvas bucket fills an inked area, closes gaps up to about 10 px and tucks the fill under the ink. Trace-across-layers is in WP-18.) |
| Recolour mode: change an existing shape's fill or stroke colour without new points. | Used for colour passes and palette changes late in production. | **data**: puppet palette, set_opts (Fills and strokes name palette slots, so recolouring a slot recolours every shape using it. set_opts recolours sets.) |
| Line width per point, with pressure, random width and whole-curve resizing. | Gives an expressive inked line weight that tapers at joints. | **partial**: `puppet parts[].strokes[].width`, WP-15 (Each stroke has one width, and Rig Canvas ink bakes pressure into its outline. Animatable per-point width belongs with line styles in WP-15.) |
| Hide edge: make chosen segments of an outline invisible while the fill stays. | Gives seamless joins where parts overlap, such as the neck or shoulders. | **gap**: WP-18 (A stroke can drop its stroke colour entirely, but not per segment. Artists draw parts past the joint instead (puppet.coach checks this).) |
| Stroke exposure: animate a line drawing itself on or off. | Used for write-on titles, signatures and diagram reveals. | **gap**: WP-15 (There is no draw-on for vector strokes (only typed consoles and procedural growth such as the roots kit). It should join WP-15 with kinetic typography.) |
| Curve profile: shape a stroke's cross-section along its length, animatable with an offset (14.2). | Used for ribbon-like hair, tubes and stylised outlines. | **gap**: WP-15 (It should join WP-15 (line styles and medium simulation for vector lines).) |
| Point colours: colour that varies across a shape, following its curve. | Gives soft colour transitions on vector characters without gradients. | **gap**: WP-15 (It should join WP-15. Every renderer would need it (WP-14).) |
| Brush opacity and colour drift per point, driven by pressure or randomness and animatable (14.3). | Gives natural-media lines (pencil, watercolour) on rigged vector art. | **gap**: WP-34 (Brush engines are a WP-34 integration candidate (Krita, libmypaint). The vector medium look is also in WP-15.) |

### Chapter 5: Bone Tools

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Build a bone hierarchy: add, select, reparent and offset bones, set pivots and pose with forward kinematics. | This is the base of every cut-out rig. | **data**: `puppet parts[].parent`, `puppet parts[].pivot` (Each part is a bone with a parent and a pivot, posed by angles relative to the drawn pose. The Rig Canvas sets pivots, and doodle.cut confirms them for doodles.) |
| Inverse kinematics while posing: drag a hand or foot and the chain follows, with bone locks and targets. | Gives fast posing and planted feet for walks. | **data**: acting timeline ik, acting timeline pins, puppet chains (There is two-bone IK with foot pins, and N-bone FABRIK chains. The Rig Canvas Pose tool drags hands and feet by IK.) |
| Bone strength and region binding: each bone's falloff weights nearby points for smooth deformation. | Gives smooth elbows, knees and torsos without visible hinges. | **partial**: `puppet.from_blend`, WP-14 (Mesh strokes with per-vertex weights (puppet v0.5) and bendy limbs exist, but only in the SVG renderer (bendy limbs also in Grease Pencil). Other renderers are in WP-14. There is no strength-painting tool.) |
| Sketch bones: draw a whole chain of bones in one gesture. | Gives quick rigging of tails, hair and props. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, `doodle.snap`, `cast.generate`, WP-18 (Rigs are made automatically (pivots estimated on import, suggested for doodles, generated for cast.generate). Interactive chain drawing in the Rig Canvas is WP-18.) |
| Bind layers and bind points: attach whole drawings or individual points to chosen bones. | Controls exactly which art follows which bone. | **data**: `puppet parts[].strokes`, `puppet swaps[].anchor`, mesh strokes weights (A part's strokes move with its bone, and swap sets anchor to a part. Per-vertex binding exists only for mesh strokes.) |
| Bone physics: pill-shaped collision regions, motors (speed and torque), and lock tip, inside a physics group. | Used for ragdolls, machines and objects that tumble. | **partial**: `physics.solve`, WP-42 (Rigid bodies (balls, boxes, props) collide with each other and with static colliders in physics.solve (deterministic Rapier). Physical bones (ragdolls, motors) are WP-42.) |
| Smart Bones: a bone's rotation automatically drives corrective actions (point shapes, colours, switches, order, flips), one action per direction. | This is Moho's signature technique: clean elbows and head turns, and face rigs driven by one dial. | **partial**: `puppet format v0.9 by_angle`, WP-11 (Controls (v0.4 driven keys) drive angles, shifts, swaps, z and scale from one value, and swaps can follow a part's angle (v0.9 by_angle); corrective point shapes driven by a bone's angle are WP-11.) |
| Control bones as dials or levers that a rig exposes for expressions and turns, including 360-degree Smart Bones (14.4). | Animators work from a small set of high-level controls instead of many bones. | **data**: puppet controls, acting timeline controls (Named controls with a range (for example Pip's 'turn') are animated per shot as [[t, value]]. Wrap-around 360 controls are not offered.) |
| Force / wind tool (13.5): wind with strength, direction and turbulence acting on dynamic bones. | Used for hair, flags, grass and clothes in outdoor scenes. | **gap**: WP-42 (physics.solve has gravity and kicks but no wind field; wind acting on springs and dynamic parts is WP-42.) |
| Vitruvian bones (13.5): one bone layer holds several alternate bone sets and drawings (for example front and side limbs), switched per frame. | Gives foreshortening, turnarounds and extreme poses in a single rig. | **data**: puppet views, acting timeline view (Views override parts, pivots and swaps per view and are switched by the timeline with a turn smear. Every renderer and exporter supports them.) |
| Bone constraints: angle limits, point-at targets (now reaching a full 360 degrees), copy and independent targeting, and ignoring IK. | Keeps rigs from breaking and automates eye aim and props. | **data**: `shots.json timeline ik_weight`, `puppet format v0.9 copy_rotation` (Joint limits, look-at with time windows, IK/FK blending, IK targets on other characters, stretchy IK and copy-rotation constraints exist.) |
| Animated bone parenting and parent switching over time. | Used for hand-offs, picking things up, and a character mounting a vehicle. | **partial**: `shots.json props attach`, `interact.solve`, WP-11 (Props switch parents (hand to hand, throws, drops); characters reach for each other's parts (interact.solve handshakes: an IK target on another character, blended in and out). Re-parenting a character's own parts over time is WP-11.) |

### Chapter 6: Layer Tools

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Transform layers (move, scale, rotate) and set a layer's origin, all animatable. | Used to place and move characters and props within a scene. | **data**: `shots.json cast[].place`, acting timeline travel, `shots.json props` (Characters are placed per shot and travel over time. Props have positions, spin and travel.) |
| Follow path: a layer travels along a drawn curve, optionally oriented to it. | Used for flights, vehicles and characters walking a curved route. | **data**: `shots.json place.path`, `shots.json props path` (Characters walk along drawn curves (place.path) and props follow paths (props path, oriented to the curve when asked).) |
| Rotate a layer about X or Y (3D tilt of flat art) and shear a layer. | Gives cheap perspective on signs, doors and cards. | **gap**: WP-36 (Flat layers in depth and the multiplane camera are WP-36.) |
| Insert text as an editable layer with font, size and colour. | Used for titles, signs and labels inside the scene. | **data**: `shots.json overlays (text, sign, title, paper)` (Text and sign overlays and title kit elements are timed per shot. Sinhala and other scripts fall back per glyph.) |
| Word balloons: speech balloons with tails pointing at a speaker. | Used for comic-style dialogue and captioned, sound-off cuts. | **data**: `shots.json bubbles`, script lines style/bubble, `shots.json extra_bubbles` (Say, shout and whisper bubbles are synced to the voice and avoid collisions. Thought clouds and narration boxes are WP-17.) |
| Layer selector and eyedropper: pick a layer or copy a style by clicking on the canvas. | Speeds up hands-on editing in dense scenes. | **n/a** (In Doodleberry, parts, cast and colours are named ids and palette slots that agents address directly. The Rig Canvas already selects parts by clicking.) |

### Chapter 7: Camera and Workspace Tools

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Track (pan) and zoom the camera, keyframed. | Used for push-ins, reveals and following action. | **data**: `shots.json camera`, `shots.json camera_auto` (Camera keys [[t, [cx, cy, zoom]]] are folded into the bake for every renderer. camera_auto adds speaker framing and punch-ins as a pure function of time.) |
| Roll the camera (tilt the frame sideways). | Used for dutch angles and impact moments. | **gap**: WP-36 (Camera keys have no rotation. It should join WP-36 (multiplane camera).) |
| Pan/tilt the camera in 3D among layers placed at depth, giving real parallax. | Gives multiplane-style depth in backgrounds. | **gap**: WP-36 (Some sets fake parallax (forest depths). True multiplane depth is WP-36.) |
| Show the camera's motion path, and orbit the workspace to view the scene from outside the camera. | Used to check and plan complex camera moves. | **gap**: WP-19 (A camera-keys lane in the Studio timeline is in WP-19.) |
| Workspace navigation: pan, zoom, rotate and flip the canvas view (not the output). | Lets the artist draw at a comfortable angle and check a drawing mirrored. | **n/a** (This is a view-only editor convenience. Doodleberry renders straight from data, and the Rig Canvas has its own canvas view.) |

### Chapter 8: Special Tools

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Switch layers: a set of alternative drawings, one shown at a time and keyed on the timeline (mouths, hands, eyes). | Used for lip-sync charts, hand poses and expression swaps. | **data**: puppet swaps, acting timeline poses (Mouths, eyes, brows, pupils and hand poses are swap sets, chosen by lip-sync, blink or name.) |
| Particle layers: emit copies of child layers with speed, spread, lifetime and gravity. | Used for rain, snow, sparks, smoke and simple crowds. | **partial**: `proc.cook`, WP-42 (Particles (confetti, snow, rain) and procedural crowds of cast members exist; emitting copies of arbitrary child layers is WP-42.) |
| Image masking and cropping: cut away part of a bitmap with a vector mask. | Used to split painted or scanned art into riggable pieces. | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, `doodle.cut`, WP-36 (Raster parts are cut by polygons with hidden overlap seams. General per-layer masks are WP-36.) |
| Video tracking (much improved in 14.5): track a feature through footage or image sequences, with a second point for angle and scale, and apply it to a layer as keys. | Used to pin animation onto live-action plates or to follow a moving camera. | **gap**: WP-12 (actout.clip tracks people only. Tracking points and backgrounds (camera-motion compensation) is in WP-12.) |
| Face tracking from video (14.5): analyse recorded footage into facial bones, facial Smart Bones and a face mesh. | Gives quick facial performance capture for dialogue. | **partial**: `actout.clip`, WP-12 (A recorded take gives eye, brow, gaze and mouth keys on an existing rig. Head yaw into the turn control is WP-12. Building a rig from the face is not planned.) |

### Chapter 9: Moho's Main Window

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A working area that previews close to the final render (the 14.0 graphics engine), with display-quality toggles for effects, masks and brushes. | Animators judge the final look while they work, without test renders. | **command**: `film.preview` (The player page is the SVG renderer itself (each frame a pure function of time), so the preview matches the render exactly.) |
| Playback controls: play, loop, step frames and jump between keys. | Used for timing reviews. | **command**: `film.preview`, `export.web` (The Studio timeline and the review room scrub and frame-step. export.web gives a standalone player with play, scrub and frame step.) |
| Toolbar, status bar, docking windows and the Pro split views (side by side, four way). | Lets each artist arrange the screen to suit them. | **n/a** (Doodleberry is agent-native: tools are commands (engine.commands lists them with schemas) rather than a docked GUI.) |

### Chapter 10: Layers Window

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A layer list with nesting, reordering, visibility and quick settings. | Organises a character and a scene into a manageable tree. | **data**: `puppet parts[].z`, `puppet parts[].parent`, `shots.json cast` (Draw order is z per part (switchable by controls and clips). Scene order comes from the cast list.) |
| Searching and filtering the layer list, including showing only animated layers (14.5). | Finds what is keyed in big rigs. | **command**: `timeline.get` (timeline.get returns, per cast member, every clip layer, emotion and gaze key. The film data is plain JSON that agents can search.) |
| Layer comps: saved sets of visible layers that can be rendered separately (for example characters only, or background only). | Used for render passes handed to compositing. | **partial**: `shot.render`, `export.lottie`, WP-37 (The Synfig and Grease Pencil renderers already draw characters on alpha over a separate set video, and Lottie can drop the background. Named multi-pass output (OpenEXR passes) is WP-37.) |
| Layer referencing: instances of a layer or character that update from a master, including from other files. | One character master is used across many scenes and episodes. | **data**: `shots.json cast[].puppet (cast library reference)` (Films reference puppets from the cast library by name, so a fix to the master reaches every film on its next render.) |
| Layer colours and tags for organisation. | Gives visual grouping in large scenes. | **n/a** (This is a GUI organisation aid. Doodleberry's data is named ids.) |

### Chapter 11: Layer Types

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Vector layers: resolution-independent drawings made of points, curves and shapes. | The default art type for rigged characters. | **data**: `puppet parts[].strokes[].d` (Parts are SVG path data in artboard units, so output is resolution independent.) |
| Image layers: bitmaps (including painted or photographed art) placed and rigged like vectors. | Used for painted characters and scanned art. | **command**: `puppet.import`, `puppet.from_ink`, `doodle.snap` (These make raster parts (puppet v0.3) from PSD or ORA layers, photos or scans. Raster parts render only in SVG. Other renderers are WP-14.) |
| Image sequence layers: a numbered sequence or video played as one layer. | Used for hand-drawn effects, rotoscope plates and pre-rendered elements. | **gap**: WP-35 (Drawn levels (a sequence of drawings with exposures) are WP-35.) |
| Group layers, with group-level masking and shared settings. | Masks a character's clothing patterns or shading inside a group. | **partial**: `puppet parts[].parent`, WP-36 (The part hierarchy groups art. Group masks are WP-36.) |
| Bone layers that carry the skeleton for their children. | The container for every rig. | **data**: puppet parts (skeleton) (The skeleton lives in the puppet file. All renderers read the baked animation from tools/bake.mjs.) |
| Interpolated switch layers: smooth morphs between switch drawings that share point counts. | Gives smooth mouth and hand transitions instead of pops. | **gap**: WP-43 (Swaps are discrete (the only exception is a squash smear on view changes). Morphs between drawings or views are in WP-43.) |
| Frame-by-frame layers: traditional hand-drawn animation inside a rigged scene. | Used for effects, smears and hand-drawn moments mixed with the rig. | **gap**: WP-35 (Hand-drawn animation with drawn levels and an X-sheet is WP-35.) |
| Note layers: sticky notes placed in the scene for collaborators. | Used for director or supervisor notes on specific shots. | **command**: `review.add`, `review.list`, `review.fix` (Notes are pinned to frames in review.json, and review.fix turns them into checked data edits.) |
| Audio layers: several sound files placed on the timeline. | Used for dialogue, music and effects tracks lined up to picture. | **partial**: `voice.build`, `film.render`, WP-40 (Voice, a procedural score and timed cues are mixed with stems. Waveform lanes and moving, trimming or replacing audio clips are WP-40.) |
| 3D layers: imported 3D models (OBJ) or 2D shapes converted to 3D, in the scene. | Used for 3D props and sets under a 2D camera. | **partial**: `engine.info`, WP-42 (A three.js GLB renderer exists as a tool (tools/three/run3d.mjs), but no film command composites it with 2D. Blender 3D sets and passes are WP-42.) |
| Patch layers: a masked copy that hides the seam where two overlapping parts meet. | Gives clean shoulders, hips and necks on cut-out rigs. | **partial**: `shots.json timeline z`, `puppet.coach`, WP-18 (Ink cuts keep hidden overlaps, puppet.coach measures gaps opened by bending, and layer order can be keyed per pose (v0.9) to swap layering at joints; masked patch copies are WP-18.) |
| Text layers. | Used for on-screen text that stays editable. | **data**: `shots.json overlays` (This is the same match as moho-layer-tools-4.) |

### Chapter 12: Layer Settings

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Layer shadows, shading and outlines (drop shadow, inner shading, outline), previewed live. | Gives a lit, finished look on flat art without extra drawing. | **partial**: `shots.json style.toon`, WP-36 (Cel-shadow bands and rim light work in SVG, and ground shadows are on by default. Other renderers are WP-14. General layer effects are WP-36. Note that drop shadows are flagged by the AI-look lint.) |
| Motion blur (realistic, Pro) on moving layers. | Smooths fast action. | **gap**: WP-15 (Sub-frame motion blur on fast shots only is in WP-15.) |
| Masking modes per layer (add, subtract, clip, exclude strokes). | Used for clothing patterns, eye whites clipping pupils, and shading inside shapes. | **gap**: WP-36 (This is in the compositor: masks and mattes.) |
| Blend modes (multiply, screen, add, Color Burn, Linear Dodge) that match PSD behaviour (14.1, 14.2). | Gives lighting passes and faithful round trips with Photoshop art. | **gap**: WP-36 (Only ffmpeg overlays exist. Blend modes are WP-36.) |
| Animatable layer opacity, blur and pixelation (14.1, 14.2). | Used for fades, focus pulls and stylised effects. | **partial**: `puppet parts[].strokes[].opacity`, WP-36 (Stroke opacity is static, and finishing presets (grade, vignette, grain) apply per shot. Animated layer opacity and blur are WP-36.) |
| Depth sort and depth shift: order layers by their distance from the camera. | Gives correct layering when characters walk in depth. | **partial**: `puppet parts[].z`, `puppet controls z.<part>`, WP-36 (Z order is per part and switchable by controls and clips (SVG and Grease Pencil only). Depth planes are WP-36.) |
| Line boil and smart line boil: redraw jitter at set intervals, optionally only while the shape moves (14.0, 14.2). | Gives a hand-drawn shimmer on rigged art. | **partial**: `shots.json style.boil`, WP-15 (Deterministic boil (fps, amount, frequency) exists on characters, bubbles and signs. Boil only during motion is new and should join WP-15.) |
| Particles tab: count, speed, spread, lifetime, emission along curves, per-particle offsets and emission order (14.4). | Used to tune effects and procedural hair or tails. | **partial**: `shots.json comp particles`, WP-42 (Count, speed spread, start and seed per particle preset; emission along curves and per-particle lifetimes are WP-42.) |
| 3D options: extrude or lathe a vector shape into a 3D object. | Used for quick 3D props from 2D drawings. | **gap**: WP-42 (3D props through Blender are in WP-42.) |
| Physics tab: rigid bodies with mass, friction, bounciness and collisions between layers. | Used for falling objects, stacks and collisions without hand keys. | **command**: `physics.solve`, `shots.json physics` (Bodies with density, friction and restitution collide with each other and with static colliders (deterministic Rapier), baked into prop tracks; contacts can be retimed onto the beat.) |

### Chapter 13: Style Window

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Shared named styles applied to many shapes, so one change updates all of them, including bulk edits of a character's line width, brush and boil (14.4). | Keeps colour and line consistent across a character and an episode. | **partial**: puppet palette, WP-15 (Palette slots share colour across all shapes. Shared line styles (width, brush) across a character belong with line styles in WP-15.) |
| Gradient and texture fills. | Gives soft shading and patterned clothing. | **gap**: WP-18 (Puppet strokes take flat palette colours only (sets drawn in code may use gradients). It should join WP-18 for authoring, with WP-14 for renderer parity.) |
| Brushes and freehand styles (wet ink, charcoal, gouache, watercolour, pencil), with brush folders. | Gives natural-media looks on vector rigs. | **gap**: WP-15 (Medium simulation for vector lines (pencil, marker, crayon) and a watercolour pass are in WP-15. Brush engines are WP-34.) |
| Swatches and a colour palette library. | Holds show palettes and colour models. | **data**: puppet palette, `brand/<id>.json palette` (Named palette slots per puppet. The brand kit palette is measured by brand.check.) |

### Chapter 14: The Library Window

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A library of reusable characters, props, scenes and actions, with tabs and search. | Teams reuse a show's cast and props from one place. | **command**: `cast.list`, `cast.add` (This is the cast library with provenance. Clips come from template libraries and character clips. Sets come from sets/*.js.) |
| A details panel with item metadata. | Shows who made an asset and how it may be used. | **command**: `cast.list`, `asset.check` (Lists author, licence and voice profile, and checks every third-party file has a licence row.) |
| Library locations for teams (shared folders that several artists use). | Gives a shared studio asset store. | **partial**: `cast.add`, `prod.merge`, WP-39 (The library is in git and new characters merge through prod.merge. Assignments and locks on characters are WP-39.) |
| Bought or downloaded content packs added to the library. | Speeds up production with stock assets. | **partial**: `asset.check`, `licence.ledger`, WP-16 (Third-party assets are licence-tracked. More packs (Kenney, Open Peeps, Humaaans) are WP-16. Downloaded packs are never committed.) |

### Chapter 15: The Character Wizard

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Parametric character generation (body, face, clothing and style tabs) into a ready rig. | Gives fast background cast and prototypes. | **command**: `cast.generate` (Builds an original rigged humanoid from build, age, skin, hair, outfit and accessories. It works with every clip and renderer.) |
| Walk-cycle designer: parametric walks with stride and style, and making the character walk across the scene. | Gives quick locomotion for crowds and blocking. | **partial**: `interact.solve`, WP-11 (interact.solve walks a character along a curved path with the stride matched to the clip and feet planted (measured under 1 px of slide); a parametric walk designer (stride, bounce, style) is WP-11.) |
| Rotating the generated character through 360 degrees. | Gives turnarounds without redrawing. | **partial**: `cast.generate`, `puppet.sheet`, WP-43 (Generated characters get a turn control (three-quarter head), and views can be drawn. Turnaround boards are WP-18. Morphs between views are WP-43.) |
| Designing your own actions that work on any wizard character. | Lets a whole cast share one motion set. | **data**: `puppet/clips/<template>.json` (Clips are defined per template, so every character on a template plays them.) |
| Creating new content (hair, outfits, parts) for the wizard. | Lets a studio extend the generator with its own style. | **partial**: `cast.generate`, WP-16 (Generator options are fixed in code. Outfits as swap sets and a family look as parameters are WP-16.) |

### Chapter 16: The Timeline Window

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Keyframes per animation channel: add, select, move, copy and delete, including editing switch-type keys. | This is the core of keyframe animation. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, `timeline.get`, WP-19 (The timeline edits clip layers (move, resize, add, delete). Per-joint keys are recorded in the Rig Canvas and saved as clips. Dragging lines and shots, and a keys lane, are WP-19.) |
| Interpolation methods per key: smooth, linear, ease in/out, step, cycle, noisy, bounce, elastic, stagger. | Shapes the timing feel of each move. | **partial**: `shots.json style.step`, `puppet acting.principles`, WP-19 (Keys ease by smoothstep. There is stepped timing, looping clips, and anticipation and overshoot as a filter. Per-key interpolation types and curves are WP-19.) |
| Motion graph: edit animation curves directly. | Gives fine spacing and polish. | **gap**: WP-19 (The curve (graph) editor is in WP-19.) |
| Onion skins: ghosts of nearby frames or keys, coloured and movable (14.5). | Essential for spacing and frame-by-frame work. | **gap**: WP-35 (Onion skins are in WP-35, with expression boards with onion skin in WP-18.) |
| Animation interval: animate on twos or threes. | Gives a hand-made, limited-animation feel. | **data**: `shots.json style.step` (Animate on 12s, 8s or 6s per shot while the film runs at 30 fps. Repeated frames are deduplicated at render.) |
| Timeline markers: named or interval markers for beats and notes. | Lets animators time to music and dialogue beats. | **partial**: `film.beats`, `timeline.get`, WP-19 (There is a beat grid from the score, plus dialogue lanes with word ticks, and times anchor to lines. User-named markers are not offered (WP-19 timeline 2).) |
| Sequencer: move and offset whole layers' animation blocks in time. | Lets an animator re-time whole performances without touching keys. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (Clip layers move and resize, and line-relative times stay line-relative. Dragging shots and lines is WP-19.) |
| Push frames left or right: insert or remove time across all layers. | Used to open up a pause or cut frames late in production. | **partial**: `film.retime`, `script.slots`, WP-19 (Changing a line's slot or voice re-flows shots, acting, captions and cues automatically. A direct insert-time edit is not offered.) |
| Relative keyframing: offset a pose additively across existing keys. | Lets an animator adjust a whole range at once. | **data**: `acting timeline clips[].additive` (Additive clip layers add on top of the stack.) |
| Mute or hide animation channels. | Isolates parts of the animation while polishing. | **data**: `acting timeline clips[].weight` (A clip layer's weight 0 mutes it. There is no per-joint channel mute.) |
| Multiple layers shown together in the timeline, and playing a chosen segment. | Used to compare timing across characters. | **command**: `timeline.get`, `shot.render` (The Studio timeline shows every character's lanes, the dialogue and the shots. shot.render renders one shot.) |

### Chapter 17: Other Windows (Audio Recording, Actions)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Audio recording inside the app, straight onto the timeline. | Used for scratch dialogue for animatics and for solo creators' final voices. | **partial**: `voice.build`, WP-13 (Voices are synthesised and aligned. Human voice-over import with phone-level alignment, fitted to slots, is WP-13.) |
| Actions window: named reusable animations (walk, wave, blink) stored with a rig and inserted on the timeline. | Builds a motion library per character. | **data**: puppet clips, `puppet/clips/<template>.json` (There are 33 library clips plus each character's own clips (v0.7). They are added with timeline.edit add_clip or from prose with acting.direct.) |
| Blending actions onto the timeline with weights, and making actions relative. | Lets an animator layer a gesture over a walk. | **data**: `acting timeline clips[].weight/fadeIn/additive` (A nonlinear clip stack with fades, weights, additive layers and base layers.) |
| Export and import actions, including child layers (14.5), between files and rigs. | Shares motion across shows. | **data**: `puppet/clips/<template>.json`, puppet clips (Clips are template-level, so they work across every character on a template. actout.clip retargets takes onto any rig.) |
| Automatic lip sync: analyse an audio track to drive a mouth switch layer, with random alternate visemes (13.5.3), and label phonemes on the timeline (14.5). | Cuts most of the lip-sync work in dialogue-heavy series. | **command**: `voice.build`, `lipsync.check`, `doodle.say` (Visemes come from the TTS phoneme timings or the aligner, are written to timing.json and drive the mouth swap. lipsync.check measures lag and correlation against the voice.) |
| Smart Bone lip sync (14.5): phoneme poses that drive the whole face (jaw, cheeks) rather than only mouth drawings. | Gives richer, rig-driven dialogue acting. | **data**: `puppet format v0.9 lipsync` (Phoneme poses drive the whole face (lipsync: angles, shifts and scales per mouth shape, cross-faded with the visemes) besides the mouth drawings.) |

### Chapter 18: File Menu

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| New document, new workspace and new from template. | Gives consistent project setups per show. | **command**: `film.create`, `film.from_template` (Format templates draft the script, shots and timing.) |
| Gather media: copy every external file a project uses into its folder. | Used to archive and hand projects to other studios. | **partial**: `film.handoff`, WP-22 (film.handoff gathers the shots, stems and captions a cut uses into one folder; gathering a project's source files is WP-22.) |
| Save as older file versions and strip comments and tags. | Used to share with collaborators on older versions. | **n/a** (There is a single in-house format, versioned (puppet 'version'). Older files stay valid in newer loaders.) |
| Project settings: size, frame rate (including 23.976), background, depth of field, 3D camera, stereo and noise grain. | Sets the delivery spec for a show. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-36 (The QA bar is fixed at 1080x1920 at 30 fps, and other aspects use film.render's 'aspect'. Grain is in the finishing presets. Depth of field is WP-36, and higher-frame-rate or 4K masters are WP-37.) |
| Import images, image sequences, movies, SVG, audio, OBJ models and other Moho documents. | Brings outside art, plates and assets into a scene. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, `puppet.from_ink`, `puppet.from_blend`, WP-35 (Characters can come from SVG, PSD, ORA, photos and Blender rigs. Image sequences and movies as scene layers are WP-35 and WP-42.) |
| Photoshop import with layers becoming separate parts (with clipping and blend modes, 14.2). | Brings illustrators' layered art into the rig. | **command**: `puppet.import` (Maps PSD layer names to template parts, swaps, pivots and anchors. PSD blend modes are not kept (WP-36).) |
| Real-time media connection: imported images update live when they are edited in another program. | Lets painters revise art while animators keep working. | **partial**: `puppet.export_layers`, `puppet.import`, WP-18 (There is a round trip by export and re-import, but no live link. Krita .kra import is WP-18.) |
| Preview renders and preview animations (lower quality, quick). | Used to check timing and look before a full render. | **command**: `film.animatic`, `film.preview`, `shot.render` (The animatic takes seconds, the player preview is live, and one shot can be rendered alone.) |
| Export animation to video and image sequences: MP4 (H.264 default in 14.5, H.265), MOV ProRes with alpha, AVI, PNG/TIFF/WebP/PSD sequences, animated GIF and SVG sequences. | Delivers to editors, platforms and compositors. | **partial**: `film.render`, `shot.render`, WP-23 (film.render delivers H.264 per the platform profiles. WebM and ProRes with alpha, GIF and APNG are WP-23.) |
| OpenEXR export and import with bit depth, colour spaces (including ACEScg) and compression (14.5). | High-precision frames for compositing and post. | **partial**: `film.master`, WP-37 (EXR export in half float with ACEScg (OCIO); EXR import is WP-37.) |
| Moho Exporter: a batch render queue in a separate process, with per-item status and reruns (14.5). | Renders a whole episode's scenes overnight. | **partial**: `film.render`, `prod.status`, WP-38 (Async jobs, incremental re-render by content hash, and parallel film sessions on the board already exist. Distributed render workers are WP-38.) |
| Export to game engines and Blender (14.4): glTF/GLB with rig, actions and Smart Bones as morph targets; FBX for Unity. | Lets 2D characters run inside games and 3D pipelines. | **gap**: WP-30 (Lottie (export.lottie) and Synfig (export.synfig) exist. Game runtime export (Godot, sprite sheets) is WP-30.) |
| Export a single frame to SVG. | Used for print, marketing stills and vector hand-off. | **partial**: `film.storyboard`, `puppet.sheet`, WP-23 (Stills and model sheets exist (PNG, plus SVG from the Rig Canvas sheet). Print-ready transparent stills are WP-23.) |
| Export the full soundtrack or a single audio layer as WAV. | Hands the mix to a sound editor. | **command**: `film.render` (Writes the mix and the voice, music and effects stems to build/. A stems hand-off to editors is WP-22.) |

### Chapter 19: Edit Menu

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Undo and redo history, with merged steps for repeated edits (14.4). | Lets artists experiment safely. | **data**: films/<name>/ (text in git) (Films are text, so every version can be diffed and re-rendered exactly. The Studio timeline also has Ctrl+Z.) |
| Copy and paste layers with their animation, and paste relative. | Reuses animation across scenes. | **partial**: `film.cuts`, `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (Clips and cast references are reused by name, and inserts come from other films in edit lists. A copy-paste of a timeline range is WP-19.) |
| Custom keyboard shortcuts and device presets. | Gives speed for power users. | **n/a** (Doodleberry is agent-native: commands are reached through the CLI, HTTP and MCP. Studio has a few fixed keys.) |
| Preferences: tool defaults, input devices, autosave and display. | Sets per-artist and per-studio behaviour. | **data**: DBE_* environment variables, `dbe/profiles.json` (Environment variables pick binaries and quality, and profiles hold per-style QA thresholds and encoding.) |

### Chapter 20: Draw Menu

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Snap to grid. | Used for precise layouts and props. | **gap**: WP-18 (The Rig Canvas has no grid. The Studio timeline snaps to frames and words.) |
| Reset or randomise line widths, and peak or smooth selected points. | Used for quick line-quality passes. | **gap**: WP-15 (Line-quality controls belong with line styles in WP-15. Point corner editing is WP-18.) |
| Raise or lower shapes, to front or back (stacking order within a layer), animatable. | Used to fix overlaps such as a hand behind a sleeve. | **data**: `puppet parts[].z`, `puppet controls z.<part>`, `clip swaps.z` (Z order is per part and per swap, and controls and clips can switch it over time.) |
| Trace image: vectorise a bitmap automatically. | Turns sketches and scans into editable vectors. | **gap**: WP-18 (Ink-fidelity import keeps scans as raster parts (puppet.from_ink). Vector tracing (VTracer, potrace) is WP-18.) |
| Create a mesh layer and triangulate a 2D mesh over artwork. | Gives smooth deformation of painted art. | **partial**: `puppet.from_blend`, WP-18 (Skinned mesh strokes exist (from Blender cut-out rigs), but there is no in-house triangulation of an arbitrary drawing.) |
| Curver layers: bend artwork along an animatable spline, including compressible curvers. | Used for rubber-hose limbs, tails and bending props. | **partial**: `puppet parts[].bend`, WP-34 (Bendy limbs curve parts at joints (SVG and Grease Pencil). Spline deformers on any layer are a WP-34 candidate.) |
| Freeze points and reset points: key the current shape, or restore the rest shape. | Used to lock and unlock poses during animation. | **data**: puppet poses (Named poses are saved and keyed (poses: [[t, name]]). Point-level freezing does not apply, because shapes are not animated per point.) |

### Chapter 21: Bone Menu

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Smooth joint for a bone pair: an automatic curved elbow or knee. | Gives clean bends without corrective drawings. | **data**: `puppet parts[].bend` ('bend': {'width'} curves a limb out of its parent while keeping its thickness. It works in SVG and Grease Pencil; Synfig and Lottie stay rigid (WP-14).) |
| Release, flexi-bind and link bones to layers (re-binding art to the skeleton). | Used to fix and rework rigs. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, `puppet.lint`, WP-18 (Binding is implicit (art belongs to a part). Re-binding means editing the puppet or re-importing it. A Rig Canvas editing pass is WP-18.) |
| Track a bone to video. | Gives motion capture of a single point onto a rig. | **partial**: `actout.clip`, WP-12 (Body landmarks retarget to joint angles and root travel. Arbitrary point tracking is WP-12.) |
| Make Smart Bone dial: build a control and its actions in one step. | Speeds up face and turn rigs. | **partial**: `cast.generate`, WP-18 (Generated characters get a turn control. Authoring other controls is JSON only, with no canvas tool (WP-18).) |
| Freeze pose, freeze selected bones, and reset bones. | Keys every bone at once to hold or restore a pose. | **data**: puppet poses, acting timeline poses (The Rig Canvas 'Key' and 'Save pose' record full poses.) |
| Hide, shy or controlled bones, and coloured bones. | Declutters complex rigs. | **n/a** (This is display-only GUI management.) |

### Chapter 22: Animation Menu

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Bone dynamics: follow-through on rotation, scale and position with weight and gravity, delayed (lagging or leading) control bones (14.0), and toggling by Smart Bones (14.2). | Gives automatic overlap on hair, ears, tails and cloth. | **data**: puppet springs, puppet chains wave (Deterministic damped springs follow a driver part, and chains have a travelling wave. Parts have no gravity and no position or scale dynamics (WP-42); props do (physics.solve).) |
| Set a layer's start time and offset its animation. | Staggers repeated characters or actions. | **data**: `shots.json line-relative times` (Any time can reference a line ('mid.start+0.9') or be given in seconds.) |
| Rescale keyframes: stretch or compress the timing of a range. | Used for quick timing fixes. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (resize_clip changes a clip layer's length, and the puppet's 'acting.tempo' scales a character's clip speed. Rescaling an arbitrary range of keys is not offered (WP-19 timeline 2).) |
| Copy deep frame or frame range: copy every layer's state at a time and paste it elsewhere. | Used for holds and returns to a key pose. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (Poses and clips can be re-added at any time. A range copy across all characters is WP-19.) |
| Nudge a physics object: give it an impulse at a frame. | Starts simulations on cue. | **command**: `physics.solve`, `shots.json physics impulses` (physics impulses {t, body, v} kick a body at a time; a prop's attach key starts a throw or drop.) |
| Audio level, mute and restart of audio and movie tracks. | Used for a working mix while animating. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-40 (The mix is automatic (ducking, then -14 LUFS measured by qa.run). Manual per-cue levels in Studio are WP-40.) |
| Align a layer with the camera (billboarding) and allow frame skipping during playback. | Keeps flat cards facing a 3D camera, and gives real-time previews. | **n/a** (There is no 3D camera in the 2D path, and the preview plays the renderer directly.) |

### Chapter 23: Scripts Menu (Pro Only)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Lua scripting API (Lua 5.4 in 14.0) for custom tools, menu commands and automation, with documentation. | Pipeline TDs automate rig building, batch fixes and studio tools. | **command**: `engine.commands` (Every feature is an @command with a JSON schema, reachable from the CLI, HTTP, MCP and Studio.) |
| Embedded layer scripts that run every frame for procedural animation. | Used for procedural wiggles, clocks and data-driven motion. | **data**: `films/<name>/scene.js` (Scene films are frame functions that must be pure functions of the frame number. Data-bound kit elements read data.json.) |
| Bundled script packs: tiling, visibility, particle effects, layer effects, warp, sound and 3D helpers. | Gives ready-made effects without coding. | **partial**: `shots.json overlays (kit elements)`, WP-42 (The explainer kit and motion effects cover some cases. Procedural tiling, scatter and particles are WP-42.) |
| Installing third-party scripts. | Lets studios add community tools. | **command**: `vendor.check`, `vendor.build` (Third-party code is vendored with UPSTREAM.json, a licence class and a recorded build. A new command is added in dbe/commands.py.) |

### Chapter 24: View Menu

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Video safe zones and a rule-of-thirds overlay. | Keeps titles and action inside the safe area for each platform. | **metric**: `qa.run safe-area text check`, `qa.run minimum type size` (Every visible word's box is measured against the platform-safe area per aspect. brand.assets writes the safe-area overlays. There is no rule-of-thirds guide.) |
| Tracing image: a reference picture shown behind the canvas. | Used to draw over model sheets, sketches or photos. | **partial**: `drawing.requests`, WP-18 (Drawing requests give ghost guides (the character at rest, the related drawing, a target box). Arbitrary reference images in the Rig Canvas are WP-18.) |
| Design mode: edit the rig without creating animation keys, then return to the main timeline. | Keeps rigging and animation separate. | **data**: `*.puppet.json (rig)`, `shots.json (animation)` (The rig and the animation are separate files by design, so rig edits never create keys.) |
| Stereo views (side by side, over-under, anaglyph) for 3D output. | Used for stereoscopic deliveries. | **n/a** (Stereo is outside the delivery targets (social video and web). Cinema-style packages are WP-37 if ever needed.) |
| Grid display, curve display and fading unselected layers. | Gives drawing and editing clarity. | **n/a** (These are editor display aids. Grid snapping is tracked under moho-draw-menu-1.) |

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Blend morphs window: mix several stored shape morphs with sliders. | Used for facial expressions and corrective shapes blended together. | **gap**: WP-43 (There are no point morphs. Morphs between drawings and views are WP-43. Controls blend only angles, shifts, swaps and z.) |
| Keyframe window: inspect and set a selected key's interpolation and its parameters. | Gives precise easing control. | **gap**: WP-19 (Per-key interpolation editing comes with the curve editor in WP-19.) |
| Switch selection window: choose which drawing a switch shows at the current frame. | Used for quick swap keying. | **data**: acting timeline poses (mouth, eyes, brows, hand_R_pose) (Swap shapes are forced by name in poses and clips. acting.direct sets them from prose.) |
| Docking layouts and toggling secondary windows. | Lets each artist arrange the workspace. | **n/a** (This is a GUI layout concern.) |

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |

| Manual, tutorials and video tutorials one click away. | Speeds up onboarding. | **command**: `engine.commands`, `docs.check` (Commands list their own schemas. docs.check keeps the plan, feature map and registry in agreement.) |
| Show autosave files for crash recovery. | Protects work after a crash. | **n/a** (There is no long-lived editor session to lose. Every edit is a file write through film.write or timeline.edit, kept in git.) |
| Update checks, licence deactivation and refresh, the welcome screen, buying content, and About. | Handles product upkeep. | **n/a** (Doodleberry is in-house with no licensing or store.) |

| Mesh warp (smart warp) layers: a triangulated mesh, posed by points or bones, bends any image or vector layer, and meshes can nest. | Gives organic squash, head turns and painted-art deformation. | **partial**: `puppet.from_blend`, WP-34 (Skinned mesh strokes deform raster textures by part weights (SVG only, WP-14). There is no animatable mesh-point warp on arbitrary layers (WP-34 deformers).) |
| Quad and perspective warp (13.5): a four-point mesh puts flat art into true perspective. | Used for moving backgrounds, floors and signs in perspective. | **gap**: WP-36 (2D layers in depth are in the multiplane work, WP-36.) |

Named passes are WP-37.) |
