## Foundry Nuke (Nuke 16.0/16.1 and 17.0 (online help read at 17.1v2; covers features introduced from 15.x on))

Sources: [Nuke Online Help: User Guide (landing page)](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/user_guide.html); [Nuke Online Help: Compositing with Nuke (17.1v2)](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/comp_environment/nuke/nuke_intro.html); [Nuke Online Help: Advanced Compositing (NukeX)](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/comp_environment/nukex/nukex_intro.html); [Nuke Online Help: Timeline Editing in Nuke Studio and Hiero](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/timeline_environment/preface/nukestudio_timeline.html); [Nuke Online Help: Nuke Studio Environments](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/getting_started/using_interface/nuke_studio_environments.html); [Nuke Online Help: Managing Scripts](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/getting_started/managing_scripts/managing_scripts.html); [Nuke Online Help: Channels](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/comp_environment/channels/channels.html); [Nuke Online Help: Merging Images](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/comp_environment/merging/merges.html)

### Interface, Node Graph and workspaces (Nuke Studio environments)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Node-based compositing: every image operation is a node, and the wired-up graph is the complete, re-renderable recipe for a shot. | Artists read a shot as a flow from plates to output and can bypass, swap or branch any step without destroying earlier work. | **gap**: WP-36 (Doodleberry's recipe is film data (shots.json) run through fixed passes (player, post, captions, mix); there is no user-editable image-processing graph. WP-36 adds a per-shot node compositor.) |
| Graph editing with live inspection: connect a viewer to any node to see that step's output. | Leads debug a comp by stepping through the tree and seeing what each node contributes. | **gap**: WP-20 (WP-20 adds the graph view of facts, script, shots and renders with per-node cache state; WP-36 makes it the compositor's editor.) |
| Properties panel for every node's parameters, with keyframing and a Curve Editor and Dope Sheet for animation timing. | Compositors animate any control (a grade, a transform) and refine its easing and timing on curves. | **partial**: `timeline.get`, `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (The Studio timeline shows and edits clip layers, emotion and gaze keys; a curve (graph) editor and a camera-keys lane are WP-19.) |
| Viewer with channel isolation, A/B wipe comparison, region of interest, zoom and frame scrubbing. | Checking an alpha, comparing a version against the last one, or focusing on one area of a heavy frame. | **partial**: `film.preview`, `shot.render`, WP-19 (film.preview gives a scrubbable live player; shot.render with a renderer override writes side-by-side files. Channel views and a visual diff between two versions are WP-19.) |
| Customisable, saveable workspaces and preferences (panel layouts per task). | Separate layouts for roto, tracking or review, shared as studio defaults. | **n/a** (Doodleberry is agent-first: commands are the interface (CLI, HTTP, Studio, MCP) and Studio pages are fixed; window layout presets have no counterpart worth building.) |
| Compositing and editorial environments in one application, sharing one project (Nuke Studio). | An editor and a comp artist move between the cut and a shot's comp without exporting. | **partial**: `timeline.get`, `timeline.edit`, `film.cuts`, WP-19 (The Studio timeline edits shots.json directly and film.cuts assembles cuts from edit.json; dragging shots and lines in the timeline is WP-19.) |

### Managing scripts (project settings and proxies)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Project settings that fix the frame range, frame rate and full-size output format for the whole script. | The delivery spec is locked at the start so every node and render agrees on size and timing. | **data**: `shots.json size`, `shots.json fps`, `shots.json shots[].start/end` (Size and fps are film fields; the QA bar (qa.run) then measures 1080x1920 at 30 fps on the output.) |
| Scripts saved as plain-text files that can be versioned (v001, v002) and read or written by pipeline tools. | Artists version up for each review; pipelines generate or patch scripts as text. | **command**: `film.create`, `film.write`, `film.get` (Film data is JSON text in git, validated on film.write; versions are git commits and per-film branches (prod.launch).) |
| Proxy mode: work and render at a reduced format or scale, then switch back to full resolution with shapes and tracks still in place. | Fast interaction on heavy plates; the final render is done at full resolution. | **partial**: `film.preview`, `film.animatic`, WP-15 (Vector frames are resolution independent and the player preview is scaled live; there is no low-resolution draft render switch. Render-speed work (render only unique stepped frames, static-layer cache) is WP-15.) |
| Separate pre-made low-resolution proxy files per Read, chosen automatically. | Offline/online workflows on large footage. | **n/a** (No heavy plate footage: sets and characters are drawn from data at render time.) |
| A project directory and relative paths so scripts move between machines and the farm. | Shots are handed to other artists or render nodes without broken file paths. | **data**: films/<name>/ folder layout, `shots.json shots[].cast[].puppet (repo-relative)` (Every film is a self-contained folder with repo-relative references; production sessions work in git worktrees.) |
| Enlarging a script: render the same comp at a larger output format. | Up-res deliverables (HD to UHD) without rebuilding the comp. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-37 (Vector rendering makes any size possible by changing shots.json size, and film.render re-stages aspects; a 4K master as a delivery profile is WP-37.) |

### Reading and writing images; working with file metadata

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Read image sequences and movies in many formats (EXR, DPX, TIFF, PNG, JPEG, QuickTime, MXF, NotchLC) with a colourspace per file. | Bringing plates, CG renders and stock elements into a comp. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, `puppet.from_ink`, `doodle.snap`, `puppet.from_blend`, WP-36, WP-37 (Images enter as puppets (layered drawings, scans, photos, Blender rigs) or set background plates; a generic footage layer in a shot is WP-36 and EXR/OpenImageIO reading is WP-37.) |
| Write renders as image sequences or movies with codec, bit depth, compression, alpha and colourspace options. | EXR to grading, ProRes to editorial, H.264 for review. | **partial**: `film.render`, `shot.render`, `export.web`, WP-23, WP-37 (Output is H.264 MP4 by platform profile (and Lottie, Synfig, web player exports); ProRes/WebM with alpha, GIF and APNG are WP-23, EXR masters WP-37.) |
| Multi-layer EXR input and output carrying many named passes in one file. | CG lighters deliver one EXR per frame with all AOVs; comps write multi-pass masters. | **command**: `film.master` (film.master writes multi-layer EXRs (beauty, line, fill, matte) per frame, read by OpenImageIO and ffmpeg.) |
| Inspect, edit and copy file metadata (timecode, camera and lens data, colour info) and pass it downstream. | Driving a comp from camera metadata, stamping timecode, tracking where an element came from. | **partial**: `film.provenance`, `film.credits`, `film.get`, WP-26 (Doodleberry records what shaped a film (provenance.json, licence classes, credits) at film level, not per-frame image metadata; C2PA content credentials on finals are WP-26.) |
| Timecode and frame-number offsets on writes to match editorial. | Renders slot straight into the editor's timeline. | **partial**: `film.handoff`, WP-22 (Hand-off timelines and EDLs carry the film's frame rate and timecode from zero; offsets to match an editorial start are WP-22.) |
| Burned-in slates and shot information (text driven by metadata) for dailies. | Every review clip shows shot, version, frame and artist. | **partial**: `film.storyboard`, `critic.pack`, WP-19 (Storyboard contact sheets and critic packets carry shot notes and times beside stills; a burn-in on review video belongs with the review room (WP-19).) |

### Reformatting elements

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Resize an element to another format (fit width, fit height, fill or distort) with a choice of resampling filter. | Conforming plates and elements to the project's delivery format. | **command**: `film.render` (film.render with an aspect (16:9, 9:16, 1:1) re-stages characters and camera from the data instead of scaling or cropping; arbitrary sizes come from shots.json size.) |
| Crop and bounding-box control, including letterboxing. | Limiting processing to the useful region and adding matte bars. | **n/a** (Bounding boxes are a raster-engine optimisation; Doodleberry never letterboxes by design (native dual-aspect composition).) |
| Pixel aspect ratio handling for anamorphic and non-square formats. | Working on squeezed anamorphic plates. | **n/a** (Square-pixel social and web formats only.) |
| Named format presets and custom formats. | Studio-wide delivery formats chosen from a list. | **data**: `shots.json size`, `shots.json aspects`, `dbe/profiles.json` (Aspect blocks and per-platform encoding profiles are data.) |
| Viewer guides for title-safe and action-safe areas. | Keeping text and key action away from screen edges and platform overlays. | **metric**: `qa.run layout checks` (Layout QA measures text inside the safe area and heads cut by the frame; brand.assets also writes safe-area overlays for covers.) |

### Channels

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Arbitrary named channels grouped into layers beyond RGBA (up to 1023 per script), flowing through the tree together. | Carrying render passes, mattes, depth and motion vectors alongside the beauty image. | **partial**: `film.master`, WP-37 (Masters carry named layers (beauty, line, fill, matte); depth, motion vectors and arbitrary channels are WP-37.) |
| Shuffling channels between layers (Shuffle). | Pulling one render pass into RGB to grade or inspect it. | **gap**: WP-36 (Needs a channel-aware compositor (WP-36).) |
| Alpha handling with premultiply and unpremultiply to keep edges clean under colour correction. | Avoiding dark or bright fringes when grading a foreground element. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-36 (Internally Synfig and Grease Pencil characters are rendered with alpha and composited over the set video; user-level premultiplication control comes with the compositor (WP-36).) |
| Copying channels from one stream to another (Copy, ChannelMerge). | Taking a matte from one render and applying it to another. | **gap**: WP-36 (Falls within the named work package's scope.) |
| Limiting any node to chosen channels and feeding a mask channel to it. | Grading only the alpha-masked region or only one pass. | **gap**: WP-36 (Falls within the named work package's scope.) |
| Utility passes such as depth (Z) and motion vectors as channels. | Driving depth of field, fog and motion blur in comp. | **gap**: WP-37 (The bake already knows every layer's position and depth plane, so exact passes are possible; output of passes is WP-37, their use in DoF WP-36.) |

### Merging images

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Layering one image over another with premultiplied alpha (the 'over' operation), with any number of inputs. | The basic operation of every comp: foreground over background. | **data**: `shots.json shots[].cast`, `shots.json shots[].overlays`, `shots.json shots[].underlays` (Layer order is data the SVG player draws; shot.render composites Synfig/Grease Pencil characters over the set video.) |
| A full set of blend operations (screen, multiply, plus, difference, minimum, maximum, overlay, hard light and more). | Adding light effects, combining passes, checking differences between versions. | **gap**: WP-36 (Blend modes are in WP-36's scope; today only ffmpeg overlays are used.) |
| Masked merges and a mix control for partial strength. | Applying an element only inside a matte or at reduced intensity. | **gap**: WP-36 (Falls within the named work package's scope.) |
| Matte-driven blend between two images (Keymix). | Swapping in a fixed region from another version or plate. | **gap**: WP-36 (Falls within the named work package's scope.) |
| Stencil and holdout operations that cut one element with another's alpha. | Hiding a foreground behind a set piece or cutting a shape out of a layer. | **gap**: WP-36 (In pure 2D the z-order of parts and layers handles most occlusion; matte cut-outs need WP-36.) |
| Bounding-box and metadata pass-through choices on merges. | Keeping processing regions small on large comps. | **n/a** (A raster performance concern; vector frames are drawn whole.) |
| Contact sheets: tiling many images or frames into one image. | Reviewing many shots or versions at a glance. | **command**: `qa.run`, `film.storyboard`, `critic.pack` (qa.run writes contact sheets; film.storyboard writes strip.png and contact.pdf; critic.pack includes contact sheets.) |
| Dissolving between two inputs over time. | Soft transitions and fades between shots or states. | **gap**: WP-15 (Shot changes use whoosh cuts; cross-dissolves, match cuts and wipes are WP-15.) |

### Removing noise with Denoise

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Analysing a plate's noise profile and removing noise while keeping detail. | Cleaning plates before keying and comp. | **n/a** (Frames drawn from vector data are noise-free; there are no camera plates.) |
| Temporal denoising that uses neighbouring frames. | Removing noise from low-light footage. | **n/a** (No camera plates.) |
| Denoise-then-regrain: re-adding a consistent grain so all elements share one texture. | Every element in the final comp looks like it came from the same camera. | **data**: `shots.json post.grain` (Film-wide grain with a fixed seed (post finishing); it is uniform rather than matched to a plate.) |

### Keying (ChromaKeyer, Primatte, Keylight, Ultimatte)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Pulling a matte from blue or green screen footage with several keying algorithms. | Separating actors from screens for live-action composites. | **n/a** (No screen footage; characters are generated with exact alpha. (Act it out takes are read for pose only, never composited.)) |
| Spill suppression and edge colour replacement. | Removing green contamination from hair and edges. | **n/a** (No screen footage.) |
| Matte refinement: clipping black and white, separate core and edge mattes, combining with garbage mattes. | Getting a solid core and soft hair detail on the same key. | **n/a** (No screen footage.) |
| Luminance or difference keying of high-contrast material, such as ink on paper. | Lifting drawn or printed elements off their background. | **command**: `puppet.from_ink`, `doodle.snap` (Scans and photos of drawings are flattened (divided by a blurred copy), ink is thresholded to alpha and specks removed.) |
| GPU-accelerated keying that stays interactive on high-resolution plates. | Fast iteration on 4K screen shots. | **n/a** (No screen footage.) |

### Using RotoPaint

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Animated Bezier and B-spline shapes as mattes, with per-point and global feather. | Isolating an element for grading, garbage mattes, articulated roto. | **gap**: WP-36 (Masks are in WP-36's scope; in Doodleberry most mattes could be derived from part shapes rather than drawn.) |
| Motion blur on roto shapes. | Mattes that match the blur of fast-moving elements. | **gap**: WP-36 (Sub-frame motion blur in general is WP-15.) |
| Clone and reveal painting for clean-up (rig removal, dust busting). | Removing rigs, wires and dust from plates. | **n/a** (No photographed plates to clean.) |
| Paint strokes with brushes, erasers, blur and dodge/burn, with per-stroke lifetimes over frames. | Frame-by-frame touch-ups and painted effects. | **gap**: WP-35 (Drawn levels, rough-line cleanup and painted effects are WP-35 (brush engines evaluated in WP-34).) |
| Layer hierarchy of shapes and strokes with per-layer transforms and blend modes. | Organising hundreds of shapes for a character. | **gap**: WP-36 (Falls within the named work package's scope.) |
| Shapes linked to tracking data so mattes follow motion automatically. | A matte that rides on a tracked object with only small corrections. | **gap**: WP-36 (Doodleberry knows every part's transform exactly, so WP-36 masks should attach to parts instead of tracks.) |
| Cutting a still into separate layers with roto shapes. | Preparing a painting or photo for 2.5D moves. | **command**: `doodle.cut`, `puppet.from_ink` (Polygon cuts with overlap seams turn one drawing into separately animated parts.) |

### Tracking and stabilizing (Tracker, PlanarTracker, CameraTracker)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| 2D feature tracking of position, rotation and scale. | Measuring how something moves in footage. | **n/a** (No footage to track; the nearest thing is actout.clip reading body landmarks from reference takes for retargeting.) |
| Match-moving: applying a track so an added element sticks to a moving feature. | Attaching signs, screens or effects to moving objects in a plate. | **data**: `shots.json prop attach keys`, bubble tails (player) (Everything's motion is known from the bake, so props attach to parts and bubble tails follow the speaker without tracking.) |
| Stabilising shaky footage by inverting a track. | Steadying a plate before work, then re-applying the shake. | **gap**: WP-12 (Only relevant to Act it out takes: WP-12 scopes camera-motion compensation from the background.) |
| Four-point corner pinning of an image onto a moving surface. | Screen replacements and sign inserts. | **gap**: WP-36 (Inserting an image onto a moving card in a scene needs WP-36 (or the 2.5D planes in WP-36).) |
| Planar tracking of flat surfaces. | Robust tracks of walls, screens and floors. | **n/a** (No footage.) |
| 3D camera solving from footage, with a point cloud. | Matching a CG camera to a real moving camera. | **n/a** (The virtual camera's keys are exact; nothing to solve.) |
| Adding handheld motion or shake to a locked-off shot. | Making CG or locked shots feel filmed. | **data**: `shots.json shots[].camera`, camera_auto (seeded handheld and shake) (Seeded handheld drift and shake come from the camera grammar, deterministic per frame.) |

### Transforming elements

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| 2D transforms (translate, rotate, scale, skew) of any element, animatable, around a chosen centre. | Positioning and animating elements in the frame. | **data**: `shots.json shots[].cast[].place`, `shots.json shots[].overlays (x, y, size)` (Character placement keys and kit element positions are data; parts transform through the rig.) |
| Reframing the whole picture: pans, zooms and push-ins. | Camera moves on a static or animated frame. | **data**: `shots.json shots[].camera`, camera_auto (Camera keys [t, [cx, cy, zoom]] are folded into the bake for every renderer; camera_auto adds follow, auto-framing and punch-ins.) |
| Motion blur on transforms (shutter and sample controls). | Fast moves that read naturally. | **gap**: WP-15 (Sub-frame motion blur on fast shots is WP-15.) |
| Concatenating successive transforms so the image is filtered only once. | Avoiding softening from stacked transforms. | **n/a** (Vector parts are redrawn, never resampled; camera and placement are already folded into one baked transform.) |
| Placing a flat element on a card in 3D space for perspective moves (Card3D). | Perspective tilts and 2.5D moves on stills. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-36 (scene.js films with a three.js scene exist (e.g. films/screwworm-3d); 2D layers on planes with a real camera are WP-36.) |

### Warping images (GridWarp, SplineWarp, IDistort, LensDistortion, Smart Vectors)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Grid and spline warps to bend and reshape parts of an image. | Subtle shape fixes, cheating a pose, squash and stretch on elements. | **partial**: `shots.json timeline scales`, `puppet format v0.9 by_angle`, WP-36 (Mesh parts deform with bones; per-part squash and stretch and angle-driven drawing substitution exist (v0.9). Grid and spline warps of images are WP-36.) |
| Morphing between two images with matched shapes. | Transformations from one form into another. | **gap**: WP-43 (WP-43 includes morphs between views (front to three-quarter to side).) |
| Distortion driven by vectors, noise or UV maps (IDistort and displacement). | Heat haze, wobble and texture effects. | **data**: line boil (style presets) (Line boil uses cycled smooth displacement fields with per-layer seeds, deterministic per frame.) |
| Removing or adding lens distortion from grids or lines (LensDistortion). | Undistorting plates for tracking and redistorting CG to match. | **n/a** (No real lenses; a stylistic lens distortion, if wanted, would join WP-36.) |
| Propagating paint or textures across a deforming surface with Smart Vectors. | Fixing faces or clothing across many frames from one painted frame. | **n/a** (No photographed surfaces; drawn parts already carry their own art.) |

### Temporal operations, retiming and motion blur (Retime, FrameHold, Kronos, VectorGenerator, MotionBlur)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Retiming: speeding up, slowing down or reversing clips, with frame blending or optical-flow interpolation. | Slow-motion, speed changes to fit an edit. | **data**: `shots.json cast[].timeline.clips[].speed`, `shots.json timewarp` (Frames are pure functions of time, so retimes render exact in-between frames rather than interpolating; the warp works on SVG-player shots only.) |
| Speed ramps: variable speed within a clip from a curve. | Ramping into slow motion on an impact. | **data**: `shots.json timewarp` (Piecewise knots [[cut time, film time], ...]; film.soundoff writes them to hold lines long enough to read.) |
| Freeze frames and holds. | Holding on a moment for emphasis or to fill time. | **data**: `shots.json timewarp` (Holds play the acting slower rather than freezing; the QA bar fails any still stretch over 0.5 s, and camera holds are exact.) |
| Slipping and offsetting clips in time. | Nudging an element's timing against the cut. | **command**: `film.retime`, `timeline.edit` (Times reference lines ('line.start+0.9'), so re-voicing slides everything together; timeline.edit moves clip layers.) |
| Cutting and splicing clips (frame ranges, appending). | Building sequences and trims inside the comp tool. | **command**: `film.cuts`, `film.render` (film.render joins shots; film.cuts assembles cuts from edit.json with cut points on beats and never inside a line.) |
| Generating motion vectors from footage for later use (VectorGenerator). | Feeding motion blur, retimes and Smart Vector tools. | **gap**: WP-15 (Exact motion would come from the bake rather than estimation; its consumer, sub-frame motion blur, is WP-15.) |
| Vector-based motion blur added in post (MotionBlur, Kronos). | Adding realistic blur to CG or fast moves without re-rendering. | **gap**: WP-15 (Sub-frame motion blur on fast shots only is WP-15; cartoon speed lines and prop smear frames exist.) |
| Frame blending and echoes for trails. | Ghosted trails and stylised motion. | **partial**: `shot.render`, `shots.json fx.speed_lines`, `shots.json props smear`, WP-15 (Speed lines trail fast parts, props smear with ghosted multiples and draw motion arcs; frame blending is WP-15.) |
| Frame-rate conversion and stepped timing. | Converting 24 fps material to 30 fps, or animating on twos. | **data**: `shots.json fps`, `shots.json shots[].style.step` (Frames render natively at any fps; style.step 12 animates on twos (stepped frames are deduplicated). Rendering only unique stepped frames is WP-15.) |

| Range-based correction of shadows, midtones and highlights, with saturation and contrast. | Targeted fixes without keying. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-36 (Possible through ffmpeg filters in post.grade, film-wide only.) |
| Curve-based correction: hue against saturation or luminance, and RGB curves. | Shifting one colour range, such as skies or skin. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-36 (ffmpeg curves and hue filters in post.grade; no per-layer curves.) |

| ACES workflows (ACEScg working space; ACES 2.0 in Nuke 17). | Industry-standard colour pipeline for VFX and features. | **command**: `film.master` (ACEScg masters under the ACES 2.1 studio config (OpenColorIO 2.6).) |

| CDL import and export for grading round trips. | Matching the on-set or editorial grade in comp. | **gap**: WP-37 (Falls within the named work package's scope.) |
| Scopes: histogram, waveform and vectorscope. | Measuring levels and colour objectively instead of by eye. | **command**: `image.scopes` (image.scopes draws an RGB histogram, a luma waveform and a vectorscope for a frame, with clipping figures.) |
| Automatic colour matching of one shot to a reference (MatchGrade, NukeX). | Matching inserts and pick-ups to the hero shot. | **gap**: WP-37 (Colour is palette data so shots already agree; a colour script and grades per scene are WP-37, palette variants WP-16.) |

Kinetic typography is WP-15, letter-by-letter typewriter WP-17.) |

### Previews and rendering (Viewer, Flipbook, Write, background and farm rendering)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Flipbook playback of a frame range in real time, with audio. | Checking motion at speed before a full render. | **command**: `film.preview`, `film.animatic`, `export.web` (The player renders every frame live as a pure function of time.) |
| Viewer capture: quick image sequences for review. | Sending stills to a supervisor. | **command**: `film.storyboard`, `critic.pack` (Stills per shot or every few seconds from the final encode.) |
| Rendering one, several or all Write nodes over a frame range. | Rendering only the outputs needed now. | **command**: `film.render`, `shot.render` (film.render takes 'only' to force a list of shots; shot.render renders one.) |
| Multi-process and background rendering (frame server). | Keeping the interface free while using all cores. | **command**: `film.render` (One browser per worker on contiguous frame ranges, with repeated frames linked instead of captured; jobs can run async over HTTP.) |
| Command-line rendering without the interface. | Batch renders in pipelines. | **command**: `film.render`, `shot.render` (Every command runs from python -m dbe, HTTP and MCP.) |
| Render farm submission and distributed rendering. | Rendering many shots overnight on many machines. | **gap**: WP-38 (Distributed workers with a shared content-hash cache are WP-38.) |
| Disk caching and localising inputs to speed up re-renders. | Avoiding repeated work on heavy upstream branches. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-15 (Shots re-render only when their content hash changes; the static-layer cache is WP-15.) |
| 32-bit float linear processing. | Keeping full dynamic range through the comp. | **gap**: WP-37 (Falls within the named work package's scope.) |
| Output to an external broadcast monitor. | Colour-critical viewing at the correct pixel aspect. | **n/a** (Delivery targets phone and web screens; review on a phone is WP-19.) |

### Organizing scripts (Backdrops, Groups, Precomps, LiveGroups, Gizmos)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Backdrops and notes to label regions of the graph. | Making a large comp readable for the next artist. | **gap**: WP-20 (There is no graph to annotate yet; the graph view is WP-20. Frame-pinned notes exist (review.add).) |
| Groups that collapse a subgraph into one node. | Hiding complexity and reusing setups. | **gap**: WP-36 (Falls within the named work package's scope.) |
| Precomps: render a branch to disk and read the result back. | Freezing finished parts of a heavy comp. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-15 (Shot renders are reused while their hash is unchanged; per-layer caching (static layers) is WP-15.) |
| LiveGroups: externally stored subgraphs shared by many scripts. | One shared setup updated for every shot that uses it. | **data**: `cast library (cast/index.json)`, `shots.json shots[].cast[].puppet` (Puppets, clips and sets live once in the library and are referenced by every film; cast.add records provenance for every change.) |
| Gizmos: packaged tools with a reduced set of exposed controls. | Studio tools distributed to every artist. | **data**: `shots.json shots[].overlays (kit elements)`, `shots.json shots[].set_opts` (Kit elements and sets are code with a few exposed options; new tools become @command functions.) |
| Toolsets and template setups. | Starting new shots from a standard setup. | **command**: `film.create`, `film.from_template` (Falls within the named work package's scope.) |

### Configuring Nuke (command line, environment, plug-ins, templates)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Command-line options and environment variables. | Controlling Nuke in pipelines and on farm nodes. | **command**: `engine.doctor`, `engine.info` (DBE_* variables (voices, models, Python) are checked by engine.doctor with exact fixes.) |
| Plug-in directories for gizmos, Python, Tcl, OFX and compiled plug-ins. | Extending Nuke with studio and third-party tools. | **partial**: `engine.commands`, WP-34 (New features are @command functions that appear everywhere; hosting OpenFX plug-ins (Natron) is evaluated in WP-34 for WP-36.) |
| Custom menus and toolbars from startup scripts. | Putting studio tools in front of artists. | **command**: `engine.commands` (A registered command appears in the CLI, HTTP API, Studio and MCP at once.) |
| Template scripts and default project settings. | Every new shot starts from the studio standard. | **command**: `film.create`, `film.from_template` (Falls within the named work package's scope.) |
| Custom viewer processes and script LUTs. | Showing artists the look as it will be delivered. | **gap**: WP-37 (Falls within the named work package's scope.) |
| Cross-platform path mapping. | Mixed Windows, Mac and Linux studios. | **n/a** (One Linux environment with repo-relative paths.) |

### Expressions

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Linking one parameter to another node's parameter. | Keeping related values in sync automatically. | **data**: `line-relative times in shots.json`, `shots.json shots[].start_line/end_line` (Times reference lines ('mid.start+0.9', 'catch.end+0.45'), so cuts and moves follow the voice.) |
| Mathematical expressions with the frame number for procedural animation. | Oscillations, offsets and rule-based motion. | **gap**: WP-36 (shots.json has no per-parameter expressions; scene.js films and kit elements are frame-pure code. Should join WP-36 (node parameters).) |
| Per-pixel maths on channels (Expression node). | Custom mattes and image operations without code plug-ins. | **gap**: WP-36 (In-house WebGL nodes are one of WP-36's options.) |
| Several expression languages (Nuke expressions, Tcl, Python) with conversion between them. | Using the language that suits the task. | **n/a** (Data is JSON and logic is Python or JavaScript; nothing to convert between.) |

### The Script Editor, Python and BlinkScript

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A Python API that creates, connects and edits nodes and starts renders. | Pipeline automation and batch fixes across shots. | **command**: `engine.commands`, `film.write`, `timeline.edit` (Every command has an input schema and runs from Python, CLI, HTTP and MCP.) |
| Headless Python sessions and Nuke as a Python module. | Scripts and services that drive Nuke without a screen. | **command**: `engine.commands` (python -m dbe <command> is the headless entry point.) |
| Callbacks before and after creation, saving and rendering. | Pipeline hooks: naming checks, publishing renders. | **gap**: WP-20 (Progress events and webhooks for long jobs are WP-20.) |
| Custom Python panels in the interface. | Studio-specific tools beside the viewer. | **partial**: `engine.commands`, WP-21 (Commands show up in Studio automatically; a chat desk and role subsets are WP-21.) |
| BlinkScript: custom image-processing kernels that run on GPU or CPU inside the comp. | Fast bespoke effects without compiling plug-ins. | **gap**: WP-36 (In-house WebGL nodes are one of WP-36's options.) |
| ParticleBlinkScript: custom particle behaviour. | Bespoke particle motion. | **gap**: WP-42 (Falls within the named work package's scope.) |

### Machine learning tools (CopyCat, BigCat, CatFileCreator, Inference)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Training a network from a few artist-finished frames to repeat the task across a shot (CopyCat). | Clean-up, beauty work or mattes on long shots from a handful of examples. | **gap**: WP-43 (WP-43 brings models in as assistants whose output a person or critic accepts.) |
| Large-scale training across many shots (BigCat, Nuke 17). | Show-wide models trained on whole sequences. | **gap**: WP-43 (Falls within the named work package's scope.) |
| Running pre-trained PyTorch models as nodes (CatFileCreator, Inference). | Using community or in-house models inside the comp. | **partial**: `level.inbetween`, `depth.planes`, WP-43 (MediaPipe pose and face, Whisper, Kokoro, RIFE (in-betweens) and Depth Anything (depth) run behind commands on onnxruntime; segmentation and a general model node are WP-43.) |

### Timeline editing and review in Nuke Studio and Hiero

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Conforming an edit from EDL or XML against media. | Rebuilding the editor's cut for VFX turnover. | **partial**: `film.handoff`, WP-22 (Our cuts go out as EDL and XML that conform against the handed-off media (read back by OTIO, rendered by melt byte for byte); conforming others' edits is WP-22.) |
| Timeline editing tools: ripple, roll, slip, slide, razor and retime. | Trimming and adjusting the cut in the finishing tool. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, `film.cuts`, WP-19 (timeline.edit moves, resizes, adds and deletes clip layers; dragging lines and shots is WP-19.) |
| Real-time effects on timeline clips (grade, transform, text), including masked multichannel effects (Nuke 16). | Quick temp fixes and looks during review. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-36 (Film-wide post finishing only; per-shot effects come with WP-36.) |
| Create Comp: send a timeline shot to a linked comp script and bring renders back as versions. | Round tripping between editorial and comp. | **n/a** (Edit and comp are the same data: shots in shots.json are rendered and joined by film.render, so there is nothing to round-trip.) |
| Annotations drawn on frames during review. | Supervisors mark exactly what to fix. | **partial**: `review.add`, `review.list`, `review.fix`, WP-19 (Text notes are pinned to frames and turned into data edits; drawn annotations and notes about camera and gaze are WP-19.) |
| Sync review: several remote people in one playback session. | Remote dailies. | **gap**: WP-19 (The review room (WP-19) adds review on a phone; shared sessions should join it.) |
| Versions and snapshots of shots, swapped and compared on the timeline. | Choosing between takes and rolling back. | **partial**: `prod.launch`, `shot.render`, WP-19 (Each film session has its own git branch and renderer comparisons can be rendered side by side; a visual diff between two versions is WP-19.) |
| Exporting shots, transcodes and edit decisions for hand-off. | Turnover to vendors and delivery to editorial. | **command**: `film.handoff` (film.handoff packages the shots, stems, captions, camera tracks and the cut in OTIO, FCP 7 XML, FCPXML, EDL, Kdenlive and MLT.) |
| Contact sheet view with pick and tag modes (Nuke 16). | Selecting takes and tagging shots visually. | **command**: `film.storyboard`, `qa.run`, `critic.pack` (Contact sheets are written as files; there is no interactive pick or tag mode (cover.make picks cover frames by score).) |
| Tags on shots and clips for tracking status and notes. | Marking shots as approved, needing work, or omitted. | **partial**: `review.list`, `prod.status`, WP-19 (Note statuses and film states exist; per-shot tags belong with the review room (WP-19).) |

### Plate repair and specialist tools (FurnaceCore, CaraVR, UnrealReader)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Removing flicker from footage (Deflicker). | Fixing timelapse and lighting flicker. | **n/a** (Rendered frames have no exposure flicker; a flash check for viewers' safety is WP-24.) |
| Matching grain to a plate (Regrain). | Making added elements share the plate's grain. | **data**: `shots.json post.grain` (Uniform seeded grain; there is no plate to match.) |
| Wire and rig removal. | Removing stunt rigs from live action. | **n/a** (No live action.) |
| Aligning and steadying shots (Align, Steadiness). | Matching two passes of a camera move. | **n/a** (No live action.) |
| 360-degree VR stitching and review (CaraVR). | VR productions. | **n/a** (Flat social video only.) |
| Pulling live renders from Unreal Engine into the comp (UnrealReader). | Real-time engine elements in finishing. | **gap**: WP-42 (No game-engine link; would join WP-42 (3D layer).) |

## OpenToonz (1.7.1 (manual baseline; 1.7 released 2023); 1.8.0 released 19 June 2026 noted where it adds features. Tahoma2D 1.6 fork noted where relevant.)

Sources: [OpenToonz manual (Read the Docs, latest): table of contents](https://opentoonz.readthedocs.io/en/latest/); [Installing OpenToonz](https://opentoonz.readthedocs.io/en/latest/installing_opentoonz.html); [Using FFmpeg with OpenToonz](https://opentoonz.readthedocs.io/en/latest/using_ffmpeg_with_opentoonz.html); [What's New in OpenToonz (v1.6)](https://opentoonz.readthedocs.io/en/latest/whats_new.html); [Production Workflow](https://opentoonz.readthedocs.io/en/latest/production_workflow.html); [Interface Overview](https://opentoonz.readthedocs.io/en/latest/interface_overview.html); [Managing Projects](https://opentoonz.readthedocs.io/en/latest/managing_projects.html); [Setting Up a Scene](https://opentoonz.readthedocs.io/en/latest/setting_up_a_scene.html)

### Getting started: installing OpenToonz and FFmpeg

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Installers for Windows, macOS, Linux and the BSDs, with the stuffs folder (presets, brushes, profiles) set up on first run. | Studios image the same build onto every workstation so scenes open identically everywhere. | **command**: `engine.doctor` (engine.doctor checks the container (packages, Node, Playwright, ffmpeg, models, voices) and names each fix; tools/setup.sh applies them idempotently. Doodleberry runs in one Linux container, not per-OS installers.) |
| FFmpeg plugged in as an external encoder so the renderer can write MP4, WebM and animated GIF and import those formats. | Artists point OpenToonz at a system FFmpeg to get delivery codecs without extra software. | **command**: `engine.doctor`, `engine.info` (ffmpeg is a required core tool checked by engine.doctor; engine.info reports which renderers and tools are present. Encoding itself happens in film.render.) |
| Reporting the running version and build so plugins, scripts and scenes can be matched to it. | Pipeline TDs pin a version for a whole production and check it before batch jobs. | **command**: `engine.info`, `vendor.check` (engine.info lists renderers and tools; vendor.check records the version of every vendored upstream (UPSTREAM.json).) |

### Production workflow (traditional and paperless)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Paper pipeline: drawings scanned, registered by peg holes, cleaned, inked and painted, then composited and rendered. | Studios with traditional animators keep paper keys and in-betweens and digitise them in batches. | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, `doodle.snap`, WP-35 (Single photos or scans become raster-part puppets in the artist's line; frame-by-frame paper sequences (registration, levels, exposure) are WP-35.) |
| Paperless pre-production: designs and storyboard panels made in the tool before animation starts. | Directors lock the board before layout so every scene's assets are known up front. | **command**: `film.storyboard`, `film.from_template`, `storyboard.check` (Storyboards are generated from shots.json and format templates rather than drawn panel by panel.) |
| Animatic: boards timed against reference audio with rough camera moves. | Editors and directors judge pacing on the animatic before committing to animation. | **command**: `film.animatic` (Storyboard stills held over the film's sound as build/animatic.mp4.) |
| Reusable libraries of modular characters, props, backgrounds, turnarounds and motion cycles. | Series reuse the same rigs and cycles across many episodes to keep cost down. | **command**: `cast.add`, `cast.list`, `cast.generate` (Cast library with provenance; template-level clip library (walk, wave and others) retargets to every character; 16 code-drawn sets.) |
| Layout stage: library assets placed in each scene with rough camera and timing. | Layout artists block scenes so animators start from correct staging. | **data**: `shots.json shots[].cast[].place`, `shots.json shots[].camera`, `shots.json shots[].set`, `shots.json shots[].camera_auto` (Placement keys, sets and camera keys (or automatic coverage) are fields in shots.json.) |
| A colour model fixed early so every character and prop keeps consistent colours through ink and paint. | Colour designers deliver a reference palette that painters must follow. | **partial**: `puppet.lint`, `brand.check`, WP-16 (Puppets use named palette slots and puppet.lint flags raw colours; brand kits are measured; colour models and per-scene palette variants are WP-16.) |

### Interface overview (rooms, panes, appearance)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Rooms: named workspaces, each a saved layout of panes for one task (drawing, timing, painting, rendering). | Artists switch rooms per task instead of rearranging windows. | **n/a** (Doodleberry is agent-native: the interface is the command registry (CLI, HTTP, MCP) plus fixed Studio pages (/timeline, /board, /doodle, /actout, Rig Canvas). Role-specific command subsets for agents are WP-21.) |
| Dockable, floating and lockable panes (viewer, xsheet, timeline, schematic, function editor, palette, level strip). | Leads lock a room layout so the team sees the same tools in the same place. | **n/a** (No user-arranged window layout; Studio pages are fixed views served by dbe/server.py.) |
| Interface themes built from editable stylesheets and a choice of interface language. | Studios ship a house theme and localised UI to international crews. | **n/a** (Single in-house owner; Studio has one theme. Film-content localisation is separate (film.localize).) |
| A command bar of favourite commands and a searchable list of every command. | Power users pin the few operations they repeat all day. | **partial**: `engine.commands`, WP-21 (engine.commands lists every command with its input schema (what agents see as tools); per-role favourites and subsets are WP-21.) |
| History pane with step-by-step undo and redo. | Artists back out of a bad edit several steps without reloading. | **data**: `films/<name>/*.json under git` (Films are plain text, so every change is a git diff that can be reverted; a per-change audit log of who changed what is WP-39.) |
| Record Audio pane that captures a voice or scratch track straight into the scene. | Animators record scratch dialogue to time a scene before the real voice arrives. | **gap**: WP-13 (Human voice-over import with phone-level alignment and fitting into slots is in WP-13; there is no in-app recorder.) |

### Managing projects

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A project root shared by every machine so all artists see the same projects. | Studios mount one shared project root so scenes and assets resolve the same everywhere. | **data**: repository layout: films/, cast/, sets/, brand/, plan/ (The git repository is the project root; production sessions get git worktrees (prod.launch).) |
| Creating a project or scene that inherits settings from an existing one. | Supervisors set up one template project per series and clone it for each episode. | **command**: `film.create`, `film.from_template`, `plan.brief` (film.create makes the folder with empty brief, facts, script, shots and score; film.from_template drafts from a format; channel brand, cast and voices are inherited (WP-32).) |
| Standard folders per project for scans, drawings, extras, outputs, scenes and palettes. | Fixed folder roles let scripts and the farm find files without per-scene paths. | **data**: `films/<name>/{brief.md,facts.json,script.json,shots.json,score.json,build/}` (Each film has fixed source files and a git-ignored build/ folder; cast and sets live in cast/ and sets/.) |
| Project browser listing projects and their scenes. | Coordinators jump between shows and episodes from one list. | **partial**: `prod.status`, `film.get`, WP-20 (prod.status lists every film on the production board and film.get returns one film; a full catalogue/graph view of every film folder fits WP-20.) |
| Per-scene sub-folders driven by path variables. | Large shows keep each scene's renders and drawings apart automatically. | **data**: films/<name>/build/ (Outputs always go under the film's own build/ folder; derived films (aspect, sound-off) get their own folders.) |
| Collect Assets: copy files loaded from outside into the project and repoint the scene, so it can be moved intact. | Before handing a scene to another studio, artists gather every external file into it. | **partial**: `asset.check`, `film.provenance`, WP-33 (Every tracked third-party file must have a manifest row and provenance is recorded; external rigs stay out of git by design, so a 'gather into a portable bundle' step would extend WP-33's vendoring.) |

### Setting up a scene

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Scene settings that start from project defaults, with the option to save a scene's settings back as the new default. | Supervisors tune one scene and promote its settings to the whole show. | **data**: `shots.json size`, `shots.json fps`, `plan/channels/<id>.json brand` (Size and fps per film; brand kit, cast and voices inherited from the channel (WP-32).) |
| Working units: inches, millimetres, centimetres, animation fields or pixels. | Paper animators lay out in fields to match their peg-bar paper. | **n/a** (Doodleberry works in pixels on fixed artboards (puppets in artboard units, frames 1080x1920); field units are a paper convention.) |
| Scene frame rate, plus rendering at another rate by stretching. | The same scene is delivered at 24, 25 or 30 fps for cinema, PAL or NTSC. | **partial**: `film.master`, WP-37 (fps is set per film and masters render at any rate (frames are functions of time); stretching footage is WP-37.) |
| Camera definition: frame size, aspect ratio, pixel resolution and DPI with locks, and saved resolution presets. | Layout sets the camera once per show so every scene renders to spec. | **command**: `film.render` (shots.json size sets the frame; film.render with aspect 16:9 or 1:1 re-stages the film natively (not a crop), with per-aspect overrides in shots.json aspects.) |
| Viewer colour calibration through a 3D look-up table for a target colour space. | Colourists preview on calibrated monitors to match broadcast or cinema targets. | **gap**: WP-37 (A LUT can be applied to the output (shots.json post.lut), but display calibration and colour management (OpenColorIO, ACES) are WP-37.) |
| Limits on undo memory and raster memory to keep long sessions stable. | Artists on heavy raster scenes cap memory to avoid crashes. | **n/a** (Renders are headless, frame-pure and per shot; there is no long interactive editing session to tune.) |

### Scanning paper drawings

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Scanning through supported USB or TWAIN scanners, with an automatic sheet feeder for batches. | Paper departments feed whole scenes of drawings through a scanner in one pass. | **n/a** (No scanner hardware; drawings arrive as photos or scan files (phone-first flow, doodle.snap, puppet.from_ink).) |
| Scan modes: black-and-white threshold, greyscale or colour lines. | Colour mode keeps blue/red construction or shadow lines separate for cleanup. | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, WP-35 (puppet.from_ink flattens lighting and thresholds ink to alpha, keeping texture; separating coloured line types across a sequence is WP-35.) |
| Peg-hole registration so every scanned drawing lines up for autocentering. | Without registration, scanned in-betweens jitter against each other. | **gap**: WP-35 (Only single drawings are imported today; registering frame sequences belongs with drawn levels (WP-35).) |
| Defining which levels and frames to scan (level name, frame count, step), with unscanned frames shown as missing. | Scan operators work from a list that tells them which drawings are still due. | **gap**: WP-35 (Drawing requests (drawing.requests) track missing swap drawings for rigs, not frames of drawn levels.) |
| A crop box limiting the scanned area. | Operators crop to the drawing area to speed scanning. | **command**: `doodle.snap`, `puppet.from_ink` (Photos are turned upright and cropped to the ink automatically; parts are cut by polygons.) |

### Cleaning up scanned drawings

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Autocentering on peg holes, rotation in 90-degree steps and mirroring. | Cleanup aligns every frame before lines are processed. | **partial**: `doodle.snap`, WP-35 (doodle.snap turns a photo upright; registration across a sequence is WP-35.) |
| A cleanup camera that crops and resizes drawings to a target field and resolution. | All drawings land at one consistent scale and centre. | **partial**: `doodle.snap`, `puppet.from_ink`, WP-35 (Single drawings are scaled to the artboard; sequence-wide normalisation is WP-35.) |
| Line processing for greyscale lines: sharpness, despeckling, antialiasing (including morphological), thickness and contrast. | Turns pencil scans into clean, paintable lines in bulk. | **command**: `puppet.from_ink` (Lighting flattened by dividing by a blurred copy, ink thresholded to alpha, specks removed; line-weight-preserving squash for blinks.) |
| Colour line separation: up to several line colours detected and mapped to output styles. | Shadow and highlight lines drawn in coloured pencil become their own styles. | **gap**: WP-35 (Colour styles applied across frames are in WP-35's scope.) |
| Cleanup palette created automatically from the detected line colours. | Painters start from a palette that already matches the cleaned lines. | **gap**: WP-35 (Palette slots are created on vector import (puppet.import) but not from cleaned raster lines.) |
| Live cleanup preview, a camera test of the crop, and an opacity check that colours solid, antialiased and removed pixels. | Supervisors tune cleanup settings on a few frames before batching a scene. | **partial**: `doodle.snap`, `puppet.coach`, WP-18 (/doodle shows suggested cuts and pivots for confirmation and puppet.coach returns an annotated image; a live cleanup-settings preview fits WP-18.) |
| Batch cleanup of whole scenes as background tasks with priorities and dependencies. | Overnight cleanup of every scanned scene. | **gap**: WP-35 (Commands run as async jobs, but sequence cleanup itself does not exist yet.) |
| Cleanup settings saved as reusable files and linked to a level. | One tuned setting is reused for every scene by the same animator. | **data**: `puppet.from_ink spec (JSON)` (The import spec (polygons, pivots, swaps and thresholds) is a reusable JSON file.) |

### Drawing animation levels

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Three drawing types: resolution-free vector levels, palette-indexed raster line levels and full-colour raster levels. | Teams pick vector for clean cartoon lines and raster for textured or scanned work. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, `puppet.from_ink`, WP-35 (Puppet parts are vector strokes, raster parts or mesh strokes; drawn levels (sequences of drawings with exposures) are a new part type in WP-35.) |
| Brush with pressure, size range, smoothing, stabilising and snapping. | Animators get confident lines from a tablet without wobble. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (The Rig Canvas has pressure ink (perfect-freehand) and simplification; stabiliser and textured brushes are WP-18 and WP-34.) |
| Geometric tool for rectangles, ellipses, polygons, polylines and arcs, closing and filling shapes automatically. | Props and backgrounds are built quickly from clean primitives. | **gap**: WP-18 (Sets and props are drawn in code (sets/*.js); the Rig Canvas has no shape tools.) |
| Text tool that turns typed text into editable vector outlines. | Signs and lettering in the drawing itself, not as overlays. | **data**: `shots.json overlays[] (sign, text, title, paper)` (On-screen text is live-rendered from OFL fonts in the same ink; it is not converted into editable drawing outlines.) |
| Vector reshaping tools: cut, join, pinch, pump thickness, magnet, bend and smooth strokes. | Cleanup artists fix lines without redrawing them. | **gap**: WP-18 (Polygon editing of cuts is planned in WP-18; vector deformer code (Synfig core, Inkscape) is evaluated in WP-34.) |
| Selection with move, rotate, scale and distort, by stroke, region or matching style. | Artists rescale or reposition part of a drawing between frames. | **gap**: WP-18 (Lasso and transform are listed in the feature map under WP-18 and WP-34.) |
| Converting raster drawings to vectors (centre-line strokes or outline fills). | Scanned roughs become editable vector levels. | **gap**: WP-18 (Vector tracing (VTracer, potrace) of scans is WP-18; today scans stay raster parts.) |
| Key, breakdown and in-between drawing workflow with drawings numbered automatically from the timeline. | Leads draw keys, assistants fill breakdowns and in-betweens. | **gap**: WP-35 (Keys with in-between assistance on drawn levels are WP-35 (proposals from WP-43).) |
| Automatic in-betweens between two vector extremes with linear or eased spacing. | Saves assistants' time on simple moves and holds. | **command**: `level.inbetween`, `level.accept` (level.inbetween proposes in-betweens between drawn drawings (RIFE 4.9, measured: 92% accepted against 58% for holds on held-out true drawings) and level.accept takes them into the exposure sheet. Vector extremes with eased spacing are WP-43.) |
| Rotoscoping by drawing over imported video or image sequences. | Reference footage gives realistic timing for complex actions. | **partial**: `actout.clip`, WP-35 (actout.clip turns a filmed take into motion on a rig (not traced drawings); drawing over footage needs drawn levels (WP-35).) |
| Onion skin in relative and fixed modes, tinted for earlier and later frames, per level or whole scene. | Essential for judging spacing while drawing frame by frame. | **gap**: WP-35 (The Rig Canvas shows the character's rest pose faintly behind the part being drawn; onion skins over time are WP-35.) |
| Shift and trace: a light table showing neighbouring keys that can be offset, rotated or placed along a path. | In-betweeners line up arcs before tracing the middle drawing. | **gap**: WP-35 |
| Viewer guides: field guide, safe areas and custom guides. | Layout keeps important action and text inside broadcast-safe areas. | **metric**: `qa.run` (qa.run measures every word's box against the platform-safe area per aspect; brand.assets writes safe-area overlays.) |

### Editing animation levels

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Level strip listing every drawing in a level, including unexposed ones, with insert, renumber and expose. | Animators manage a level's drawings apart from their timing. | **gap**: WP-35 |
| Sequence operations on drawings: reverse, swing, repeat, thin out, duplicate and merge. | Cycles and holds are built from existing drawings without redrawing. | **gap**: WP-35 (Clips can be reversed in speed or looped on rigs, but not drawing sequences.) |
| Merging several columns of drawings into one flattened level, and merging painted raster levels. | Simplifies scenes and prepares levels for hand-off. | **gap**: WP-35 |
| Level processing: brightness and contrast, colour fade, antialias, binarise, adjust levels and vector line thickness. | Batch fixes to whole levels, especially scans, before painting. | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, WP-35 (Thresholding and speck removal happen on import; whole-level processing across frames is WP-35.) |
| Saving levels with optional backups and saving a copy under a new name without changing the scene. | Artists branch a level safely while the scene keeps its reference. | **data**: `cast/*.puppet.json under git` (Puppets are JSON under git; cast.add records content hashes in cast/provenance.jsonl.) |
| Exporting levels as image sequences or layered files with background, antialias and scale options. | Hand drawings to compositing or another tool. | **partial**: `puppet.export_layers`, `puppet.sheet`, WP-23 (Puppets export as layered SVG/ORA/PSD and model sheets as PNG; print-ready transparent stills are WP-23.) |

### Managing palettes and styles

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Palette per level with indexed, named styles on pages; changing a style recolours every drawing that uses it. | Colour changes late in production update every frame at once. | **data**: puppet palette slots, `shots.json shots[].set_opts` (Puppet colours are palette slots named after the parts they paint; sets recolour through set_opts.) |
| Studio palette: a shared library of production palettes, with styles linked to their source so edits propagate. | Character colour masters live in one place for the whole show. | **partial**: `brand.check`, `plan.channel`, WP-16 (Brand kits are shared and inherited by a channel's films; shared character colour models and palette variants are WP-16.) |
| Animated palettes: style colours keyed over time. | Lighting changes (sunset, a blush) without repainting. | **gap**: WP-16 (Palette variants per scene (night, sick, cold) are WP-16; keying colours over time within a shot would join it.) |
| Style types beyond flat colour: textures, generated patterns, strokes that repeat an image, MyPaint-style raster brushes. | Gives painted, textured or decorative looks on top of clean lines. | **gap**: WP-15 (Line boil, toon shading and grain exist; pencil, marker and crayon line media are WP-15 and brush engines (libmypaint, Krita) WP-34.) |
| Bulk adjustment of many styles at once (hue, saturation, value, alpha, blend between two styles). | Colour stylists derive night or desaturated palettes from a day palette. | **gap**: WP-16 (A grade on the whole picture is possible (shots.json post.grade), but palette-level derivation is WP-16.) |
| Style naming conventions applied through a name editor. | Consistent names let scripts and match tools find the same style across characters. | **command**: `puppet.lint` (Slots follow template names and puppet.lint flags raw colours outside the slots.) |
| Matching a palette to a reference palette within a colour tolerance. | Keeps imported characters consistent with the show's colour model. | **gap**: WP-16 (brand.check measures contrast and colour-blind separation, not palette matching.) |

### Painting animation levels

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Fill tool clicking, or by rectangle, freehand or polyline area, filling areas, lines or both. | Ink-and-paint artists fill whole frames in a few strokes. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (Rig Canvas bucket fills an ink-outlined area with a palette slot; area-select fill modes are WP-18.) |
| Closing gaps in outlines so fills do not leak, with a check view that highlights closable gaps. | Avoids leaks on sketchy or scanned lines. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (The bucket closes gaps up to about 10 px and refuses open outlines; a visual gap check is not there.) |
| Painting across a range of frames, or picking colours from an onion-skinned neighbour frame. | Speeds painting of long sequences with the same regions. | **gap**: WP-35 (Colour styles applied across every frame are WP-35.) |
| Selective fill that skips painted areas, and fill depth through antialiased raster edges. | Repaint only unfinished regions without undoing others. | **gap**: WP-35 |
| Lines that take the colour of the area they border automatically. | Coloured lines in the style of Japanese production without extra painting. | **gap**: WP-35 |
| Painting checks: ink-only, paint-only, transparency and black-background views to find unpainted spots. | Final check before render to catch holes and wrong colours. | **partial**: `puppet.lint`, `puppet.coach`, WP-35 (Rigs are checked for raw colours, joint gaps and missing parts; per-frame paint checks on drawn levels are WP-35.) |
| Match lines: merging lines from one column onto another level (for tone or shadow lines). | Shadow lines drawn separately are joined to the paint level at the end. | **gap**: WP-35 |
| Colour model viewer: a reference drawing or animation to pick styles from while painting. | Painters copy colours straight from the approved model. | **partial**: `puppet.sheet`, WP-16 (puppet.sheet gives a model sheet; colour models as references for painting are WP-16.) |

### Working in the Xsheet/Timeline

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Exposure sheet and layer timeline as two views of the same columns-by-frames grid. | Animators time on the sheet; compositors prefer the layer view. | **partial**: `timeline.get`, `timeline.edit`, WP-35 (The Studio timeline (clips, emotion and gaze keys, dialogue lanes) is clip-based; an exposure-sheet view is WP-35.) |
| Exposing drawings into cells, and replacing a level while keeping its timing. | Swap a rough level for the cleaned one without retiming. | **data**: `shots.json shots[].cast[].puppet` (Clips are template-level, so changing a cast member's puppet keeps all its timing.) |
| Column controls: name, fold, lock, hide from preview or render, opacity and colour filter. | Compositors isolate layers and tint references while working. | **gap**: WP-36 (Per-layer opacity and colour filters belong to the compositor (WP-36); shots can be rendered selectively (film.render only).) |
| Cell timing operations: on twos and threes, each, increase or decrease step, reverse, swing, random, roll, time stretch. | Fast retiming of drawings and holds without redrawing. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-35 (style.step animates on 12s, 8s or 6s; clips have speed, fades and weights; cell-level exposure tools are WP-35.) |
| Inserting or removing frames across the whole scene with keys shifting to match. | Opening up or tightening a scene after an edit note. | **partial**: `film.retime`, `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (Line-relative times reflow everything after a voice change; dragging lines and shots in Studio is WP-19.) |
| Sub-xsheets: nest columns into a reusable sub-scene, open, edit in place, collapse, explode or save separately. | Reusable cycles and complex sections stay manageable. | **partial**: `film.cuts`, WP-36 (film.cuts assembles inserts from other films at edit level; nested sub-compositions inside a shot fit WP-36.) |
| Soundtrack columns: load audio files, trim and move clips, set volume, scrub with the frame cursor. | Dialogue and music are timed against picture in the sheet. | **partial**: `voice.build`, `film.render`, WP-40 (Voices are synthesized and placed on lines and the score is generated from score.json; waveform lanes, trimming and importing music files are WP-40.) |
| Automatic lip sync from audio (Rhubarb), assigning mouth drawings to phonemes in a column. | Gets a first pass of mouth timing in seconds. | **command**: `voice.build`, `lipsync.check`, `doodle.say` (Visemes come from word and phoneme alignment; lipsync.check cross-checks against the voice and Rhubarb.) |
| Importing lip-sync data from Papagayo-NG or Magpie files. | Lip-sync specialists break down dialogue in a dedicated tool. | **gap**: WP-13 (Importing external phoneme timings fits WP-13's recorded voice-over import with phone-level alignment.) |
| Memos pinned to a frame and column. | Directors leave timing notes where the animator will see them. | **command**: `review.add`, `review.list` (Notes pinned to frames; review.fix turns them into data edits.) |
| Save, autosave, revert and rolling backups of scenes. | Protects work against crashes and bad saves. | **data**: films/<name>/ under git (film.write validates every write; history is git.) |
| Importing or exporting scenes between projects with their files copied to the right folders. | Moving scenes between productions or vendors. | **data**: films/<name>/ folder (A film is a self-contained folder of text files referencing cast and sets by path; prod.merge brings a film's branch back into the main tree.) |
| Printing the exposure sheet as HTML or PDF. | Paper sheets go to animators and to overseas studios. | **partial**: `film.storyboard`, `timeline.get`, WP-35 (contact.pdf gives notes per shot; a printable exposure sheet is WP-35.) |

### Creating movements (stage schematic, animating objects, 3D view)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Stage schematic: a node graph of every scene object (columns, pegbars, cameras, table) and their links. | TDs read and fix scene hierarchy at a glance. | **partial**: `timeline.get`, WP-20 (timeline.get returns shots, cast and clip layers as data; a node view is WP-20.) |
| Pegbars and multi-level parenting so children add their own motion to a parent's. | A character rides a vehicle or a group moves as one. | **gap**: WP-11 (Bones parent within a puppet and props switch parents (attach keys), but a scene-level null object grouping characters and props is missing; it fits WP-11's constraints.) |
| Several cameras per scene, each with its own size and animation, one active at a time. | Alternate framings of the same action for editorial choice. | **data**: `shots.json shots[].camera`, `shots.json hooks` (Each shot has its own camera keys; the same action can be shot again as another shot or hook variant.) |
| Camera linked to an object so it follows a character. | Tracking shots without hand-keying the camera. | **data**: `shots.json shots[].camera_auto` (Camera grammar follows the speaker, shakes and punches in as a pure function of time.) |
| Hooks on drawings as attach points and pivots, up to many per level. | Props stay in a hand and feet stay planted in walk cycles. | **data**: puppet anchors (anchor:<name>), `timeline.pins` (Anchors and pivots come from import markers; foot pins hold feet on the ground.) |
| Tracker tool that follows image regions across frames and outputs hooks. | Attach drawn elements to filmed or animated features. | **gap**: WP-36 (Region tracking in footage is missing; it should join WP-36 (compositor), with camera-motion compensation from WP-12.) |
| Animate tool for position, rotation, scale, shear, depth and stacking order keys, per channel or all at once. | Core layout and cut-out animation of whole objects. | **partial**: `shots.json place`, `shots.json timeline z`, WP-36 (Placement keys carry x, y, scale and flip, and per-part layer order is keyed (v0.9); whole-object rotation and shear keys and stacking order across characters are WP-36.) |
| Motion paths: objects move along a drawn path, optionally turning to its direction, with keys snapped to path points. | Flying objects and smooth arcs without many keys. | **partial**: `shots.json place.path`, `shots.json props path`, WP-20 (Characters and props move along drawn paths, turning to face their direction; snapping keys to path points is WP-20.) |
| Keys shown on timeline cells, with easing handles, constant-speed segments and cycles. | Timing tweaks happen right in the sheet. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (Keys use a fixed smooth ease and clips loop; per-segment easing handles and a camera-keys lane are WP-19.) |
| 3D view with depth (Z) for multiplane and truck effects; objects scale with distance. | Classic multiplane depth and parallax on camera moves. | **gap**: WP-36 (True 2D multiplane depth is WP-36 (some sets have parallax depths, and scene.js films can use a three.js renderer).) |
| Camera animation: pan, zoom and rotate. | Pushes, pans and Dutch angles for staging. | **data**: `shots.json shots[].camera` (Camera keys [t, [cx, cy, zoom]] are folded into the bake for every renderer; camera roll is not a field.) |

### Editing using spreadsheet and curves (function editor)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Spreadsheet of per-frame numeric values for every animated parameter. | Precise numeric fixes and copy-paste of values. | **partial**: `timeline.get`, WP-19 (Keys are readable and writable as JSON; a numeric spreadsheet view is part of Studio timeline work (WP-19).) |
| Graph editor of curves with tangent handles, linked to the spreadsheet. | Animators polish spacing and overshoot visually. | **gap**: WP-19 (Curve (graph) editor for keys is WP-19.) |
| Segment interpolation types: constant, linear, speed in/out, ease in/out by frames or percent, exponential. | Each move gets the spacing it needs. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (Keys interpolate with a fixed smooth ease and holds (views, swaps); choosing interpolation per segment is WP-19.) |
| Expressions that compute a parameter from other objects, effects, frame number and functions (wave, random, cycle). | Procedural motion and linked controls without keys. | **data**: puppet controls (driven keys), line-relative times, {{path}} bindings (Driven keys link controls to angles, shifts, swaps and z; times reference lines; text binds to data.json. General formulas on arbitrary parameters are not supported.) |
| Parameter values read from an external data file. | Drive animation from measured or simulated data. | **command**: `film.data` (Analysis scripts write data.json and overlays bind to it (data_from).) |
| Similar-shape curves and editing many curves at once. | Keep secondary channels in step with a primary one. | **gap**: WP-19 |
| Stepped values on curves (hold every n frames). | Match curve motion to drawings on twos. | **data**: `shots.json shots[].style.step` (Stepped timing per shot on 12s, 8s or 6s.) |
| Saving and loading curves as files. | Reuse motion between scenes. | **data**: clip library JSON, puppet poses and clips (Clips and poses are JSON and travel with the puppet.) |

### Creating cut-out animation (skeleton tool)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Building a skeleton by linking part columns and setting their pivots. | Riggers turn a model sheet into an animatable puppet. | **command**: `puppet.import`, `cast.generate`, `puppet.save` (Layered SVG/PSD/ORA by layer name with pivots from markers or estimated; generated humanoids; Rig Canvas saves through puppet.save.) |
| Hook-based joints so parts stay attached even when a part is a bending animated drawing. | No gaps at elbows and knees during extreme poses. | **command**: `puppet.coach` (Bendy limbs and mesh strokes deform at joints; puppet.coach bends each joint by 50 degrees and measures any gap.) |
| Switching a part's drawing from within the rig (drawing substitution). | Hands, mouths and turned heads changed per pose. | **data**: puppet swaps, puppet controls (Swap sets driven by clips or controls; drawing.requests asks for missing drawings.) |
| Forward kinematics posing that keys automatically, with global keys for scale and shear. | Fast pose-to-pose cut-out animation. | **command**: `timeline.edit`, `puppet.save` (Clips are placed on the timeline; poses made in the Rig Canvas travel with the puppet.) |
| Inverse kinematics with permanent and temporary pins. | Plant feet or a hand on a table while the body moves. | **data**: `shots.json timeline.ik`, `shots.json timeline.pins` (Two-bone IK, FABRIK chains and foot pins; IK/FK blending is WP-11.) |
| Showing only the active skeleton and resetting centres and pins. | Keeps complex rigs readable while posing. | **n/a** (Viewer convenience for manual posing; agents edit data directly.) |

### Animating with the Plastic tool (mesh deformation)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Generating a triangle mesh from a drawing with set density and margin. | Deform painted or complex drawings smoothly instead of cutting them into parts. | **partial**: `puppet.from_blend`, WP-18 (Mesh strokes with skinning come from Blender cut-out rigs; meshing an arbitrary drawing inside Doodleberry would join WP-18.) |
| Editing the mesh: move points, swap, collapse, split or cut edges. | Fix bad deformation around eyes or mouths. | **gap**: WP-18 |
| A vertex skeleton inside the mesh that bends it. | Organic bends in tails, cloth and bodies. | **data**: `puppet mesh strokes (v0.5)`, `puppet parts[].bend` (Bones with skinning weights and bendy limbs; SVG renderer only, others in WP-14.) |
| Several skeletons on one mesh, switched at chosen frames. | Turnarounds and big shape changes on one drawing. | **data**: puppet views, `shots.json timeline.view` (Multi-view rigs switch views over time; a turn control drives three-quarter offsets.) |
| Per-vertex angle limits and no-stretch locks. | Prevent impossible poses. | **data**: `puppet parts[].limits` (Joint limits clamp after clips, IK, pins, chains and springs.) |
| Painting rigid and flexible zones on the mesh. | Keep faces or hands rigid while limbs bend. | **gap**: WP-18 (Weights come from the Blender source rig; painting them in the Rig Canvas would join WP-18.) |
| Parenting other levels to skeleton vertices or hooks. | Props and accessories ride a deforming body. | **data**: prop attach keys, kit face attach {cast, part, at} (Props and faces attach to parts.) |
| Vertex values in the function editor and in expressions (for example mirrored shoulders). | Fine control and linked motion on deformation rigs. | **data**: puppet controls (driven keys) (No curve editor yet (WP-19).) |
| Applying a mesh to a sub-xsheet so a group of layers deforms together. | Deform a whole assembled character as one. | **gap**: WP-36 (Deforming a composite group is missing; it should join WP-36 (compositor), with renderer support via WP-14.) |

|---|---|---|
| FX schematic: a node graph connecting columns and effects to output nodes, with several outputs and one active. | Compositors build and reuse per-scene effect trees. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-36 (Finishing is a fixed ffmpeg chain (shots.json post) plus motion effects (fx); a node compositor is WP-36.) |
| Inserting, adding or replacing effects on links and toggling each on or off for rendering. | Quick A/B checks of an effect in context. | **gap**: WP-36 |
| Effect settings panel with preview and keyframed parameters. | Effects that change over a shot, like a growing glow. | **gap**: WP-36 (post settings are static per shot.) |
| On-screen handles for effect positions and sizes. | Place a light or blur centre directly in the frame. | **n/a** (Parameters are data set by agents; a visual editor for the compositor is WP-36 via the WP-20 graph view.) |
| Effect presets and macro effects combining several nodes. | Studios standardise looks across scenes. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-36 (Finishing presets (crisp, film, warm, cool, paper, noir) exist; user macros of nodes are WP-36.) |
| Blur, glow and light effects (including bokeh and radial or spin blur). | Atmosphere, magic and depth cues. | **gap**: WP-36 (Note the generic-AI-look lint (WP-04) flags blur glows, so any glow must be deliberate.) |
| Image adjustment and colour correction effects. | Grade scenes for mood and continuity. | **data**: `shots.json post (grade, lut, vignette, grain)` (Applied to the joined picture before captions; grain has a fixed seed.) |
| Layer blending modes (add, multiply, screen, lighten and others). | Shadows, light passes and glows composited over paint. | **gap**: WP-36 |
| Mattes and masks (matte in/out, erode or dilate). | Restrict effects or reveal elements inside shapes. | **gap**: WP-36 |
| Distortion and noise effects (warp, ripple, fractal noise, random wave). | Heat haze, water and handmade wobble. | **data**: `shots.json shots[].style.boil` (Deterministic line boil (turbulent displacement with cycled seeds); general distortion nodes are WP-36.) |
| Motion blur computed from the movement of a layer. | Smooth fast action at film frame rates. | **gap**: WP-15 (Sub-frame motion blur on fast shots is WP-15.) |
| Line effects for drawn levels: art contour patterns, calligraphic thickness, outlines, palette filters, external palettes. | Stylise lines and isolate colours for compositing. | **gap**: WP-15 (Medium simulation for vector lines is WP-15; toon outlines and rims exist (style.toon).) |
| Generated backgrounds, gradients and colour cards. | Quick backgrounds and fills behind animation. | **data**: `shots.json shots[].set`, `shots.json shots[].underlays` (Sixteen code-drawn sets and underlay elements.) |
| GLSL shader effects and plug-in effects. | Studios add custom looks without patching the app. | **gap**: WP-36 (In-house WebGL nodes are one of WP-36's options.) |
| Caching effect results to speed previews. | Iterate on top layers without recomputing heavy effects below. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-15 (Shots are re-rendered only when their content hash changes and repeated frames are linked; the static-layer cache is WP-15 and node-level re-cook WP-20.) |

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
