| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Previewing the rendered result in the viewer, framebar showing which frames are done. | Quick checks of effects and timing before a full render. | **command**: `film.preview`, `export.web` (Player page with live scrubbing; web player with play, scrub and frame step.) |
| Separate preview settings (lower resolution, sub-camera region) for faster checks. | Directors review fast low-res previews; final settings stay untouched. | **partial**: `shot.render`, `film.animatic`, WP-20 (Single shots and animatics render quickly; a draft-scale or region option with a time estimate fits WP-20.) |
| Flipbook with snapshot comparison, RGB and alpha channels, histograms and linked playback. | Compare takes and check alpha and levels. | **partial**: `critic.pack`, `qa.run`, WP-19 (Contact sheets and stills are generated; a visual diff between two versions is WP-19.) |
| Output to image sequences and movies (PNG, TIF, TGA, MP4, WebM, GIF, MOV, APNG, sprite sheets). | Deliverables for editorial, web and games. | **partial**: `film.render`, `export.web`, `export.lottie`, WP-23 (MP4 (H.264/AAC), web player and Lottie exist; WebM and ProRes with alpha, GIF and APNG are WP-23, sprite sheets WP-30.) |
| High bit depth, floating-point and linear-light rendering with gamma control. | Clean gradients and correct light blending for film delivery. | **gap**: WP-37 |
| Rendering with an alpha channel. | Hand transparent animation to compositing. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-23 (Synfig and Grease Pencil render characters with alpha internally before compositing; alpha deliverables are WP-23.) |
| Multiple rendering: separate outputs per column or FX branch. | Passes for later compositing (line, paint, shadow). | **command**: `film.master` (film.master renders separate passes (beauty, line, fill, matte) as layers of each EXR.) |
| Stereoscopic output with a camera offset. | 3D cinema and VR releases. | **gap**: WP-37 (Not planned; it should join WP-37 as a delivery profile.) |
| Frame range and step settings for partial renders. | Re-render only a fixed section or every nth frame for tests. | **partial**: `film.render`, `shot.render`, WP-20 (film.render takes only (a set of shots); arbitrary frame ranges would join WP-20's re-cook-only-what-changed work.) |
| Soundtrack merged into movie output. | Picture and sound delivered together for review. | **command**: `film.render` (Voices, score and effects are mixed to -14 LUFS and muxed; stems are kept.) |
| Batch render tasks with priorities and dependencies, saved and reloaded. | Render queues run overnight. | **partial**: `prod.launch`, `prod.status`, WP-38 (Commands run as async jobs and production sessions queue beyond max_parallel; a render queue across workers is WP-38.) |
| Multithreaded, tiled rendering to control memory on heavy scenes. | Large frames render without running out of memory. | **command**: `film.render` (One browser per CPU on frame ranges, frame-hash dedupe of repeats.) |
| Clapperboard (slate) frames added to renders. | Editorial identifies scene, take and version from the first frames. | **command**: `film.master` (Masters start with a slate (title, resolution, fps, frames, colour, passes, OCIO config, version) as an EXR and a PNG.) |

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |

| Headless rendering of a scene file with output path, frame range, step and shrink options (tcomposer). | Farms and scripts render without opening the interface. | **command**: `film.render`, `shot.render` (All rendering is headless; frame range and shrink options are not exposed (see the rendering chapter).) |
| Headless batch cleanup of scans (tcleanup). | Overnight cleanup on servers. | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, WP-35 (Single-drawing ink cleanup only.) |
| Format conversion between image, level and movie formats (tconverter). | Convert deliverables or legacy assets in bulk. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, `puppet.export_layers`, WP-23 |
| Render tasks show the exact command line they will run, so it can be rerun by hand. | Wranglers reproduce and debug farm failures. | **command**: `engine.commands` (Every command has a JSON input schema and the same call works from CLI, HTTP and MCP.) |
| Driving OpenToonz itself from Doodleberry data (a scene rendered by tcomposer). | Reuse OpenToonz's renderer and level model inside an automated pipeline. | **gap**: WP-34 (WP-34's done-when names exactly this; WP-35 builds drawn levels on OpenToonz's level and X-sheet model if chosen.) |

### What's new: release notes 1.6 to 1.8 (and Tahoma2D fork notes)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Preproduction board panel for laying out story panels (1.7; Tahoma2D refactored its own board in 1.6). | Boards and timing live in the same tool as animation. | **command**: `film.storyboard`, `film.animatic` |
| Interchange of layered cel animation through the Open Cel Animation format (export 1.7, import 1.8). | Move drawn animation between Krita, Blender and other cel tools. | **partial**: `puppet.export_layers`, `export.synfig`, `export.lottie`, WP-22 (Rig exports exist (layered files, Synfig, Lottie); OCA in and out would join WP-22's hand-off formats.) |
| Exposure-sheet interchange: XDTS integration (1.8), plus sheet export to PDF and TVPaint JSON (1.6). | Timing sheets exchanged with Japanese studios and other paint tools. | **gap**: WP-35 (Belongs with the exposure sheet in WP-35.) |
| Camera track export (1.7). | Camera moves reused in a compositing or 3D tool. | **command**: `film.handoff` (Each shot's camera per frame is exported as a Nuke .chan (the perspective camera the 3D sets use) and a CSV of the 2D camera.) |
| Navigation tags and coloured cell marks on the timeline (1.6, 1.7). | Jump to story points in long scenes. | **data**: `script.json line ids`, `review.json` (Lines are named anchors that shots and clips reference; review notes are pinned to frames.) |
| Screentone fills (1.8). | Manga-style tone shading. | **gap**: WP-15 (Joins WP-15's medium and style passes.) |
| Stroke replication (symmetry-style drawing) and improved perspective/assistant guides (1.8). | Symmetrical props and correct perspective in backgrounds. | **gap**: WP-18 |
| New effects: Flow effect series and Floor Bump (1.7), Smoother and lazy-brush style effects (1.8). | Painterly flow-driven distortion and line smoothing. | **gap**: WP-36 |
| OpenEXR image support (1.6) and floating-point linear rendering (1.7). | Film-grade intermediates. | **partial**: `film.master`, WP-37 (Half-float linear (ACEScg) EXR output exists; EXR input is WP-37.) |
| Multithreaded FFmpeg encoding (1.6) and faster MP4 renders with audio (1.7). | Shorter turnaround for review movies. | **command**: `film.render` (Parallel capture plus -tune animation encoding.) |
| Pasting images from the system clipboard, custom panels and a see-through main window (1.7, 1.8). | Interactive conveniences for artists. | **n/a** (Desktop UI conveniences; Doodleberry is driven by commands.) |
| Camera capture for stop motion and pencil tests, with camera calibration (1.6); Tahoma2D adds DSLR support through Canon SDK and gphoto. | Capture paper pencil tests or puppets frame by frame. | **gap**: WP-35 (actout.clip uses a webcam for performance capture, not frame capture; stop-motion capture should join WP-35 (drawn levels as image sequences).) |
| Tahoma2D: clipping masks in the timeline and per-column alpha lock (1.6). | Paint inside a character's shape without spilling. | **gap**: WP-36 (Masks and mattes are WP-36.) |

## Synfig Studio (1.4.5 (current stable, 1.4.x line) and 1.5.5 (current development, 1.5.x line); manual at synfig.readthedocs.io/en/latest, read 2026-11-02)

Sources: [Synfig User Manual: contents](https://synfig.readthedocs.io/en/latest/index.html); [Main concepts](https://synfig.readthedocs.io/en/latest/main_concepts.html); [Keyframes](https://synfig.readthedocs.io/en/latest/main_concepts/keyframes.html); [Interface](https://synfig.readthedocs.io/en/latest/ui.html); [Library Panel](https://synfig.readthedocs.io/en/latest/ui/panel_children.html); [Sets Panel](https://synfig.readthedocs.io/en/latest/ui/panel_sets.html); [Canvas](https://synfig.readthedocs.io/en/latest/canvas.html); [Misc](https://synfig.readthedocs.io/en/latest/misc.html)

### Installation and setup

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Ready-to-run packages for Windows, macOS and Linux (an AppImage on Linux), so a workstation is set up in minutes. | Studios image every artist seat with the same build so files open identically everywhere. | **command**: `engine.doctor` (Doodleberry runs in one Linux container; engine.doctor lists what is missing (Synfig among the tools) with the exact fix and tools/setup.sh applies it.) |
| Stable and development builds that can be installed side by side, so new features can be tried without risking production files. | Leads trial a development build on a copy of a shot before the team upgrades. | **n/a** (One pinned toolchain per container; engine.info reports the versions found. A second environment is a second container, not a second install.) |
| Building the application from source (Autotools, CMake from 1.5, vcpkg), with reproducible builds from 1.5.3. | Pipeline teams patch the renderer and ship an in-house build. | **command**: `vendor.build`, `vendor.check` (vendor.build runs a vendored component's upstream build recipe in a copy; vendor.check verifies its upstream record and licence. Synfig core itself is not vendored yet (WP-34 decides drive vs vendor).) |

### Quick start

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A first animation from scratch: draw a few shapes, key them at two times, render a movie. | Onboarding and quick tests of an idea before a real shot is set up. | **command**: `film.create`, `film.from_template`, `film.render` (A film folder from scratch or a format template, rendered with QA in one command; the doodle flow (doodle.snap) gets a talking character in seconds.) |
| A basic cut-out rig with bones on separate drawn parts. | The standard way to make a reusable character for a series. | **command**: `puppet.import`, `cast.generate`, `doodle.snap` (Layered drawings become rigged puppets by layer name; cast.generate makes an original rigged humanoid.) |
| Basic masking: show one piece of artwork only inside another shape. | Mouths inside heads, reveals, wipes and eye-in-socket tricks. | **gap**: WP-36 (No general mask in puppet or shot data (only internal clipping in toon shading and mesh triangles); masks and mattes belong to the compositor WP.) |

### Interface and panels

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Dockable panels with workspace layouts that can be saved and switched (1.4). | Artists keep separate layouts for drawing, rigging and timing. | **n/a** (Agent-native: the command registry (engine.commands) is the interface; Studio pages are fixed views over the same commands.) |
| Layers panel: the stacked tree of layers and groups, with show/hide, reordering, grouping and renaming. | Organising a character into named parts and keeping heavy scenes navigable. | **data**: `puppet.json parts (hierarchy, z)`, `shots.json overlays/underlays/cast` (The part tree and its z order live in the puppet; shot layers (set, underlays, cast, overlays) in shots.json. No layer-tree editor in Studio; the Rig Canvas edits parts.) |
| Parameters panel: every parameter of the selected layer is editable numerically, including sub-parameters of converted values. | Precise values for placement, colours and timing that are hard to drag by hand. | **command**: `film.get`, `film.write`, `film.validate` (Every field of a film is plain JSON, read with film.get and written (schema-validated) with film.write.) |
| Timetrack panel: waypoints of every parameter aligned to the time bar, with box selection, dragging, duplicating and scaling (rewritten in 1.5.0). | Retiming a performance by shifting or compressing groups of keys. | **partial**: `timeline.get`, `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (The Studio timeline moves, resizes, adds and deletes clip layers (line-relative); per-key lanes, camera-key lane and line/shot dragging are WP-19.) |
| History panel: an undo/redo list where individual past actions can be toggled off. | Safe experimentation and backing out of a change made several steps ago. | **partial**: `film.write`, WP-39 (Films are text under git (any version re-renders exactly) and the Studio timeline keeps an undo stack; a per-change audit log of who changed what is WP-39.) |
| Info panel: pointer coordinates and colour, and render progress (progress bar from 1.4). | Checking exact positions and watching long renders. | **gap**: WP-20 (Jobs run async but report no progress events; progress events and webhooks are WP-20.) |
| Palette editor: a document palette of named colours, loadable and savable, applied to selected shapes. | Keeping a character's colour model consistent across every shot. | **data**: `puppet.json palette (slots)`, `brand/<id>.json palette` (Strokes reference named palette slots, so recolouring is a data change; puppet.lint flags raw colours and brand.check measures the brand palette.) |
| Sets panel: named selections of layers scattered through the document, selected or switched off together. | Toggling all of a prop's pieces, or selecting all left-side parts at once. | **gap**: WP-18 (No named part selections in the Rig Canvas or data (clip tracks target parts one by one); should join WP-18 (Rig Canvas 2).) |
| Navigator and canvas browser: a thumbnail for panning a zoomed view, and a browser of open files and their nested canvases. | Moving around large backgrounds and multi-canvas files. | **n/a** (View navigation is a desktop UI concern; films and their files are listed by film.get.) |

### Canvas, workarea and document settings

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Canvas properties: pixel size, image area in units, frame rate, start and end time, background. | Setting a delivery format once per project. | **data**: `shots.json size`, `shots.json fps`, `shots.json shots[].start/end` (Size and fps are film fields; qa.run enforces 1080x1920 at 30 fps; shot times may be line-relative.) |
| Resizing the canvas with presets and a keep-aspect option (1.5.1, 1.5.3). | Making a square or landscape version of a vertical piece. | **command**: `film.render` (film.render with aspect 16:9 or 1:1 re-stages the film for the new frame rather than cropping; one command for all aspects is WP-24.) |
| Per-document gamma (1.4; new files linear at 1.0, old files kept at 2.2). | Matching colour blending to the rest of the pipeline. | **gap**: WP-37 (Colours are plain sRGB in the browser and ffmpeg; managed colour (OCIO, ACES) is WP-37.) |
| Grid with snapping for placing handles. | Aligning layout elements and mechanical props. | **n/a** (Positions are numbers in data written by agents or commands; there is no hand placement to snap.) |
| Rulers and guides, rotatable and editable from 1.5.3. | Marking title-safe areas, horizons and eye lines. | **metric**: `qa.run` (Instead of drawn guides, qa.run measures every word's box against the platform-safe area and type minimums; brand.assets writes safe-area overlays.) |
| Workarea preview quality modes, background rendering and a frame cache for smooth scrubbing (1.4). | Judging motion interactively in heavy scenes. | **command**: `film.preview`, `export.web` (The player page renders every frame as a pure function of time, so it scrubs live in the browser.) |
| Preview render of a time range at reduced size and frame rate. | Quick timing checks before a full render. | **command**: `film.animatic`, `film.preview`, `film.storyboard` (Animatic over the sound in seconds, live preview, and a storyboard strip.) |
| Onion skin: faint past and future frames, counted in frames or in keyframes (1.4, 1.5.1). | Spacing and arcs while keying poses. | **gap**: WP-35 (The Rig Canvas onion shows only the rest pose; onion skins over time come with hand-drawn animation (WP-35), boards with onion skin in WP-18.) |
| Animate editing mode: edits either change the base value or create waypoints at the current time. | Separating rig setup from animation without accidental keys. | **data**: `puppet.json (rest pose)`, `shots.json cast[].timeline` (Separation is structural: the puppet holds the rest state, the shot timeline holds everything that changes.) |
| On-canvas handles and the group transformation widget (move, rotate, scale, move the origin) for direct manipulation. | Posing and placing by hand rather than by numbers. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-19 (The Rig Canvas poses by dragging hands and feet (IK) and puppet.save keeps poses and clips; dragging characters, camera and lines within a shot is WP-19.) |
| Canvas dependencies dialog listing external files with path relinking (1.5.3). | Collecting and repairing a project's assets before hand-off. | **command**: `film.provenance`, `asset.check`, `film.credits` (Every file that shapes a film is listed with its licence class; paths are data, so relinking is a film.write.) |
| Colours dialog (RGB, HSV, hex, alpha) and defaults for new layers (fill and outline colours, width, blend). | House colours and line weights applied to every new shape. | **data**: `puppet.json palette`, `brand/<id>.json` (Palette slots and brand kit defaults; brand.check measures contrast and colour-blind separation.) |

### Tools

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Transform, smooth move, scale and rotate tools for selecting and manipulating layers and vertices, with box selection. | Day-to-day posing and layout. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (Posing by drag in the Rig Canvas and clip edits in the timeline; in-shot transforms of characters and props are data only until WP-19.) |
| Spline tool: a Bezier pen that makes a region, outline, advanced outline, plant or curve gradient from one path in one pass. | Clean vector character lines with editable tangents. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, `drawing.fulfil`, WP-18 (Strokes are SVG paths; the Rig Canvas draws freehand ink and drawing.fulfil lifts ink into parts, but there is no Bezier pen with tangent handles or polygon editing (WP-18).) |
| Draw tool: freehand strokes fitted to splines with smoothing, optional pressure-driven width. | Sketch-quality lines that stay editable vectors. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (Rig Canvas pressure ink with simplify (perfect-freehand); stabiliser, textured brushes, lasso and transform remain in WP-18.) |
| Sketch tool: rough guide strokes that are never rendered, saved and reloaded separately (redo from 1.5.5). | Roughing poses and layout before clean-up. | **partial**: `drawing.requests`, `puppet.import`, WP-18 (Drawing requests carry ghost guides and puppet.import skips sketch and guide layers; a sketch layer inside the Rig Canvas is not there (WP-18).) |
| Width tool: brush along an outline to thicken or thin it locally. | Line weight that tapers into joints and swells on curves. | **gap**: WP-18 (Strokes carry one width; editing width along a line belongs with Rig Canvas tools 2.) |
| Fill and eyedrop tools: fill a closed area with the current colour, pick a colour from the canvas (fill colour on Ctrl+click from 1.5). | Fast colouring of line art. | **data**: `puppet.json strokes[].fill (palette slot)` (The Rig Canvas gap-closing fill writes a palette slot name into the stroke; picking is by slot name, not by sampling.) |
| Circle, rectangle, star and polygon tools creating parametric shapes as chosen layer types. | Props, graphic elements and motion-design shapes. | **data**: `puppet.json strokes[].d`, `shots.json overlays (draw, icon)` (Any static primitive is an SVG path; animatable parametric primitives (radius, point count) do not exist and would join WP-42 (procedural 2D).) |
| Gradient tool: drag to create linear, radial, conical or spiral gradient layers. | Sky ramps, soft shading and lighting passes. | **gap**: WP-36 (Gradients are flattened on import and absent from puppet and shot data; should join WP-36 (compositor) as generator nodes.) |
| Text tool: place a text layer, then edit text, font, size and orientation. | Titles, labels and signage. | **data**: `shots.json overlays (text, title, sign, paper, card)` (Timed text elements with fonts from the brand kit; type sizes measured by qa.run.) |
| Mirror tool: reflect selected vertices across a horizontal or vertical axis. | Building symmetric drawings and flipping poses. | **gap**: WP-18 (No mirror operation on strokes or poses (puppet.coach only reports left/right mismatch); should join WP-18.) |
| Cutout tool: draw a polygon to cut a region out of an image layer. | Splitting a painted character into animatable parts. | **command**: `doodle.cut`, `puppet.from_ink` (Parts are cut from one ink layer by polygons with overlap seams that hide the cut.) |
| Brush tool (experimental, MyPaint-based) for raster painting on image layers. | Painted texture, backgrounds and touch-ups inside the animation file. | **gap**: WP-34 (No raster painting; WP-34 picks the brush engine (libmypaint, Krita) and WP-35/WP-18 build on it.) |

### Layers: geometry

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Region layer: a filled area bounded by a spline whose vertices can be animated. | Fills of character parts and backgrounds. | **data**: `puppet.json parts[].strokes[] (d, fill)` (Filled paths per part; the path itself is static per drawing (shape changes are swaps, bends or mesh skinning).) |
| Outline layer: a stroke along a spline with per-vertex width, round tips and sharp or smooth cusps. | Ink lines of characters. | **data**: `puppet.json strokes[] (stroke, width)` (Strokes have one width and round caps; export.synfig writes them as outline layers.) |
| Advanced outline: width points independent of vertices, dashes, several tip styles (two new dash tips in 1.4), smoothing. | Expressive tapered ink and dashed lines. | **gap**: WP-15 (No variable-width or dashed character lines (only dashed whisper bubbles); line media simulation is WP-15.) |
| Animating spline vertices directly: shapes morph between keyed outlines. | Shape tweens for cloth, mouths and squash without swaps. | **partial**: `puppet.from_blend`, WP-43 (Shapes deform by bendy limbs and skinned mesh strokes (SVG only); there is no per-vertex keyed morph, and morphs between drawings are WP-43.) |
| Parametric circle, rectangle (rounded corners, feather; 1.4), star and polygon layers with animatable parameters. | Motion graphics shapes whose radius or point count animates. | **gap**: WP-42 (Static paths and kit elements only; animated parametric primitives should join WP-42 (procedural 2D).) |
| Solid colour layer filling the canvas or group. | Backgrounds and colour holds. | **data**: `shots.json set (plain) and set_opts` (The plain set with a colour option; exporters write a background colour or the set plate.) |
| Feathered (soft) shape edges. | Soft shadows, glows and light pools. | **gap**: WP-36 (No soft-edge shapes in data; blurs and mattes come with the compositor.) |
| Winding style (non-zero or even-odd) to make holes in self-overlapping regions. | Rings, letters and cut-through shapes from one path. | **data**: `puppet format v0.9 rule` (Strokes take a fill rule (rule: evenodd) that cuts holes; Lottie and Synfig write it, Grease Pencil gets keyhole polygons.) |

### Layers: gradients

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Linear and radial gradient layers with animatable endpoints and colour stops. | Skies, vignettes, soft lighting and depth fades. | **gap**: WP-36 (Only sets drawn in code use gradients; nothing in puppet or shot data. Should join WP-36 (compositor generator nodes). The AI-look lint (WP-04) also limits large gradient fills.) |
| Conical and spiral gradient layers. | Radar sweeps, stylised backgrounds, hypnotic effects. | **gap**: WP-36 (Same as linear/radial: join WP-36.) |
| Curve gradient: a gradient laid along a spline with a width, for shading along edges. | Rim shading and form shading that follows a contour. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-14 (style.toon gives cel shadow bands and rim light from a fixed light in the SVG renderer; other renderers (Synfig, Lottie, Grease Pencil) are WP-14.) |
| Noise gradient: animated procedural noise mapped to a gradient. | Textures, clouds, flicker and film grain. | **data**: `shots.json post.grain`, `shots.json style.boil` (Seeded grain and turbulent line boil, both deterministic; not a general noise fill.) |
| Gradient editor dialog and gradient converters (rotate, repeat, two-tone, gradient colour) to edit and reuse gradients. | House gradients shared across shots. | **gap**: WP-36 (No gradient data type; join WP-36.) |

### Layers: filters

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Colour correct layer: hue shift, brightness, contrast, exposure, gamma and (1.5.5) saturation on everything below it. | Shot grading and matching a character to a background. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-36 (shots.json post (grade, LUT, vignette, presets) grades the joined picture; per-layer correction inside a shot is WP-36.) |
| Clamp layer: keep colour values within a range (no negatives, a ceiling on brightness). | Cleaning up results of additive blends before output. | **gap**: WP-37 (The 8-bit sRGB path cannot go out of range; a float or HDR pipeline (WP-37) would need clamping.) |
| Luma key: turn brightness into transparency. | Using scanned line art or light effects over other layers. | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, WP-36 (Ink import thresholds a scan's ink to alpha at import time; a keying node inside a shot is WP-36.) |
| Chroma key layer with invert (1.5.3). | Compositing green-screen footage or flat-colour plates. | **gap**: WP-36 (No footage keying; compositor WP.) |
| Halftone 2 and halftone 3 layers: print-style dot screens. | Comic and retro print looks. | **gap**: WP-15 (Style passes cover paper, grain and boil; a halftone pass fits WP-15's medium simulation.) |
| Filters act on everything below them in their group, so effects can be scoped by grouping. | Grading only the background, or only one character. | **gap**: WP-36 (Effects are whole-frame (post) or built in; scoped effect stacks need the per-shot node compositor.) |

### Layers: blurs

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Blur layer with several kernels (box, fast Gaussian, cross, Gaussian, disc) on the content below. | Soft shadows, glows and out-of-focus backgrounds. | **gap**: WP-36 (No blur in shot or puppet data.) |
| Motion blur layer: accumulates sub-frame samples over an aperture; can leave a sharp trail instead (1.5.3). | Fast action that reads smoothly; ghosted trails as a stylistic effect. | **gap**: WP-15 (Speed lines are drawn from motion (fx), but sub-frame motion blur is open in WP-15.) |
| Radial blur: a zoom-burst blur from a centre point. | Impact frames and dramatic push-ins. | **gap**: WP-36 (Impact stars exist (fx.impacts) but no radial blur.) |
| Depth-of-field effects by blurring far layers. | Focusing the eye on the speaking character. | **gap**: WP-36 (Multiplane depth of field is in WP-36's scope.) |

### Layers: distortions

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Curve warp: bends the artwork below along a spline. | Rubber-hose limbs, tails and bending props without redrawing. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-14 (puppet bend curves a part along the joint's arc and chains bend tails (SVG and Grease Pencil); not along an arbitrary spline, and Synfig/Lottie output stays rigid until WP-14.) |
| Noise distort: turbulent displacement of the content below, animatable. | Wobbly hand-drawn lines, heat haze, water. | **data**: `shots.json style.boil` (Deterministic cycled displacement fields make line boil on characters, bubbles and props.) |
| Spherize, twirl, stretch and inside-out lens distortions. | Cartoon bulges, dizzy spins, comedic stretches. | **gap**: WP-36 (No distortion nodes; compositor WP.) |
| Perspective layer (the rewritten warp layer, 1.5.0): four-corner perspective warp of everything below, with interpolation quality. | Putting a flat drawing on a wall, a floor or a screen in the scene. | **gap**: WP-36 (2D layers in 3D exist only in the three.js renderer; layers on planes with a camera are WP-36.) |

### Layers: transform

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Translate, rotate and scale layers that transform everything below them in their group. | Moving a whole character or scene section at once. | **data**: `shots.json cast[].place (x, y, s)`, `puppet.json parts (pivot, parent)` (Characters are placed by keyed position and scale; parts rotate about pivots down the hierarchy.) |
| Group transformation with offset, angle, skew, scale and a movable origin (1.4). | Rigging nested pieces and adding a lean or shear. | **data**: `puppet.json parts (pivot)`, `pose.shifts`, squash (Rotation about pivots, shifts and whole-body squash; skew and per-part squash are missing (per-part squash and stretch is WP-11).) |
| Camera moves made by transforming a top-level group (pan, zoom, rotate). | Pans, push-ins and shakes in a scene without a camera object. | **data**: `shots.json camera`, `shots.json camera_auto` (Camera keys and automatic coverage (speaker framing, punch-ins, handheld) are baked for every renderer; camera roll is not supported.) |

### Layers: stylize

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Shade layer: a drop shadow (or inner shadow, inverted) made from the alpha below, with offset, softness and colour. | Lifting characters off the background, and graphic-style shadows. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-36 (Ground shadows under characters and cel shadows (style.toon) exist; a general drop or inner shadow from alpha needs the compositor.) |
| Bevel layer: a light and dark edge that makes shapes look embossed (handles on parameters from 1.5.3). | Buttons, logos and a soft 3D finish on props. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-14 (style.toon's rim light and shadow band give a similar edge on filled vector shapes in SVG; other renderers are WP-14.) |
| Animating the light angle, depth and softness of shade and bevel over time. | Lighting that changes as a lamp swings or the sun sets. | **gap**: WP-36 (The toon light is fixed per shot; animated lighting with the compositor (time-of-day grades also touch WP-37).) |

### Layers: time

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Time loop layer: repeats a span of the animation below (local start, duration, link time). | Walk cycles, blinking lights, idling crowds. | **data**: `clip library clips[].loop`, `shots.json cast[].timeline.clips` (Library and character clips loop (idle, walk); chains carry a travelling wave.) |
| Stroboscope layer: samples the animation below at a lower rate. | Animating on twos or threes for a hand-made feel. | **data**: `shots.json style.step` (Stepped timing on 12s, 8s or 6s per shot or character; repeated frames are deduplicated at render.) |
| Time offset and speed on groups and imported canvases: shift, slow, speed up or freeze a nested animation. | Reusing one animation at a different moment or tempo. | **data**: `shots.json clips start/end (line-relative)`, `puppet.json acting.tempo`, `shots.json timewarp` (Clips are placed on the words and play at a tempo; timewarp holds and slows a shot (SVG player only).) |

### Layers: other (group, switch, duplicate, image, text, procedural)

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Group layer: nests layers into a sub-canvas with its own transform, time offset, z range and outline grow. | Building a character as nested parts and reusing groups. | **data**: `puppet.json parts (parent tree)` (Parts form the hierarchy; export.synfig writes one group per part.) |
| Switch group: shows exactly one child, chosen by name or (1.4) by depth index, animatable. | Mouth charts, hand poses, eye sets and outfit changes. | **data**: `puppet.json swaps`, `puppet.json views`, `puppet.json controls (swap targets)` (Swap sets and views, driven by visemes, clips and controls; exported to Synfig as hold-keyed visibility.) |
| Duplicate layer: repeats its group with an index value, so copies can vary by index. | Radial patterns, rows of objects, simple crowds. | **partial**: `proc.cook`, WP-42 (Seeded scatter and instancing of cast members and 3D blocks with per-copy variation; duplicating a group by index is WP-42.) |
| Import image layer: a bitmap placed by its corners, openable in an external editor and reloaded (1.4). | Painted backgrounds and raster character parts. | **command**: `puppet.from_ink`, `puppet.import`, `puppet.export_layers` (Raster-part puppets from scans or PSD/ORA, and a layered file out to an artist and back (raster parts render in SVG only, WP-14).) |
| Text layer: font family list, size, orientation, spacing, animatable text; rewritten in 1.5.1 with right-to-left support and a direction parameter. | Titles, captions and signage in several languages. | **partial**: `shot.render`, `film.localize`, WP-25 (Text overlays, bubbles and captions with Sinhala fallback; right-to-left and complex scripts are WP-25.) |
| Plant layer: procedural branching growth along a spline. | Growing vines, roots and hair-like flourishes. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-42 (The kit's roots element grows a root system timed to lines; general procedural growth is WP-42.) |
| Super sample layer: renders a group at higher resolution for cleaner antialiasing. | Fixing jaggies on fine detail. | **n/a** (Vector parts are rasterised by Chromium at output resolution with antialiasing; nothing to supersample.) |
| Procedural generator layers: checkerboard, XOR pattern, metaballs, Mandelbrot and Julia fractals. | Backgrounds, test patterns and abstract motion design. | **gap**: WP-42 (Procedural 2D layer scope.) |

### Layer parameters and blend methods

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Blend methods per layer: composite, straight, onto, behind, screen, overlay, hard light, multiply, divide, add, subtract, difference, brighten, darken, colour, hue, saturation, luminance, alpha over, and alpha intersection (1.5.4). | Shading, lighting passes, glows and graphic colour effects. | **gap**: WP-36 (Everything composites normally; blend modes are in the compositor scope.) |
| Masking through blends: draw a layer only onto what is below, or use one layer's alpha to cut another. | Clothing patterns inside a shape, shading clipped to a body part. | **gap**: WP-36 (Only internal self-clipping (toon shading) exists.) |
| Opacity (amount) per layer, animatable. | Fades, ghosts and dissolving effects. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-36 (Strokes have a static opacity and swaps/views switch by visibility; animated opacity on parts and layers is not in data (would sit with WP-36; dissolves between shots are WP-15).) |
| Z-depth parameter: moves a layer within its group's order, animatable. | An arm passing in front of then behind the body. | **partial**: `shot.render`, `export.synfig`, WP-14 (pose.z, clip swaps.z and controls drive order in SVG and Grease Pencil; Synfig and Lottie exports keep a static order (WP-14).) |
| Origin and focus point parameters: the pivot for transforms and the centre for effects. | Rotating parts about joints. | **data**: `puppet.json parts[].pivot` (Pivots per part, estimated on import and checked by puppet.coach.) |
| Invert parameter: a shape becomes a hole that shows everything outside it. | Iris wipes, spotlights and cut-through windows. | **gap**: WP-36 (No inverted shapes or mattes; iris wipes are also a WP-15 transition.) |
| Antialias switch and feather per shape. | Crisp pixel-art edges, or softened edges. | **n/a** (Antialiasing is always on in the browser rasteriser; soft edges are covered under geometry feathering (WP-36).) |

### Bones and skeleton deformation

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Skeleton layer: a hierarchy of bones (origin, angle, length, width, tip width, depth) that is shown while editing and never rendered. | Cut-out character rigs. | **data**: `puppet.json parts (parent, pivot, limits)` (Each part is a bone with a pivot and joint limits; FK, two-bone IK and FABRIK chains are in the loader.) |
| Skeleton tool (1.5.0): click to create bones and re-parent them. | Rigging new characters quickly. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, `doodle.cut`, `cast.generate`, WP-16 (Rigs come from templates (humanoid, doodle) with pivots from markers or estimated; free-form hierarchies such as quadrupeds and birds are WP-16 templates.) |
| Link to bone: attach chosen vertices to one bone so they move rigidly with it. | Rigid cut-out pieces such as a forearm or a shoe. | **data**: `puppet.json parts[].strokes` (Strokes belong to a part and follow it rigidly.) |
| Link to skeleton: vertices are bound to every bone whose influence shape covers them, weighted by coverage; weights are editable. | Smooth bends at elbows, knees and torsos from one drawing. | **partial**: `puppet.from_blend`, WP-11 (Skinned mesh strokes carry per-vertex part weights (from Blender rigs) and bend in SVG; automatic weighting of vector strokes is missing and should join WP-11.) |
| Skeleton deformation layer: bones warp raster artwork below through a subdivided mesh, with a rest pose reset. | Bending painted characters without cutting them into pieces. | **partial**: `puppet.from_blend`, `export.synfig`, WP-14 (Mesh strokes give this in the SVG renderer; the .sif exporter does not write Skeleton Deformation, which the feature map assigns to WP-14.) |
| Bone link and bone influence converters: any parameter (a prop's position, a value) follows a bone. | Props held in hands, accessories that follow the head. | **data**: `shots.json props[].attach`, `puppet.json anchors`, timeline lookat (Props attach to parts and move hand to hand; look-at aims a part at another character's part (SVG player).) |
| Changing a bone's parent during the animation. | Passing an object between hands, or a character picking something up. | **data**: `shots.json props[].attach (keyed parents, arcs)` (Parent switching for props with travel arcs; parts of a character keep a fixed hierarchy.) |
| Bone length scaling (local and recursive) to stretch limbs. | Cartoon stretch on reaches and jumps. | **partial**: `shots.json timeline scales`, `shots.json timeline ik_stretch`, WP-11 (Per-part scale along the bone (local, carrying the children's pivots) and stretchy IK limbs; a recursive scale that also scales the children is WP-11.) |
| Rest pose versus animated pose (reset pose), and a quick humanoid skeleton from a bundled plugin (add-skeleton-simple; image-as-stickman). | Starting rigs from a standard template. | **command**: `cast.generate`, `puppet.sheet` (Every puppet has a rest pose and named poses; cast.generate builds a rigged original from a short spec; puppet.sheet shows rest and key poses.) |

### Value nodes and converters

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Arithmetic converters: add, subtract, scale, reciprocal, power, exponential, logarithm, modulo, absolute (1.5.5), range and map range (1.5.4). | Driving one value from another with a formula, such as a wheel turning with distance. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-42 (puppet.json controls drive targets through piecewise-linear key curves (covers map range and scale), and every renderer plays them; general expressions on any field would join WP-42's rules.) |
| Trigonometric and vector converters: sine, cosine, atan2, dot product, vector angle, length, X and Y, composite and radial composite. | Oscillations, orbits and values split into components for separate animation. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-42 (Built-in oscillators exist (chain wave, idle noise, camera float), but no general trig driver on arbitrary fields (WP-42).) |
| Logic and comparison converters: and, or, not, compare, switch between values by a condition. | Rig switches: show the hand pose that matches a control. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-42 (controls can drive discrete swaps (swap.<id>) by value, which covers switch-by-threshold; boolean logic is absent.) |
| Linear converter: a value that changes at a constant rate over time. | Spinning wheels, drifting clouds, ticking clocks. | **data**: `shots.json props[].spin`, `shots.json style.float` (Props spin, sets drift and the camera floats; not available on every field.) |
| Random converter: seeded smooth random motion with speed, radius and loop. | Jitter, handheld camera and flicker that stay repeatable. | **data**: `shots.json camera_auto.handheld`, puppet acting idle, `shots.json style.boil` (Seeded noise everywhere; frames are pure functions of the frame number.) |
| Spline converters (spline, vertex, tangent, width, segment): bind a value to a point or tangent along a path. | Objects travelling along paths and orienting to them. | **partial**: `shots.json place.path`, `shots.json props path`, WP-20 (Positions of characters and props are bound to splines by arc length; binding other values to spline points or tangents is WP-20.) |
| Dynamic converter: a point with mass, spring and damping that lags and overshoots its target. | Follow-through on hair, ears, antennae and props. | **data**: `puppet.json springs`, `shots.json timeline.springs` (Deterministic spring re-simulation on parts; prop physics solved at bake time.) |
| Animation from file converter: values read from an external file (Papagayo and Rhubarb lip-sync; more value types in 1.5). | Driving a mouth switch from a lip-sync tool's output. | **command**: `voice.build`, `film.data` (Visemes come from the voice alignment (voice.build); data-bound overlays read analysis output (film.data). Arbitrary fields cannot be fed from a file.) |
| String converters (integer, real, angle and time to text). | On-screen counters, timers and debug readouts. | **data**: `shots.json overlays counter` (Animated counters with prefix, suffix and decimals; values can come from data.json.) |
| Time-shaping converters: step, timed swap, time loop and reverse, applied to a single parameter. | Stepping one element on twos while the rest moves smoothly; reversing a move. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-15 (style.step works per shot or character and timewarp per shot; per-parameter stepping and reversed clips are not there (12-fps-native stepped rendering is WP-15).) |
| Colour converters: greyed, gradient colour, two-tone, stripes. | Desaturated flashbacks, colour cycling and patterned fills. | **gap**: WP-16 (Runtime recolour exists through palette slots; palette variants per scene (night, sick, cold) are WP-16.) |
| Average, weighted average and derivative converters. | Blending between poses, and reacting to speed. | **data**: `shots.json timeline.clips[].weight`, `shots.json fx` (Clips blend by weight with fades; speed lines and dust are computed from motion speed.) |

### Linking, exported values and the library

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Linking: several parameters share one value, so moving one handle moves all linked ones. | Joints that never gap and colours that change together. | **data**: `puppet.json palette slots`, `puppet.json parts[].pivot (shared joints)` (Colours link through slots; joints share pivots with overlap so bends do not gap (checked by puppet.coach).) |
| Exporting a value to the library under a name, then connecting it elsewhere; exported values keep their animation. | A shared light angle or colour scheme across a whole shot. | **data**: `puppet.json poses`, `brand/<id>.json`, clip library (Named poses, brand values and clips are reusable by name; there is no named value shared between arbitrary shot fields.) |
| Exported canvases: nested animations stored once (also in other files) and reused as many instances. | A walk cycle or a prop animation reused across shots and files. | **command**: `cast.add`, `cast.list`, `puppet.save` (Characters and their own clips live in the cast library with provenance and content hashes; shots reference them by path.) |
| Reusing an animation on a different character by importing a file with exported values. | One library of motion for a whole cast. | **data**: clip library (template-level clips) (Clips are template-level, so every character on a template plays them (retargeting across characters).) |
| Static parameters: mark a value so it cannot be animated by accident. | Locking rig settings while animating. | **n/a** (Rig values live in the puppet and only timeline fields animate; nothing in the rig can be keyed by accident.) |

### Animation: waypoints, interpolation, keyframes and the time track

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Any parameter change in animation mode creates a waypoint at the current time. | Pose-to-pose keying directly on the canvas. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, `puppet.save`, WP-19 (Keys are written as data (place, look, ik, controls, camera) and clips are added in the timeline; the Rig Canvas records keyed poses into clips. Per-key editing in Studio is WP-19.) |
| Waypoint editing: select, drag, duplicate and scale groups of waypoints; jump between them (1.5.0). | Retiming and polishing a performance. | **partial**: `timeline.edit`, WP-19 (Clip layers move and resize; individual keys inside a clip and key scaling are WP-19.) |
| Interpolation per waypoint side: clamped, TCB (tension, continuity, bias), constant, ease in/out and linear; changed in bulk from 1.5.3. | Holds, snaps, smooth arcs and overshoot shaped key by key. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-19 (Clip keys always ease with smoothstep, swaps and views hold, anticipation/overshoot comes from acting.principles; choosing interpolation per key needs the curve editor of WP-19.) |
| Keyframes: named marks across all parameters; changing a keyframe's length shifts every later waypoint. | Retiming a whole scene section without touching individual keys. | **command**: `film.retime`, `script.slots` (Spoken lines play the role of keyframes: times are line-relative, so re-voicing or retiming moves everything after it.) |
| Keyframe locks for past and future keyframes (hotkeys in 1.5.4), so editing between keys keeps neighbouring poses. | Protecting finished poses while adjusting an in-between. | **n/a** (Clips are separate layers with their own start and end; editing one never writes keys into neighbours.) |
| Keyframe activation toggle and description notes. | Labelling story beats on the time bar. | **data**: `script.json line ids`, `shots.json start_line/end_line`, `review.json` (Lines are named beats; review notes pin comments to frames (review.add).) |
| Time track with playback range, looped playback, frame previews on hover and markers for segments with constant value (1.4, 1.5). | Reviewing a section repeatedly while timing it. | **partial**: `timeline.get`, WP-19 (The Studio timeline scrubs with live preview over clip and dialogue lanes; per-parameter lanes, camera lane and graph view are WP-19.) |
| Graphs panel: value curves with draggable control points and tangents, multi-select, nudging (1.4). | Fine-tuning ease and spacing. | **gap**: WP-19 (Curve (graph) editor is in WP-19's scope.) |
| Baking: turn an interpolated or converted parameter into one waypoint per frame (1.4). | Freezing procedural motion for export or hand-editing. | **command**: `export.synfig`, `export.lottie` (All renderers read the baked animation (tools/bake.mjs); the exporters write per-frame keys from it.) |
| Copying and pasting waypoints or whole poses to other times. | Reusing a key pose later in the shot. | **data**: `puppet.json poses`, `shots.json timeline.poses` (Named poses can be placed at any time; clips can be added again with timeline.edit.) |

### Sound and lip-sync

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Sound layer: an audio file placed with a delay and volume (audio is not rendered into the output file). | Animating to dialogue and music in the scene. | **command**: `voice.build`, `film.render` (Voices are synthesised and aligned, and film.render mixes voice, score, cues and foley and muxes them with the picture.) |
| Sound panel with a waveform for syncing (1.4), with a choice of which sound layer to show (1.5.0). | Hitting accents and phonemes by eye. | **gap**: WP-40 (Words and beats are known from timing.json and film.beats, but no waveform lanes in Studio (WP-40).) |
| Audio playback while scrubbing and playing in the workarea. | Checking sync interactively. | **partial**: `export.web`, `film.animatic`, WP-40 (The web player and animatic play with sound; editing and scrubbing audio cues in the timeline is WP-40.) |
| Lip-sync import from Papagayo (.pgo) and Rhubarb (TSV/XML, 1.5.0) driving a switch group of mouths. | Fast dialogue animation from a breakdown. | **command**: `voice.build`, `lipsync.check` (Visemes come from phoneme timings automatically; lipsync.check measures sync against the voice and against Rhubarb when installed.) |
| Per-layer volume. | Balancing dialogue, music and effects. | **metric**: `qa.run` (The mix is rule-based (ducking, effects under music) and measured: -14 LUFS, true peak, effect onsets; mixcheck checks intelligibility.) |
| Joining sound and picture after rendering with an external tool (the manual suggests ffmpeg). | Delivering a finished movie with audio. | **command**: `film.render` (Muxing is part of the render, with captions (.srt, .vtt) and QA.) |

### Importing artwork

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Bitmap import (PNG, JPEG and more; many formats through Magick++ from 1.5.3) with scale-to-fit (1.5.0). | Bringing in painted backgrounds and character art. | **command**: `puppet.from_ink`, `doodle.snap`, `puppet.import` (Scans and photos become raster-part puppets; layered PSD/ORA become puppets by layer name.) |
| Image sequence import as an animated layer, skipping duplicate frames (1.4, 1.5.0). | Bringing hand-drawn frame-by-frame animation or renders into a scene. | **gap**: WP-35 (Drawn levels (a sequence of drawings with exposures) are WP-35.) |
| Vectorization: bitmaps traced into outline layers (centreline and outline modes, ported from OpenToonz, 1.4). | Turning paper drawings into editable vectors. | **gap**: WP-18 (Ink import keeps raster lines; vector tracing (VTracer, potrace) is WP-18.) |
| SVG import into layers (groups, paths, colours; SVG text from 1.5.4). | Bringing in art made in Inkscape or Illustrator. | **command**: `puppet.import` (Layered SVG becomes a vector puppet; colours become palette slots and transforms and gradients are flattened.) |
| Video import as a layer (basic; slow on large files). | Reference footage for rotoscoping and timing. | **partial**: `actout.clip`, WP-36 (Video of a person becomes an action clip (pose and face retargeting); footage as a layer in a shot, for reference or compositing, belongs with WP-36.) |
| Swf2svg: a helper to convert Flash files to SVG. | Migrating old Flash libraries. | **n/a** (No Flash legacy to migrate; SVG import covers converted files.) |
| Round trip of an image layer through an external editor (1.4). | Painting fixes in a raster editor and seeing them in the scene. | **command**: `puppet.export_layers`, `puppet.import` (A puppet goes out as a layered SVG, ORA or PSD and comes back by layer name.) |

### Rendering and the command-line renderer

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Render dialog: target, file name, image size, quality, antialiasing, a time range or a single frame. | Final renders and test renders of a section. | **command**: `shot.render`, `film.render` (One shot or the whole film; film.render only re-renders changed shots (content hash).) |
| Output targets: PNG and PNG sequences, GIF, BMP, ffmpeg video (AVI default from 1.4, lossless H.264, WebM from 1.5.3), ImageMagick formats. | Delivering to editors, the web and print. | **partial**: `film.render`, `film.package`, WP-23 (H.264 MP4 with per-platform encoding profiles; WebM and ProRes with alpha, GIF, APNG and stickers are WP-23.) |
| Headless command-line rendering: choose file, target, output, size, start and end time, frame rate. | Batch and farm rendering from scripts. | **command**: `shot.render`, `export.synfig` (shot.render with renderer synfig writes the .sif and drives the Synfig CLI; characters render with alpha and are composited over the SVG set.) |
| Multithreaded rendering with a thread count (preferences from 1.5.0) and render engine choice on the command line (1.5.3). | Using every core and choosing speed versus quality. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-38 (One browser worker per CPU with frame dedupe; renderer choice per shot. Distributed workers across machines are WP-38.) |
| Rendering one exported canvas instead of the root, and listing canvases. | Rendering a single element or variant from a master file. | **command**: `shot.render`, `variants.hooks` (Single shots, and hook variants as alternative openings joined to the rest of the film.) |
| Alpha output, including writing alpha to a separate file. | Handing characters to a compositor over separate backgrounds. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-23 (Synfig and Grease Pencil renders are made with alpha internally and composited; alpha deliverables (WebM, ProRes) and EXR passes are WP-23 and WP-37.) |
| Render progress output, stopping a render (1.5.0) and a notification when done. | Managing long renders. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-20 (Renders run as async jobs; progress events and webhooks are WP-20 (owner notifications WP-03).) |
| Benchmarks and repeated renders of the same target (-b, --set-repeats in 1.5.3). | Measuring render cost before committing a farm. | **gap**: WP-20 (A cost and time estimate before rendering (frames times measured renderer rate) is WP-20.) |
| Physical resolution (DPI) for print output. | Posters, books and merchandise stills. | **gap**: WP-23 (Print-ready stills are WP-23.) |
| Gamma and quality settings per render. | Matching delivery colour and trading quality for speed in tests. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-37 (Draft versus final is a renderer and animatic choice; colour management is WP-37.) |
| Introspection from the command line: list layers, targets, importers, value nodes, modules; print layer and canvas info. | Pipeline scripts checking what an installation can do. | **command**: `engine.info`, `engine.commands`, `engine.doctor` (Renderers and tools available, every command with its schema, and what is missing.) |

### Export: Lottie, still images and interchange

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Lottie export (1.4; under File > Export from 1.5.0) producing JSON, an HTML preview and a log of unsupported features; bones are not exported and spline shapes are baked per frame. | Animations for web pages and mobile apps. | **command**: `export.lottie`, `export.web` (Baked puppet animation as parented Lottie layers with views, swaps, lip-sync and springs; the web player export plays the whole film.) |
| Lottie text layers (1.5.4) and a smaller-file mode that drops variable-width outlines (1.5.0). | Animated titles in apps; light files for the web. | **gap**: WP-14 (export.lottie writes characters and the set image only; text, props and other elements in Lottie are renderer parity (WP-14); Lottie QA and dotLottie are WP-23.) |
| Exporting a still image of a chosen frame. | Thumbnails, posters and review stills. | **partial**: `film.storyboard`, `cover.make`, WP-23 (Storyboard stills and scored cover frames per cut and platform; print-ready transparent stills at high resolution are WP-23.) |
| The native .sif/.sifz/.sfg file as an editable exchange format for other people and tools. | Handing a shot to an animator for hand finishing. | **command**: `export.synfig` (One shot as a .sif with one group per part and animated transforms, editable in Synfig Studio (bends, raster parts and dynamic z are not exported yet, WP-14).) |

### Plugins and scripting

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Python 3 plugins run from the menu: Synfig hands the script a copy of the document file, then reloads the edited result. | Batch edits and pipeline automation inside the editor. | **command**: `engine.commands`, `film.write` (Every capability is an @command exposed to CLI, HTTP, MCP and Studio; agents edit film data directly.) |
| Plugin metadata file declaring names (translatable) and exec, importer and exporter entries. | Distributing in-house tools to every seat. | **command**: `engine.commands` (Commands carry their own input schemas, listed by engine.commands; adding one is a decorated function.) |
| Importer and exporter plugins that add file formats to File > Import and File > Export (1.5.0). | Supporting studio-specific or new formats. | **command**: `export.lottie`, `export.synfig`, `export.web`, `puppet.import` (Formats are commands; editor formats (FCPXML, EDL) are WP-22 and sticker/alpha formats WP-23.) |
| Plugins with simple dialogs that receive the current time and the selected layer (1.5.3). | Context-aware tools such as 'add a blink here'. | **command**: `acting.direct`, `review.add`, `review.fix` (Commands take times and frames as arguments; prose directions and frame notes become data edits.) |
| Third-party plugins such as importing labels and timings from an audio editor, controller rigs and free-form deformation. | Bringing audio breakdowns in as timing marks; extra rigging aids. | **partial**: `film.beats`, `script.slots`, WP-40 (Timing comes from alignment, slots and the beat grid; importing labels from Audacity or Ardour is in WP-40's sound-editing scope.) |

### Configuration and preferences

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Autosave and backups (on by default from 1.4). | Not losing work after a crash. | **n/a** (Every edit is a schema-checked file write in a git working tree; there is no unsaved editor state.) |
| Unit system, default sizes and new-document settings (default background colour or image, 1.4). | House defaults for every new project. | **command**: `film.create`, `film.from_template` (New films start from defaults or a format template; brand kits are inherited per channel.) |
| Experimental feature switches (the brush tool) and render thread count. | Opting into new features per seat. | **n/a** (Features are commands, enabled for all; worker count follows the CPU count.) |
| Keyboard and mouse shortcuts, including new hotkeys for keyframe locks, duplicating layers and switching tabs (1.5.3, 1.5.4). | Speed for full-time artists. | **n/a** (Agent-driven; the Studio timeline has a few shortcuts (undo).) |
| Icon themes chosen through an environment variable (1.5.0) and interface theming. | Comfort and accessibility of the interface. | **n/a** (Desktop UI theming.) |
| Environment settings for external tools (for example the Python interpreter used by plugins). | Pointing the application at pipeline tool versions. | **command**: `engine.doctor`, `engine.info` (DBE_* environment variables name tool paths (Blender, Rhubarb, Python); the doctor reports and fixes them.) |

### Tutorial workflows

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Bone morphing: a shape that changes between two drawn states as bones move. | Turning heads and changing silhouettes smoothly. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-43 (The turn control slides and parallaxes face parts for a 2.5D head turn and views switch; vector morphs between views are WP-43.) |
| Reusing animations across files through exported values and imported canvases. | Building a motion library for a series. | **data**: clip library, `puppet.json clips` (Template clips and character clips play on every character of a template and in every renderer.) |
| Symmetric patterns from one drawing with the duplicate layer (the snowflake). | Motion-design ornaments and kaleidoscopic effects. | **gap**: WP-42 (Instancing and procedural repetition.) |
| Subtracting shapes from each other with blend methods to cut holes. | Moons, donuts, cut-out lettering and shape booleans. | **gap**: WP-36 (No shape booleans or alpha-subtract blends in puppet or shot data.) |

## TVPaint Animation (12.1.0 (TVPaint Animation 12; manual at doc.tvpaint.com))

Sources: [TVPaint Animation 12 user manual (doc.tvpaint.com), introduction and sidebar table of contents](https://doc.tvpaint.com/docs/doc-introduction); [doc.tvpaint.com sitemap](https://doc.tvpaint.com/sitemap.xml); [TVPaint Animation 12.1.0 changelog](https://doc.tvpaint.com/docs/changelogs/12-1-0); [TVPaint Animation 12: what's new (TVPaint blog)](https://tvpaint.com/en/blog/tvpaint-animation-12-nouveautes); [Brush engine chapter pages (drawing modes, profiles, connections, custom and anim brushes)](https://doc.tvpaint.com/docs/drawing-basic-tools/tool-panel/brush-engine/brush-engine-overview); [Light table and advanced animation pages](https://doc.tvpaint.com/docs/animation-advanced-functions/light-table/light-table-overview); [Puppet layer chapter (rig mode, animate mode, poses, export options)](https://doc.tvpaint.com/docs/animation-puppet-layer/overview); [CTG layer colouring and texturing pages](https://doc.tvpaint.com/docs/colorize-texturize/colorize-with-ctg-layers/quick-overview)

### Introduction

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Downloadable example projects that go with the lessons, so each tool can be tried on real material. | Studios hand these to new hires and freelancers so they can learn the house tools on known scenes. | **data**: films/fx-demo, films/kit-demo, films/explainer-demo, films/review-demo (Doodleberry's demo films each exercise a feature set and double as golden-frame fixtures; no lesson text goes with them.) |
| Video walkthroughs for the more complex tools (camera, puppet layer). | Teams use them to onboard artists quickly, without a trainer. | **gap**: WP-28 (Teaching mode (turn a pipeline into a step-by-step tutorial) is in WP-28's scope.) |
| A manual in several interface languages (French, Japanese, Chinese planned). | International studios and outsourcing partners work from the same reference. | **n/a** (Doodleberry is in-house with one owner and agents; its docs are English. Localising film content is a separate matter (film.localize, WP-25).) |

### Download, install and manage your license

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Installers for Windows, macOS and Linux, with per-OS setup steps. | IT teams roll the same version out across a studio's mixed workstations. | **command**: `engine.doctor` (engine.doctor lists what the machine has and lacks with the exact fix; tools/setup.sh applies them idempotently.) |
| Licence activation, rehosting and troubleshooting through a copy-protection system (software or dongle). | Studios move seats between machines and recover after hardware changes. | **n/a** (Doodleberry is in-house and built on open-source parts; there are no seat licences. Third-party licence classes are tracked separately (licence.ledger).) |
| Network installation for many seats from one server. | Large studios deploy and update all workstations centrally. | **partial**: `engine.doctor`, WP-38 (One machine runs every command over CLI, HTTP and MCP; render workers on several machines, sharing a content-hash cache, come in WP-38.) |

### Interface

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A modular interface where every panel can be detached from the main window and arranged across several monitors. | Animators keep the canvas on the tablet display and timeline, colour and light table on a second screen. | **partial**: `film.preview`, `timeline.get`, WP-21 (Studio is a set of separate browser pages (timeline, review, board, Rig Canvas) that can sit on different screens, but the layout cannot be saved. A combined, arrangeable desk belongs with WP-21.) |
| Rooms: saved workspaces for each stage of the work (storyboard, roughs, clean-up, compositing). | Artists switch their whole layout when they move from roughing to cleaning or compositing. | **gap**: WP-21 (The nearest equivalent for agents is a command subset per role (director, animator, editor), in WP-21.) |
| A timeline that carries layer drawings, camera keys, effect keys, notes and audio tracks in one view. | Everything that is timed in a scene can be checked against everything else at a glance. | **partial**: `timeline.get`, `timeline.edit`, WP-19, WP-40 (The Studio timeline has shot, dialogue (with word ticks) and clip lanes. A camera-keys lane is in WP-19 and audio waveform lanes in WP-40.) |
| A layer stack for creating, ordering, grouping, merging, splitting and renaming layers. | Compositing order and per-layer edits are managed by hand on every scene. | **partial**: `shots.json: underlays / cast / overlays order`, puppet: part order and z controls, WP-35 (Layering is declarative: set, then underlays, cast in order, then overlays; inside a puppet, part order plus z controls. There is no free stack of drawn layers until drawn levels (WP-35).) |
| A playback bar with play, loop, frame step and playback-rate options. | Animators review timing constantly while drawing. | **command**: `film.preview`, `export.web` (The player page (every frame a pure function of time) and the exported web player both play, scrub and step frames.) |
| An info bar that reads out cursor coordinates, the colour under the cursor and preview status. | Artists use it to place elements precisely and check colour values. | **n/a** (Agents read exact positions from the baked animation (tools/bake.mjs, the PROBE used by review.fix) rather than from a cursor.) |

### Project creation and management

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A new project set up with resolution, frame rate, pixel aspect, field order and background. | Each production fixes its delivery format before any drawing starts. | **command**: `film.create` (film.create makes the folder; shots.json holds size and fps (1080x1920 at 30 fps is the QA bar). Pixel aspect and fields have no use in a progressive digital pipeline.) |
| Loading existing projects, with a list of recent ones. | Artists hop between scenes all day. | **command**: `film.get` (film.get returns a film's sources and build outputs; prod.status lists the films on the board.) |
| Saving and save-as, so successive versions can be kept. | Teams keep numbered versions to roll back after bad changes. | **command**: `film.write` (film.write validates each source file on write; films are text, so git holds every version.) |
| Guidance and features for keeping work safe: backups, local storage, not touching files while they are open. | Studios guard against lost days of drawing after a crash. | **data**: git history of films/<name>/, `prod.launch worktree per film` (Films are small text files under version control. Each production session gets its own worktree and branch.) |
| Project templates: blank projects with preset resolution, rate and layers. | Series productions start every episode from the same technical setup. | **command**: `film.from_template` (Format templates build a storyboard from a brief, with the frame size and pacing rules set. These are narrative templates as well as technical ones.) |
| Modify project: resize or resample, crop and change the frame rate of an existing project. | Re-purposing a scene for a new delivery format or a higher-resolution master. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-24, WP-37 (film.render with an aspect re-stages a film natively for 16:9 or 1:1, and vector art scales freely. One command for every aspect is in WP-24; higher frame-rate and 4K masters are in WP-37.) |

### Canvas display

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Rotate, pan and zoom the view without changing the drawing, and open several windows on one project. | Animators turn the page to draw natural arcs and zoom in for clean-up. | **gap**: WP-18 (The Rig Canvas has no view rotation or zoom; drawing-tool upgrades are in WP-18.) |
| A canvas background (colour or checkerboard) kept out of exports, with layers stacked on it like cels. | Artists judge line and colour against neutral or transparent grounds and export with alpha. | **partial**: `export.lottie`, WP-23 (Synfig and Grease Pencil render characters with alpha before compositing, and export.lottie can leave out the set (background none). Alpha video deliverables are in WP-23.) |
| Display overlays: stencil, paper texture, current selection, an animator disc and a safe frame. | Artists see framing and masking aids while drawing. | **partial**: `qa.run`, `brand.assets`, WP-18 (Safe areas are measured by qa.run and drawn as overlays on brand assets. Live drawing overlays belong in the Rig Canvas tools (WP-18).) |

### Drawing: basic tools

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A set of natural-media raster brushes (pen, pencil, airbrush, crayon, oil, watercolour-like) with many settings. | Frame-by-frame animators draw roughs, clean lines and painted backgrounds in the one program. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, `puppet.from_ink`, WP-34, WP-18 (The Rig Canvas has one pressure ink (perfect-freehand), and puppet.from_ink keeps a real pen's texture from a scan. Textured brush engines (Krita, libmypaint) come through WP-34 and WP-18.) |
| Pen gestures (pressure, tilt, speed, direction) mapped to brush parameters through editable curves. | Line weight that responds to the hand is central to the look of hand-drawn animation. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (Pressure (or a speed-simulated pressure with a mouse) drives ink width in the Rig Canvas. Mapping to other parameters, and tilt, is in WP-18.) |
| Custom brushes cut from any drawing, with stamp spacing and wet profiles, plus animated brushes that cycle through several images. | Studios build a brush kit for a show's signature texture; animated brushes stamp foliage or crowds fast. | **gap**: WP-34, WP-18 (Painting and brush engines are WP-34 candidates; scatter of animated stamps also touches WP-42.) |
| Drawing modes that decide how a stroke combines with what is already there: colour, behind, erase, shade, light, colorize, panto and others. | Painting behind line art and shading without masks speeds up clean-up and colour. | **gap**: WP-34 (The Rig Canvas bucket tucks fills under the ink, which acts like a 'behind' mode. Layer-level blend modes are in WP-36.) |
| Shape tools: freehand strokes, lines, rectangles, ellipses and circles (2 and 3 point), and Bezier or B-spline curves, filled or outlined. | Used for props, layouts and clean geometric elements. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, WP-18 (Vector paths are native puppet and kit data, and puppet.import flattens basic SVG shapes. Interactive shape tools in the Rig Canvas are in WP-18.) |
| Papers: grain textures applied through the brush, including two papers at once. | Gives digital drawings the tooth of real paper, a signature of the medium. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-15 (The paper set and the post presets (paper, grain) texture the whole frame. Medium simulation for each stroke (pencil, marker, crayon) is in WP-15.) |
| Line smoothing (stabiliser), either in real time or after the stroke. | Clean-up artists get steady, confident lines without redrawing. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (Strokes are simplified after drawing (Ramer-Douglas-Peucker); a live stabiliser is in WP-18.) |
| Cut, select and crop tools: rectangle, freehand and colour-based selection, a selection brush, and cropping. | Artists isolate parts of a drawing to move, fix or reuse them. | **partial**: `doodle.cut`, `puppet.from_ink`, WP-18 (Polygon cuts split a drawing into rig parts (with overlap seams). General lasso and selection editing are in WP-18.) |
| A colour panel with A/B colours, sliders, a picker, a mixer, palettes, a colour wheel, variations and a history. | Colourists keep a show palette and mix related shades fast. | **data**: puppet: palette slots, `brand/brand.json palette` (Colours are named palette slots in puppets and a measured brand palette (brand.check). There is no interactive mixer, which agents do not need.) |
| Construction guides with snapping: grid, field chart, circles, ellipses, angle lines, segments, vanishing points and image guides. | Layout artists draw in correct perspective; animators keep arcs and paths true. | **gap**: WP-18 (No drawing guides yet; they fit the Rig Canvas tools of WP-18.) |
| A safe-area guide showing title-safe and action-safe zones. | Keeps important action and text clear of cropping on delivery. | **metric**: `qa.run`, `brand.assets` (qa.run measures every word's box against the platform-safe area of each aspect; brand.assets draws safe-area QA overlays.) |
| Tool presets and a tool history to recall configured brushes. | Artists switch between rough, clean and colour brushes many times an hour. | **gap**: WP-18 (Belongs with brush engines (WP-34) and the Rig Canvas tools (WP-18).) |

### Drawing: transformations

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Panning a drawing or selection across the canvas, on one image or across many. | Repositions drawings during clean-up or fixes registration between frames. | **data**: `shots.json: cast[].place keys` (Characters are moved by keyed placement (x, y, scale). Moving drawn pixels needs drawn levels (WP-35).) |
| Transform: scale, rotate, shift and flip with handles or typed values, a movable pivot, applied to one frame or many. | Adjusts the size and staging of drawings without redrawing. | **data**: `shots.json: cast[].place {x, y, s}`, `shots.json: cast[].flip`, puppet: part pivots and angles (Scale, flip and move are placement data, and rotation comes through part angles about pivots. A whole character's rotation is not a placement field.) |
| Perspective: four corner points distort a drawing. | Fits a drawing onto a receding surface such as a sign or a floor. | **gap**: WP-36 (Corner-pin distortion fits the per-shot compositor.) |
| Warp: an elastic grid with pins that bends pixels smoothly. | Fixes proportions or adds organic bends to a finished drawing. | **partial**: `puppet: mesh strokes with skinning (v0.5), bendy limbs`, WP-35 (Bone-driven mesh deformation exists in the puppet format (SVG renderer). Free pin warping of a drawing fits WP-35 drawn levels, or the compositor (WP-36).) |
| Transformation resampling that preserves image quality, with a preview-quality option. | Avoids softening line art when drawings are scaled or rotated repeatedly. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-37 (Vector art is resolution independent, but raster parts are resampled by the browser with no quality control. 4K masters are in WP-37.) |

### Animation: first steps

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Animation layers: each layer is a strip of drawings, like a roll of film shown on the canvas. | The core of frame-by-frame work: every character, effect and background is drawn layer by layer. | **gap**: WP-35 (Drawn levels (a sequence of drawings with exposures) are the new part type of WP-35.) |
| Instances and exposure cells: one drawing held for any number of frames, and instances split or joined. | Timing on ones, twos or threes, and holds, are set per drawing. | **partial**: `shots.json: style.step`, WP-35 (style.step animates a shot or character on 12s, 8s or 6s. Exposure per drawing comes with WP-35.) |
| Adding or removing exposures and instances, shrinking a layer, and reversing a run of images. | Retiming hand-drawn animation without redrawing. | **gap**: WP-35 (timeline.edit retimes and resizes clips, but not drawings.) |
| A layer's start frame and moving a whole layer in time. | Offsets overlapping actions and syncs an element to sound. | **command**: `timeline.edit` (move_clip moves a clip layer, and times can be line-relative so they follow the dialogue after re-voicing.) |
| Moving, cutting, copying and pasting images within a layer and between layers. | Reuses drawings and rearranges keys while blocking. | **gap**: WP-35 (Needs drawn levels.) |
| Navigation from instance to instance and selection of instance ranges. | Animators flip quickly between keys while in-betweening. | **partial**: `timeline.get`, WP-19 (timeline.get gives every clip's resolved start and end, and the Studio nudges by frame. Key-to-key navigation and a keys lane are in WP-19.) |
| Layer folders that group layers and move them together. | Keeps big scenes with many characters and effects tidy. | **data**: puppet: part hierarchy, `shots.json: cast[] entries` (A character is a group of parts with one timeline, and a shot groups its cast. Arbitrary user folders arrive with drawn levels (WP-35).) |
| Flipping a layer horizontally or vertically. | Reverses a character's facing or reuses a drawing mirrored. | **data**: `shots.json: cast[].flip` (flip is 1 or -1 per cast member.) |

### Animation: additional functions

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Pre and post behaviour: what a layer shows before its first image and after its last (nothing, hold, loop, ping-pong), now also per folder. | Cycles (walks, flames, idles) run for a whole scene from a few drawings. | **partial**: `shots.json: timeline clips (looping base layers such as idle)`, WP-35 (Clips can loop and base layers fill the gaps between gestures. Ping-pong and pre/post settings for drawn levels are in WP-35.) |
| Per-layer opacity, and layer attenuation during playback. | Dims reference layers and builds transparent effects. | **gap**: WP-36 (Opacity and mattes for each layer belong to the compositor.) |
| Locking layers against accidental edits, and collapsing them to save room. | Protects approved backgrounds or clean lines while others are worked on. | **gap**: WP-39 (WP-39 adds assignments and locks on films and characters.) |
| A background preview proxy that caches played frames, with settings for quality and memory. | Smooth real-time playback of heavy scenes on ordinary machines. | **command**: `film.preview`, `film.animatic`, `shot.render` (The live player renders frames on demand, the animatic takes seconds, and renders are cached per shot by content hash so only changed shots re-render.) |
| Colour groups: tag layers with colours to show, hide, select or protect them together. | Organises dense scenes by character or department. | **gap**: WP-19 (Timeline organisation features fit the timeline room upgrade (WP-19).) |
| Layer blending modes (multiply, screen, add, overlay and so on) and merging layers through a mode. | Shadows, glows and colour tints are composited from separate layers. | **gap**: WP-36 (Blend modes are in WP-36's node compositor scope.) |
| Bookmarks and coloured image marks that set key drawings apart from breakdowns. | Lead animators mark keys and breakdowns so assistants know what to in-between. | **partial**: `review.add`, WP-35 (review.add pins notes to frames. Marking drawings by role (key, breakdown, in-between) comes with WP-35.) |
