| Preserve alpha (paint only on existing pixels) and stencils (mask one layer by another's opaque pixels). | Shading, texturing and recolouring inside line art without leaking. | **partial**: `shots.json: style.toon`, WP-36 (Toon shading clips each shape to itself to paint cel shadows. General masks and mattes are in WP-36.) |
| Layer presets that set the defaults (type, colour, opacity, mode) of new layers. | Departments start every scene with a consistent layer setup. | **gap**: WP-35 (Only meaningful once drawn layers exist (WP-35); style defaults today live in dbe/profiles.json.) |

### Animation: advanced functions

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A light table (onion skin) showing nearby frames faded and tinted, with per-frame opacity and modes by instance, frame, bookmark, rig key or image mark. | Essential for in-betweening and checking spacing in hand-drawn animation. | **gap**: WP-35 (The Rig Canvas 'Onion' only shows the rest of the character faintly (a spatial guide, not frames in time). Onion skins over time are in WP-35.) |
| Out of pegs: slide the onion-skin frames on top of each other, without changing the drawings, to line them up for in-betweening. | Animators line up overlapping volumes when an in-between travels a long way. | **gap**: WP-35 (Part of the onion-skin work in WP-35.) |
| Flipping: roll through a few drawings back and forth as one would with paper. | The quickest check of a motion's flow while drawing. | **gap**: WP-35 (film.preview scrubs a film, but flipping drawings belongs to WP-35.) |
| Sketch panel presets for roughing in colour and cleaning up in a separate pass. | The usual rough, tie-down and clean-up workflow. | **gap**: WP-35 (Clean-up of rough lines is in WP-35's scope.) |
| An animator panel of one-click actions such as setting a layer to ones, twos or threes or adding exposure. | Speeds up retiming and layer housekeeping. | **partial**: `shots.json: style.step`, WP-35 (Stepped timing per shot or character exists; per-drawing exposure actions are in WP-35.) |
| Repeat images: exposures reuse content from earlier instances to build cycles without duplicating drawings. | Makes cycles and repeated actions cheap to change. | **partial**: `shots.json: timeline clips (loops)`, WP-35 (Motion clips loop; drawn cycles come with drawn levels.) |
| Animated strokes: a single stroke spread over successive frames. | Fast hand-made effects such as rain, flying insects or sparkles. | **gap**: WP-35 (Procedural rain and sparks are in WP-42; hand-made strokes through time fit WP-35.) |
| A drawing recorder that replays how a drawing was built, stroke by stroke, as an animation. | Draw-on reveals for titles, explainers and process videos. | **partial**: `shots.json: overlays type draw`, WP-18 (The kit 'draw' element makes paths draw themselves over time. Recording a live drawing session in the Rig Canvas would join WP-18.) |
| An image library per project storing images, sounds, rigs and poses for reuse. | Shared assets stay consistent across scenes and artists. | **partial**: `cast.add`, `cast.list`, WP-16 (The cast library registers puppets with licence and provenance, and poses and clips travel with each puppet. Props, outfits and set packs are in WP-16.) |

### Animation: puppet layer

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Building a puppet from parts drawn on separate layers grouped in a puppet folder. | Cut-out characters for TV series where full redraws are too costly. | **command**: `puppet.import` (Layered SVG, PSD or ORA files become a puppet by layer name, with pivots estimated where markers are missing.) |
| Rig mode: placing and editing bones and joints on the drawing. | Riggers set where each limb bends before animation starts. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, `doodle.cut`, `puppet.save`, WP-16 (Rigs follow templates (humanoid, doodle), with pivots and cuts that can be confirmed or dragged. Free-form bone layouts and new templates (quadruped, bird, object) are in WP-16.) |
| Animate mode: keying bone rotations and positions, with in-betweens made automatically. | Fast keyed motion for simple actions without drawing in-betweens. | **command**: `puppet.save`, `timeline.edit` (Rig Canvas posing records keys and saves them as a clip (puppet v0.7). Clips are placed and retimed on the timeline.) |
| Squash and stretch of individual members, scaled in both directions. | Adds elasticity and impact to cut-out motion. | **data**: `shots.json timeline scales`, `puppet format v0.9` (Per-part squash and stretch (timeline scales, controls, stretchy IK), local to the part so children keep their proportions; every renderer draws it.) |
| Bone locking and bone constraints. | Keeps feet planted and limbs bound while other bones move. | **data**: `shots.json timeline ik_weight`, `puppet format v0.9 copy_rotation` (Two-bone and FABRIK IK with IK/FK blending and targets on other characters, stretchy limbs, foot pins along paths, joint limits, look-at, copy rotation and parent switching exist.) |
| Speed curves on rig keys for easing. | Gives keyed motion weight and accents. | **partial**: `puppet/loader/acting.js: smoothstep between keys`, WP-19 (Keys ease with a fixed smoothstep. Editable curves per key are in WP-19's graph editor.) |
| Swapping a member's drawing (mouth, hand, eye shape) at any key from stored poses. | Lip-sync and hand poses on cut-out characters. | **data**: puppet: swap sets, clips: swaps tracks, puppet: controls (Swap sets with keyed swaps and controls; visemes come from the voice automatically (voice.build).) |
| A pose selector with previews, and adding new poses from existing ones. | Animators pick from a library of approved poses instead of re-posing. | **command**: `puppet.save`, `puppet.sheet` (Saved poses live in the puppet (used by poses keys in any timeline); the model sheet shows poses and expressions.) |
| Saving and importing whole rigs and rigged animations through the library. | Rigs and stock motions are reused across episodes and artists. | **command**: `cast.add`, `puppet.save` (Clips are template-level, so any character on a template plays any library clip.) |
| Turning puppet layers into ordinary frame-by-frame layers for redrawing. | Animators rough with the rig, then plus the animation by hand. | **gap**: WP-35 (The bake (tools/bake.mjs) gives every frame's pose, but there is no conversion into drawn levels to redraw. puppet.export_layers exports only the rest pose.) |

### Working with sound

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Loading several audio tracks from files or from the library, project-wide or per clip. | Dialogue, music and effects tracks drive timing in animatics and animation. | **partial**: `voice.build`, WP-13 (Voices are synthesised and aligned, the score is procedural and foley is automatic. Importing recorded human takes into slots is in WP-13.) |
| Per-track settings: mute, volume, colour group, compact or full view. | Editors balance temp tracks while timing. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-40 (The mix follows rules (ducking, effects below music, panning by screen position). Controls for each track in Studio are in WP-40.) |
| Waveform display with drag-to-offset, trimming and a master track summing all tracks. | Animators read accents and syllables from the waveform. | **partial**: `timeline.get`, WP-40 (Dialogue lanes show lines with word ticks. Waveform lanes and cue trimming are in WP-40.) |
| Audio scrubbing: a short piece of sound plays as the playhead is dragged. | Finds the exact frame of a sound accent. | **gap**: WP-40 (Word timings are exact in data (timing.json), but there is no audio scrub in Studio.) |
| A stopwatch for tapping beats to measure the rhythm of music or action. | Timing action to music or to an acted reference. | **command**: `film.beats` (The beat grid comes from the score's bpm, and shot changes and line starts are measured against it.) |
| Timeline notes: drawn and typed annotations placed along time. | Directors leave timing notes for animators on the scene itself. | **command**: `review.add`, `review.list` (Notes are pinned to frames, and review.fix turns many of them into data edits.) |
| Sound files stored in the project library and dropped on the timeline (12.1). | Reusable effect and music libraries per show. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-13 (Effect cues are typed (shots.json cues) and come from a synthesised set in the manifest. A sample library (Kenney CC0) is in WP-13.) |

### Colorize and texturize

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A paint bucket with source options (this layer, another layer, all layers), tolerance, gap closing and antialiasing control. | Ink and paint: colour fills read the line layer while painting on a separate colour layer. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-35 (The Rig Canvas bucket closes gaps up to about 10 px and tucks fills under the ink, on one drawing. Filling across frames is in WP-35.) |
| Fill inside: a brush stroke that only paints inside the closed area it starts in. | Quick shadow and highlight passes without masks. | **gap**: WP-35 (Colour work on drawn frames is in WP-35.) |
| Gradient fills (linear, radial, conical, multi-point). | Soft lighting on characters and props. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, WP-18 (Gradients in imported SVG are flattened, and sets drawn in code use gradients. Gradient fill tools are in WP-18.) |
| Opacity mapping to control transparency across a fill. | Fades and soft-edged fills. | **gap**: WP-36 (Mattes and transparency ramps fit the compositor.) |
| A colour checker that finds pixels left unfilled next to lines. | Ink-and-paint QA before shots go to compositing. | **partial**: `puppet.lint`, WP-35 (puppet.lint flags raw colours that are not palette slots, and vector fills sit under the ink so they leave no gaps. A per-frame fill check belongs with drawn levels (WP-35).) |
| CTG layers: colour a whole animation from a few squiggles, with the colour following the lines as they change from frame to frame. | Cuts colouring time for frame-by-frame animation by a large factor. | **gap**: WP-35 ('Colour styles applied across every frame' is in WP-35's scope.) |
| A CTG palette for editing and extracting colours, so a colour can be changed everywhere at once. | Colour-model changes (a night version, a corrected skin tone) are applied across a scene. | **data**: puppet: palette slots, `shots.json: set_opts` (Named palette slots recolour a character everywhere, and set_opts recolours sets. Palette variants per scene (night, sick, cold) are in WP-16.) |
| CTG textures that stay on coloured areas as they move and change shape. | Textured looks (fabric, paper, brushwork) on animated characters without boiling by accident. | **gap**: WP-35 (Boil and paper grain exist (style.boil, post paper). Textures that track drawn regions fit WP-35, or WP-15 for medium simulation.) |

### Camera

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A key-based 2D camera layer with position, rotation, zoom and size keys on the timeline. | Pans, trucks and tilts that frame the action, designed in layout. | **partial**: `shots.json: camera keys [t, [cx, cy, zoom]]`, WP-19 (Camera keys cover pan and zoom only, and the bake folds them in for every renderer. Camera rotation is missing (it should join WP-19 with the camera-keys lane, or WP-36). There is no camera lane in Studio yet.) |
| Camera parameters: format presets, aspect, pixel aspect, fields. | Matches the camera to the delivery format. | **data**: `shots.json: size`, `film.render aspect` (The frame size is film data, and other aspects are native re-stagings, not crops.) |
| Speed curves for ease-in and ease-out of position, zoom and rotation. | Camera moves that start and stop softly feel filmed rather than mechanical. | **partial**: `shots.json: camera_auto.ease`, WP-19 (Keys ease with a fixed smoothstep, and auto-framing has an ease setting. Editable curves are in WP-19.) |
| Path modes between camera keys: linear, spline or Bezier, with linear sections where needed. | Curved camera travel around a layout. | **gap**: WP-19 (Interpolation between keys is fixed; curve control comes with the graph editor (WP-19).) |
| Keyframe management: create, delete, move, copy, invert keys and jump between them. | Layout artists refine camera timing against the animation. | **partial**: `film.write`, WP-19 (Keys are edited as data and may be line-relative. A camera-keys lane is in WP-19.) |
| Copying the last camera position of the previous clip to begin the next one. | Continuity between shots that start where the last one ended. | **gap**: WP-19 (Continuity checks and tools are in WP-19.) |
| Typed camera moves (12.1) matching common Japanese camera work, recorded on keys and exported with the timesheet. | The camera intent travels to the compositing studio with the exposure sheet. | **partial**: `shots.json: camera_auto`, WP-22 (Auto-framing gives a grammar (follow the speaker, wide shots between, punch-ins, handheld). Typed move annotations in interchange formats would join WP-22.) |

### Timesheet

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| An exposure-sheet panel in Japanese or Western layout, one column per layer. | The standard way to plan and hand off timing between key animators, in-betweeners and compositors. | **gap**: WP-35 (The X-sheet is in WP-35, built on OpenToonz's model where WP-34 chooses it.) |
| A memo panel, dialogue notes, waveform and camera columns on the sheet. | Directors and animators plan acting against the dialogue frame by frame. | **partial**: `timeline.get`, `film.storyboard`, WP-35 (timeline.get gives lines with word timings, and the storyboard PDF carries lines and directions per shot. A sheet layout is in WP-35.) |
| A dialogue breakdown per frame for lip-sync. | Animators read mouth shapes from the sheet. | **command**: `voice.build`, `lipsync.check` (Visemes come from the voice's phoneme timings automatically, and lipsync.check measures their lag against the audio.) |
| Frame roles (key, breakdown, in-between, special in-between) with to-do and done status. | Tracks who still has to draw what in a scene. | **gap**: WP-35 (Key poses with in-between assistance (WP-43) are part of WP-35.) |
| Automatic reuse: a new key that shares a name with an existing key takes its content. | Repeated keys (a return to a pose) stay identical. | **gap**: WP-35 (Named poses already exist for rigs (puppet.save); for drawings this needs WP-35.) |
| Timesheet export to PDF, and XDTS import and export for exchange with other animation software. | Sheets go to partner studios and other tools in a shared format. | **gap**: WP-35 (The storyboard contact PDF exists (film.storyboard). XDTS fits the X-sheet work in WP-35, and interchange formats are in WP-22.) |

### Storyboarding

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A project organised as scenes and clips (shots), shown as thumbnails, in columns or on a timeline. | Storyboard artists structure the episode before animation. | **command**: `film.storyboard`, `timeline.get` (shots.json is the shot list; film.storyboard draws the strip of every shot with its notes.) |
| Creating, splitting, joining, duplicating and reordering scenes and clips. | Restructures the edit as the story changes. | **partial**: `film.write`, WP-19 (Shots are edited as data (validated); dragging shots in Studio is in WP-19.) |
| Action, dialogue and notes text fields per clip. | Boards carry the instructions animators need. | **command**: `film.storyboard` (The script lines and directions are data, and the contact sheet prints them beside each shot.) |
| Turning the board into an animatic by setting clip durations, with sound and camera moves. | Directors judge pacing before committing to animation. | **command**: `film.animatic`, `shot.render` (film.animatic holds stills over the mix or voice in seconds (no camera moves); an SVG draft render gives a moving animatic with the camera.) |
| PDF publishing of the storyboard with layout options. | Printed boards for meetings and clients. | **command**: `film.storyboard` (Writes contact.pdf (shots per page with notes) and strip.png.) |
| HTML publishing: a web page whose thumbnails play their animation and camera move on hover. | Shares boards with remote reviewers without software. | **partial**: `export.web`, `film.storyboard`, WP-19 (export.web makes a self-contained interactive player of the film; a board page that plays each shot on hover would join WP-19 (review on a phone).) |
| EDL publishing: each clip exported as a video, plus an edit list that an editing program can rebuild. | Hands the animatic to editorial. | **command**: `film.handoff` (Each shot is handed off as a video with an EDL (and OTIO, FCP XML, Kdenlive) that rebuilds the cut.) |
| A bridge between clips (12.1): faint views of the previous clip's last frame and the next clip's first. | Checks continuity across cuts while drawing. | **partial**: `qa.run`, WP-19 (qa.run measures seam smoothness at cuts; visual continuity checks (screen direction, eyeline) are in WP-19.) |

### Import / Export

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Importing images, image sequences and video into layers, either embedded or linked to the source file. | Brings in layouts, backgrounds, live-action reference and rotoscoping plates. | **partial**: `puppet.import`, `puppet.from_ink`, WP-36 (Layered drawings and photos become puppets, and video is read for pose by actout.clip. Image or video plates as shot layers fit the compositor (WP-36).) |
| Exporting movies and image sequences in many formats (MP4, MOV, AVI, GIF, PNG, TGA, TIFF, PSD), with palette reduction and dithering options. | Delivers to clients, compositors and the web. | **partial**: `film.render`, `shot.render`, WP-23 (H.264 MP4 per platform profile. WebM and ProRes with alpha, GIF, APNG and dithered line-art GIFs are in WP-23.) |
| A slate burned into exported frames (project name, frame counter, time). | Review copies are identifiable frame by frame in dailies. | **gap**: WP-19 (Burn-ins for review copies fit the review room upgrade.) |
| Layered structure export: PSD with layers, CSV with images, the older project format. | Hands a scene to compositing with every layer separated. | **partial**: `puppet.export_layers`, WP-37 (A puppet exports as a layered SVG, ORA or PSD to repaint. Per-shot multi-pass output (beauty, line, shadow, mattes in EXR) is in WP-37.) |
| JSON export that rebuilds the project, with its layers and timing, in a compositing program. | Moves animation into After Effects for compositing without flattening. | **partial**: `export.lottie`, `export.synfig`, WP-22 (Lottie and Synfig exports keep parts and keys editable; editor hand-off formats are in WP-22.) |
| A sprite-sheet generator. | Delivers animation to game engines. | **gap**: WP-30 (Game runtime export (sprite sheets, Godot) is in WP-30.) |
| Cleaning up scanned drawings: removing paper white and grey, cleaning rough pencil lines. | Paper animators scan their work and prepare it for colour. | **command**: `puppet.from_ink`, `doodle.snap` (Flattens lighting, thresholds the ink to alpha and removes specks, keeping the artist's line.) |
| Peg-hole registration that realigns scans using the peg holes. | Feeder-scanned drawings play back without jitter. | **gap**: WP-35 (Imported paper animation would come in as drawn levels (WP-35).) |
| Printing images or sequences with page layouts. | Paper copies for review or for animating over. | **partial**: `film.storyboard`, WP-23 (Storyboard PDFs exist; print-ready stills are in WP-23.) |
| Share manager: sending projects, clips or panels to colleagues on the same network. | Quick hand-offs inside a studio. | **partial**: `prod.launch`, `prod.merge`, `cast.add`, WP-39 (Films move through git branches and worktrees per production session, and characters through the cast library. People and roles are in WP-39.) |
| A stand-alone converter that batch-exports projects to many formats without opening the main program, with a client-server mode. | Render wranglers convert whole episodes overnight. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-38 (Every render runs headless from the CLI or HTTP API; distributed workers are in WP-38.) |
| Video input: capturing frames from a camera for stop motion or line tests. | Animators test paper animation and shoot stop motion directly. | **partial**: `actout.clip`, `doodle.snap`, WP-35 (The camera feeds Act it out (pose to clips) and a photo feeds a doodle puppet. Capturing paper drawings as frames needs WP-35.) |

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |

| User-built panels holding buttons for brushes, tools, scripts and menu actions. | Each studio or artist builds a toolbar for its own pipeline. | **partial**: `engine.commands`, WP-21 (Every command is a tool for agents (CLI, HTTP, MCP, Studio). Curated subsets per role are in WP-21.) |
| Action buttons that chain many internal actions as one macro. | Repetitive pipeline steps become one click. | **partial**: `engine.commands`, WP-21 (Composite commands (film.render, film.heal, film.revoice) already chain steps, and agents script any sequence. Saved user macros and natural-language routing are in WP-21.) |
| Exporting a panel with its brushes, scripts and plug-ins as one package file to share. | Studios distribute their tool kits to every artist and partner. | **gap**: WP-29 (Creator packages (rig, boil-preset and loop-pack templates) are in WP-29.) |

### The FX Stack

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A non-destructive stack of effects on a layer, applied in order. | In-program compositing of glows, blurs and colour without leaving the program. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-36 (Shot-level post (grade, vignette, grain, LUT), motion effects (fx), toon shading and boil are data on a shot. An ordered stack per layer is in WP-36.) |
| Preview modes and real-time preview of effects, including on all layers (12.1). | Artists tune effects while watching playback. | **partial**: `film.preview`, WP-36 (fx, boil and toon show in the live player; post grades apply only at render (ffmpeg).) |
| Stacking, reordering, swapping, renaming and collapsing (baking) effects. | Builds complex looks step by step and bakes them for performance. | **gap**: WP-36 (Node graph editing is in WP-36, with the graph view of WP-20 as its editor.) |
| Saving, resetting and reusing effect settings as presets. | Consistent looks across a show. | **data**: `shots.json: post presets (crisp, film, warm, cool, paper, noir)` (Named presets plus explicit parameters; the brand kit fixes colours.) |
| Keyframed effect parameters, several per effect, interpolated over time. | Animated glows, fades and colour shifts. | **gap**: WP-36 (Post parameters are fixed for each shot today; motion effects are derived from motion, not keyed.) |
| Speed curves for effect keys. | Eases effect animation like any other motion. | **gap**: WP-36 (Needs keyed effects (WP-36) and the curve editor (WP-19).) |

### Effect categories

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Blurs: Gaussian, radial, directional, median, chroma, and motion blur. | Depth, speed and softening in compositing. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-36, WP-15 (post.grade accepts ffmpeg filters on the whole frame. Per-layer blurs and depth of field are in WP-36, and sub-frame motion blur in WP-15.) |
| Colour correction: curves, histogram, adjustment sliders, tint and colour replacement, black-and-white, multi-colour replace. | Matches shots and sets mood. | **partial**: `film.render`, WP-37 (Post presets, grade filters and a LUT file apply to the whole frame. Colour management (OCIO, ACES) is in WP-37, and per-layer correction in WP-36.) |
| Scan clean-up effects: dust cleaner, black-and-white and colour scan cleaners, colour eraser, line colorize. | Prepares scanned paper animation and recolours line art. | **partial**: `puppet.from_ink`, WP-35 (Ink thresholding and speck removal are in puppet.from_ink. Recolouring the lines of drawn frames is in WP-35.) |
| Distortions: bump and displacement maps, warp grid, four-point perspective, kaleidoscope, tornado and wave, optical flow. | Heat haze, water, impact wobbles and retiming. | **partial**: `level.inbetween`, WP-36 (Boil uses cycled displacement fields and flow-based in-betweening proposes drawings (level.inbetween); other distortions are in WP-36.) |
| Keying: chroma, luma, colour and cross keyers, plus alpha control. | Pulls mattes from footage or from colour-coded drawings. | **gap**: WP-36 (Mattes are in the compositor's scope; Doodleberry rarely uses live footage.) |
| Motion effects: path tools and a path recorder, a pixel tracker, a shaker, stabilisation. | Attaches elements to moving footage, shakes the camera on impacts, steadies scans. | **partial**: `shots.json props path`, WP-36 (Handheld drift, keyed placement and paths for characters and props exist; tracking footage is WP-36.) |
| Rendering generators: lighting, volumetric light, lens flare, Perlin noise and plasma, patterns, backgrounds, simple and multi-line text, timecode. | Generated elements for backgrounds, titles and lighting. | **partial**: `shots.json: overlays (title, sign, text, card)`, `shots.json: style.toon rim`, WP-36 (Text and title elements, sets drawn in code, and toon rims exist. Volumetric light, flares and glows are in WP-36; a timecode burn-in is in WP-19.) |
| Rototracking and a healing tracker that follow a region across frames and paint or patch it. | Paint fixes and rotoscoping over many frames. | **gap**: WP-36 (Little need without live footage; fits the compositor.) |
| Stylise effects: toon shading, drop shadow, bevel, border, glow and erode, bloom, grain, halftone and print looks, mosaic, posterise, antialiasing. | Finishing looks for cel and graphic styles. | **partial**: `shots.json: style.toon`, `shots.json: post.grain`, WP-36, WP-15 (Cel shadows and rims, ground shadows and grain exist. Glow and bloom are in WP-36, halftone and medium looks in WP-15.) |
| Transitions: fade, blinds, blocks, page turn, rotary and slide, and blur transitions. | Scene changes in animatics and finished films. | **partial**: `film.cuts`, WP-15 (Whoosh cuts and crossfaded audio at cut points exist. Cross-dissolves, match cuts and wipes are in WP-15.) |

### Advanced tools

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| KeyFramer: moves any element (brush, layer, spare image, paper) along keyed or preset paths, with rotation on three axes, motion blur and pre/post behaviour. | Animated graphic elements and flying props without redrawing. | **partial**: `shots.json props path`, WP-15, WP-36 (Keyed placement, paths for props and characters, ballistic flights and drops exist. Motion blur is WP-15, and 3D-style rotations of 2D layers WP-36.) |
| Multiplane camera: background planes at different depths, each moving at its own speed for parallax. | Classic depth in pans across layered backgrounds. | **partial**: sets: forest parallax depths, WP-36 (Some sets have built-in parallax depths; true multiplane depth for any layers is in WP-36.) |
| Depth-of-field blur set by a focus distance on the multiplane camera. | Focus pulls between foreground and background. | **gap**: WP-36 (Depth of field is in WP-36's multiplane scope.) |
| Stage views (perspective, side, front, top) for arranging planes in space. | Layout artists place planes with confidence. | **partial**: `shot.render`, WP-36 (A three.js renderer places 3D sets; 2D multiplane layout and its views are in WP-36.) |
| A multiplane wizard that builds planes from layers automatically. | Sets up depth fast from an existing layered background. | **gap**: WP-36 (Automatic depth estimates for parallax (Depth Anything) are in WP-43.) |
| A particle generator: emitters (shape, rate, life, size, colour), a world (gravity, wind) and particles drawn with any brush, for rain, snow, smoke, fireworks or fountains. | Natural effects that would take days to draw by hand. | **partial**: `shots.json comp particles`, WP-42 (Seeded particle presets (rain, snow, confetti) exist alongside motion effects and prop gravity; emitters with shape, rate and life are WP-42.) |

### Preferences

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| Keyboard shortcut configuration for nearly every action. | Artists tune their hands-on speed. | **n/a** (Agents call commands by name; Studio's shortcuts are fixed and few.) |
| Interface language and resolution scaling. | Multilingual teams and high-DPI or tablet screens. | **n/a** (In-house tool with one owner; Studio is a web page that follows the browser's zoom.) |
| Pen and tablet settings: eraser-end behaviour, pressure handling, OS tablet APIs. | Makes the pen feel right on each artist's hardware. | **partial**: `puppet.save`, WP-18 (Pointer pressure works in the Rig Canvas; full tablet support is in WP-18.) |
| Save alarm and backup settings. | Prevents losing work after a crash. | **command**: `timeline.edit`, `film.write` (Every Studio edit is written to the film's files immediately and validated, and git keeps the history.) |
| Startup configurations (panel layouts, tools, shortcuts) and a default template. | Studios give every seat the same setup. | **partial**: `engine.doctor`, `film.from_template`, WP-21 (The machine setup is checked (engine.doctor) and films start from templates. Per-role setups are in WP-21.) |
| Timeline and sound preferences (default lengths, audio scrub, playback sync). | Matches playback behaviour to the work. | **gap**: WP-40 (Audio behaviour in Studio is in WP-40.) |

### George scripting

| Capability | How professionals use it | Doodleberry |
|---|---|---|
| A scripting language that drives almost every tool: layers, drawing, saving, bookmarks. | Technical directors automate repetitive pipeline tasks. | **command**: `engine.commands` (Every feature is an @command, available at once in the CLI, the HTTP API, Studio and MCP. Agents are the main users.) |
| Scripts as plain text files that run from panel buttons or at startup. | Pipeline scripts are kept in version control and shared. | **command**: `engine.commands` (python -m dbe <command> '<json>' runs any command; the tools/ scripts are plain files in git.) |
| Passing arguments to scripts and choosing launch modes. | Scripts are parameterised per shot or per project. | **command**: `engine.commands` (Every command has a JSON input schema, and results are JSON-safe.) |
| Querying project state from scripts (layers, frames, current settings). | Scripts make decisions from the current scene. | **command**: `film.get`, `timeline.get`, `film.validate` (These return sources, resolved timing and validation results as JSON.) |
| Language features: variables, operators, strings, arrays, functions and procedures, parsing. | Write non-trivial tools inside the program. | **n/a** (Doodleberry is written in Python and JS directly, so no in-app language is needed.) |
| A plug-in SDK (C/C++, now hosted on GitHub) for native extensions. | Studios add native tools and integrations. | **command**: `engine.commands`, `vendor.build`, `vendor.check` (A new @command appears everywhere at once; vendored upstream code is built and recorded (WP-33).) |
| Script dialogs and messages for interacting with the user. | Tools ask artists for options at run time. | **n/a** (Commands take arguments and return results; human approvals go through the board and posting gates.) |
